A white, semitransparent cloud floats across this rock, hiding all but its summit and its base. From time to time a lightning-flash darts from this cloud, and the reverberated echoes of the thunder-peals resound like long-drawn-out chords from this majestic organ of Nature’s own workmanship.

Over yonder, a mountain chasm suddenly comes into view, where two rocky fragments, whose rugged surfaces seem to exactly correspond, stand face to face. Through this rocky chasm, many hundred feet below, rushes a stray branch of the icy Szamos, disappearing among the thick oak woods which cover its banks.

In one place the rocks form a flight of steps, steps never fashioned for the foot of man, for each of them is as high as a tower; in another place the rocky boulders are piled one on the top of the other, in such a way that if the undermost block were disturbed, the whole of the enormous mass would fall into a differently-shaped group.

Everything indicates that here the dominion of the world and of man ends. Not a single human habitation is visible from the dizzy heights; even vegetation is rare and scanty; on every side bald rocks and gaping chasms, among which the mountain torrents toss and tumble; only the wild goat is there to be seen leaping from crag to crag.

“Which is the way now?” asked Clement of his guide, casting an anxious glance at his surroundings, in which the possibility of hopelessly losing oneself was more than probable.

===Chinese===

那是一片叫人眼花缭乱的景象。远处，钻石般的晶亮尖峰覆着皑皑雪原，一道道墨绿的松林线缀在其间。近旁是一面玄武岩，由塔一样大的棱柱并排组成，宛如一架巨型管风琴的音管，柱顶还戴着一圈圈圆头树的花环。一朵半透明白云横飘过岩壁，把山顶山脚以外的一切都遮住了。云里不时劈下一道闪电，雷声的回响隆隆荡开，像大自然亲手造的这座雄伟风琴奏出的绵长和弦。

那边，突然现出一道山隙：两块巨岩参差的断面看来严丝合缝，面对面立着。山隙之下几百尺深处，冰冷的索莫什河（Szamos）一条岔流奔涌而过，消失在两岸茂密的橡树林里。

一处地方，岩石排成一级级台阶——那台阶从来不是给人的脚预备的，每一级都有一座塔那么高；另一处，巨石一块摞一块，摞得那样巧，只要底下那块一动，整个庞然大物就会塌成另一副模样。

一切都表明：世界与人类的辖境到此为止。从令人目眩的高处望不见一个人家；连草木都稀疏罕见；四面尽是秃岩裂谷，山涧在其间翻腾跌宕；只有野山羊在，从一块巉岩跳到另一块巉岩。

“现在走哪条道？”克莱门特问向导，一边不安地扫视四周——在这种地方，走迷了路再也出不来，是最有可能的结局。

===Original===

“Trust only to me,” said Sange Moarte, and he guided them through the uninhabited wilderness with the unerring precision of instinct. In places where it seemed impossible to go a step further, he always found a path. He recollected every root or shrub which could serve as a support to clamberers down the mountain side; every fallen tree which spanned the abyss, every narrow ledge which could only be passed by bending forward over the precipice and holding fast behind to the fissures of the rock, was familiar to him; in short he seemed quite at home in this interminable labyrinth.

“We are near,” he cried suddenly, after clambering up a steep rocky wall and surveying the horizon; then he held out his hands to his companions and drew them up after him.

A new spectacle then presented itself.

The opposite slope of the rocky ridge which they had just ascended was perfectly smooth and shiny, and encompassed the whole region in a semicircle, forming a sort of basin, at the very bottom of which﻿—and it was six hundred feet deep﻿—lay a little mountain lake, the dark-green waters of which perpetually boiled and bubbled, though not a breath of air was stirring: perhaps it felt the ebb and flow of ocean. The opposite side of the rocky basin was formed by a gigantic chain of mountains, fringed only at its base by fir trees, and at the point where the two mountain systems met, a small stream in a deep bed trickled into the little mountain lake. The masses of ice which had fallen into the valley formed a crystal vault over this stream.

“Whither are we going?” asked Clement, aghast.

“To the source of that brook,” returned Sange Moarte. “It has dug its way through the ice, and by following its course we shall come to the place we seek.”

“But how are we to get there? This rocky slope is as smooth as a mirror; if a man begins sliding down it there is no stopping till he plumps into the lake.”

“You have only to take care. We must lie on our backs and glide down sideways. Here and there you will find a tuft of Alpine roses to cling on to. But you’ve nothing to fear if you slide down barefoot. Do as I do.”

A hair-bristling pastime truly!

Taking off their sandals they held on by their hands and feet to the smooth, shelving, stony wall, at the foot of which lay the darkly-gleaming, fathomless lake.

===Chinese===

“只管信我，”桑格·莫阿尔特说。他领着他们穿过这片无人荒野，凭本能走得毫厘不差。在看似再挪一步都不可能的地方，他总能找出一条道。哪条树根、哪丛灌木可以当攀下山坡的抓手，他全记得；每一棵横跨深渊的倒木，每一条只能俯身贴崖、手抠身后岩缝才过得去的窄蹬，他都熟；总之，在这座没完没了的迷宫里，他就像在自己家里。

“快到了，”他攀上一面陡峭的石壁、望了望天际，忽然喊道；随即向同伴们伸出手，把他们一个个拽了上来。

一幅新的景象展现在眼前。

他们刚登上这道石脊的另一面坡，光滑如镜，泛着亮光，把整个地区环成半圆，围出一个盆地；盆底——深达六百尺——卧着一口小小的山湖，墨绿的湖水终年沸涌冒泡，虽然四野没有一丝风：兴许它感应着海洋的潮汐。盆地的另一面是一条巍峨山脉，只在山脚镶着一线枞树林；两道山系相接的地方，一条小溪在深深的河床上潺潺注入山湖。坠入谷中的冰块，在小溪上方结成了一道水晶穹顶。

“我们这是要上哪儿？”克莱门特惊骇地问。

“到那条溪的源头去，”桑格·莫阿尔特答道，“它从冰里给自己钻出了一条路，我们顺着它走，就能到要找的地方。”

“可我们怎么下去？这石坡滑得跟镜子似的，人一滑起来，不到扑通栽进湖里停不住。”

“你们只需当心。仰面躺着，侧着身子往下溜。一路上东一丛西一丛的高山玫瑰，可以抓一把。光着脚溜就更不怕了。照我的样做。”

这真是叫人毛发倒竖的消遣！

他们脱下凉鞋，手脚并用，扒着那面平整倾斜的石壁；壁脚下，幽幽发亮的是深不见底的湖。

===Original===

They had already slided halfway down the incline, when from the mountain opposite arose a muffled, mysterious roar. They felt the cliff on which they lay quaking beneath them.

“Ha! stay where you are,” cried Sange Moarte, looking back at them. “An avalanche from the mountain opposite is approaching.”

And at the very next moment they could see a white ball descending from the immeasurably distant heights, plunging with mad haste down the mountain slope, tearing away with it whole masses of rock and uprooted pines, swelling every moment into a more tremendous bulk, and dashing down the decline in leaps of two hundred feet at a time into the valley below.

“Heaven defend us!” cried the terrified Clement, clutching his guide with one hand and holding on to the rock with the other. “It is coming this way, and will overwhelm us all.”

“Keep still,” cried Sange Moarte, seeing them inclined to clamber up again and thus expose themselves to the danger of a fall. “The avalanche will take the direction of that block of rock standing in its way, and will there either stop or disperse.”

And indeed they could see that the snow-slip, now grown colossal, was making for a projecting point of rock which was dwarf-like in comparison. Every other sound was lost in the thunder of the avalanche.

And now the huge snowball bounded upon the obstructive rock, and fell prone across it with a terrific thud, which shook the whole mountain to its very base.

For a moment the whole region was enveloped in a cloud of steam-like snow-spray, and after the final crash the thunder of the avalanche ceased. But immediately afterwards it began again with a frightful crackling; the weight of the snowy mass had uprooted the obstructing rock, and whirling down with it in dizzy rotations, plunged perpendicularly into the lake below.

The agitated lake, lashed out of its basin on both sides, rose in an enormous wave, three hundred feet high, up to the very spot where the bold climbers were clinging to the naked rock, and after poising in the air for a second, like a huge transparent green column, broke and fell back into the lake, which very slowly subsided.

“Now we will go on our way,” said Sange Moarte. “The rock is moist now, and the descent will be all the easier.”

After the lapse of half-an-hour, the wanderers found themselves at the mouth of a stream.

===Chinese===

他们已经溜到半坡，对面山上忽然响起一阵沉闷而神秘的轰鸣。他们感到身下的崖壁在颤。

“哈！别动，”桑格·莫阿尔特回头朝他们喊，“对面山上雪崩下来了。”

紧接着，他们就看见一个白球从远得望不到顶的高处滚下来，发疯似的顺坡直泻，裹挟着成片的岩石和连根拔起的松树，每一刻都涨得更骇人，一步两百尺地蹦跳着朝下面的深谷砸下去。

“老天爷保佑！”克莱门特吓坏了，一手死抓住向导，一手抠住岩石，“它朝这边来了，要把我们全埋了！”

“别动，”桑格·莫阿尔特见他们想往上爬——那样反倒要摔下去——连忙喊道，“雪崩会照着挡道的那块岩石的方向走，到了那儿要么停住，要么散开。”

果不其然，只见那已长成巨物的雪崩，直奔一处凸出的岩尖而去——那岩石同它一比，矮得像个体儒。雪崩的雷霆淹没了万籁。

紧接着，那个巨大的雪球撞上阻路的岩石，轰的一声横趴在上面，整座山连根都摇了三摇。

一时间，整个地区都罩进了一团蒸汽似的雪雾里；最后一撞过后，雪崩的轰鸣停了。可紧接着又响起一阵骇人的爆裂——雪团的重压把那块阻路的岩石连根掀了起来，带着它天旋地转地翻滚而下，直挺挺扎进下面的湖里。

被搅怒的湖水从盆沿两侧泼溅而出，掠起一堵三百尺高的巨浪，一直扑到胆大的攀登者们扒着裸岩的地方，在半空悬停了一瞬，像一根巨大的透明绿柱，随即崩碎，落回湖中；湖水这才缓缓平息。

“现在接着走吧，”桑格·莫阿尔特说，“岩石打湿了，下去反倒更省力。”

过了半个钟头，几个行路人来到一条溪流的源头口。

===Original===

A wondrous corridor lay open before them. The brook sprang from a hot spring, which, after racing down the deep valleys, buried itself beneath icebergs and snowdrifts. But the hot water had bored a passage through the ice, constantly melting the frozen mass around it with its warm stream, so that only the thick outermost layer remained, which, constantly renewed by the cold air without, and as constantly dissolved by the hot stream within, grew into a sort of transparent crystal arcade with huge dependent glittering stalactites above the stream.

Through this channel Sange Moarte now led his companions.

Clement could not but call to mind the fabulous fairy palace where spellbound mortals only see the light of day through transparent waters.

Wading thus in the bed of the stream, they reached a point where the bright arcade began to grow dark. Its transparent roof grew thicker and thicker, passing gradually into an ever deeper blue, till at last it became quite black, and the murmuring of the stream was the wanderers’ only guide. As they advanced, with their hose tucked up to their knees, into the ever-darkening darkness, they felt the water getting hotter and hotter, till at last they heard a hissing sound and saw once more the daylight streaming through the rocky chasm, through which the brook rushed down into its subterraneous cave.

Here, with the help of some dangling shrubs, they scaled the hillside to avoid the onslaught of the boiling spring, and after a brief exertion found themselves on the other side of the mountain, in a deep, well-like valley.

This is the *Gradina Dracului*.

===Chinese===

一条奇妙的廊道在他们面前敞开。这溪水发于一股温泉，在深谷里一路奔流之后，潜到了冰山雪堆底下。可热水在冰里钻出了一条通道，用温暖的流水不断融蚀着周围的冻结体，只剩最外一层厚壳；外壳被外面的冷空气不断续上，又被里面的热流不断融开，便长成了一道透明的晶拱长廊，溪流上方垂悬着硕大闪亮的钟乳石。

桑格·莫阿尔特领着同伴们穿行在这条水道里。

克莱门特不由想起传说中的仙宫——中了魔的凡人只有透过透明的水才能望见天光。

他们就这样蹚着溪床前进，来到一处，亮堂的拱廊开始暗下来。透明的顶越来越厚，渐渐变成愈来愈深的蓝色，最后彻底黑了；溪水的淙淙声成了行路人唯一的向导。他们裤腿挽到膝盖，朝愈来愈浓的黑暗里走，觉出水越来越烫，最后听见一片咝咝声，重新望见天光从石隙间泻进来——溪水正是从那里冲进它的地下洞穴。

在这里，他们攀着几丛垂悬的灌木爬上山坡，避开沸泉的袭击；没费多大劲，就到了山的另一面，置身一道深井似的山谷。

这，就是格拉迪纳·德拉库卢伊——魔鬼花园。

===Original===

It is a perfectly round dell, shut in on every side by a wall of perpendicular cliffs more than six hundred feet high. Whoever wishes to look down from above, must approach the edge of the rock lying on his stomach, and even then must have a good head not to be seized by vertigo. At the bottom of this dell the flowers have an amaranthine bloom. When the snow is falling thickly all around, and the ice is sparkling everywhere else, here in the depths of the hardest winter may then be seen those dark-green flowers with broad, indented petals, and those little round-leaved trees the like of which are to be met with nowhere else in this district. Just at this time too the leather-leaved *Nymphaea* opens its light-yellow calices here; the grass, both summer and winter, is of the brightest green; and the wild laurel climbs high up into the crevices of the rocks, and casts its red berries down into the valley, when Nature all around is cold and dead.

Throughout the winter this dell is clothed with the rarest flowers. Therefore the Wallach calls it “the Devil’s Garden,” and fears to approach it.

But the whole wonder has quite a natural cause.

In the depth of the dell a hot mineral spring bubbles up in a cave, never coming to light, but soaking all the circumambient soil through and through, and it is because these warm waters possess a flora of their own that these unknown shrubs and flowers are forever blooming in the neighbourhood of the vivifying element. The whole thing is a splendid open-air orangery in the midst of snowstorms and icebergs.

Sange Moarte beckoned to his comrades to follow him. A feverish impatience possessed him, and when he had advanced a few steps into the cavern, he pointed with trembling hand at a dark recess, in which an iron door was visible.

“What is it?” cried Clement, clutching his sabre. “Does anybody dwell here?”

“Yes,” rejoined Sange Moarte (his blood at that moment seemed to be on fire, and the veins of his temples stood out like cords). “There, in that water-basin, she is wont to bathe. There have I watched her, from day to day, without ever daring to approach her,” stammered he, in a whisper that was scarcely audible, but full of the most passionate ardour.

“Who?” asked the Patrol-officer, much amazed.

“Oh! the fairy,” stammered the Wallach, with trembling lips, and he buried his glowing head in his hands.

===Chinese===

这是一口浑圆的谷地，四面围着一圈六百多尺高的直立峭壁。谁想从上面往下看，必须肚皮贴地爬到岩边，就这样还得有个好脑袋，才不至于头晕目眩。

谷底的花开得永不凋谢。四外大雪纷飞、到处冰光闪烁的时候，就在这隆冬最深处，却看得见那些花瓣宽大带缺刻的墨绿花朵，那些小圆叶的树木——这地界里再无第二处有它们。也正是在这个时节，革质的睡莲（Nymphaea）在这里绽开淡黄色的花萼；草，无论冬夏，都绿得晃眼；野月桂攀上高高的岩缝，把红果撒进谷里——当四野的大自然冷寂死灭的时候。

整个冬天，这谷地都披着最珍奇的花。所以瓦拉几亚人称它“魔鬼花园”，不敢近前。

可这全部奇观，来由再自然不过。

谷底的一个洞穴里，一股温矿泉汩汩上涌，从不露出地面，却把四周的土壤浸了个透。正因为这些温水有自己的植物群落，那些无名的灌木花草才永远开在这赋予生机的水元素近旁。这整个儿就是一座露天暖花房，开在暴风雪和冰山之间。

桑格·莫阿尔特招手叫同伴跟上。一阵狂热的躁动攫住了他；他往洞里走了几步，便用颤抖的手指向一处幽暗的凹室，里面看得见一扇铁门。

“怎么回事？”克莱门特抓住马刀喊道，“这儿住着人？”

“是，”桑格·莫阿尔特答道（那一刻他的血仿佛着了火，太阳穴上的血管像绳索一样暴起），“在那水池里，她常洗澡。我就在那儿一天一天地守着她，从来不敢靠近。”他结结巴巴地说，声音低得几乎听不见，却满是最炽烈的渴望。

“谁？”巡官大为惊讶地问。

“哦！仙女，”瓦拉几亚人嘴唇发抖地结巴道，随即把滚烫的头埋进了双手。

===Original===

“What’s all this about?” said Clement, turning to Zülfikar. “ ’Tis not a fairy that I’m after but a panther!”

“Pst! a key is turning in the lock,” cried Zülfikar. “Away back into the dark cave!”

The two men had to drag Sange Moarte away from the iron gate, which a moment afterwards opened noiselessly, and a girlish form stepped forth leading a panther by a golden chain.

Sange Moarte was right in calling her a fairy.

Before them stood a dazzlingly beautiful woman in oriental deshabille. Her locks were enveloped in a red fez, the long gold tassels of which fell across her white turban over her pale face; her ivory-smooth shoulders gleamed forth from the sleeves of her short, ermine-embroidered kaftan; her eyes sparkled in the dark; every movement of her lithe body was serpentine, fascinating, maddening.

The three men held their breath. The girl passed by without observing them.

“Ah, that is she,” whispered Zülfikar in amazement, when she had gone.

“Who? Do you know her?” asked Clement.

“It is Azrael, Corsar Beg’s former favourite.”

“What a place for her to be in!”

“Pst! she’ll hear us.”

Meanwhile the girl had reached the basin where the subterraneous waters poured their mingled flood, sat down on a stone bench, and commenced to unwind her turban. Her jasper-black hair fell down over her shoulders.

Sange Moarte’s hot panting resounded through the darkness.

The panther lay quietly at his mistress’s feet, his shrewd head resting on his front paws.

Azrael now removed her bright Persian shawl from her slim waist, and next prepared to slip off her light kaftan, taking a couple of steps towards a projecting rocky buttress which hid her from the eyes of the watchers.

Sange Moarte was about to rush after her. It was all the two men could do to hold him back.

“Are you mad?” growled Zülfikar in his ear. “Would you betray us with your infernal curiosity?”

“The poor devil is in love with the girl!” whispered Clement.

At that moment there came the sound of a splash, as of someone leaping into the water and playing with its waves.

Sange Moarte frantically tore himself loose from his companions’ arms, and with a furious yell rushed towards the basin.

At this yell Azrael, in all the maddening witchery of her charms, sprang out of her watery mirror, looked at the presumptuous wretch with flashing eyes, and cried savagely﻿—

“Oglan! Seize him!”

===Chinese===

“这算怎么回事？”克莱门特转向祖尔菲卡尔，“我要找的不是仙女，是豹子！”

“嘘！锁眼里钥匙在转，”祖尔菲卡尔喊道，“快退回黑洞里去！”

两人只好把桑格·莫阿尔特从铁门边拖开。片刻之后，铁门无声地开了，一个少女的身影走出来，金链子牵着一头豹子。

桑格·莫阿尔特管她叫仙女，叫得没错。

他们面前立着一位光艳夺目的美人，一身东方式的居家装束。她的头发裹在一顶红色非斯帽里，帽上长长的金流苏越过白头巾垂到苍白的脸上；象牙般光洁的双肩从绣貂的短长袍袖口里透出光来；她的眼睛在黑暗中闪烁；柔软身躯的每一个动作都如蛇一般，勾魂、慑魄、令人发狂。

三个男人屏住了呼吸。姑娘从旁边走过，没有察觉他们。

“啊，就是她，”她走远后，祖尔菲卡尔惊得低声说。

“谁？你认识她？”克莱门特问。

“是阿兹拉尔（Azrael），科尔萨尔贝伊（Corsar Beg）从前的宠姬。”

“她怎么会待在这种地方！”

“嘘！她会听见的。”

这时姑娘已走到地下水汇流的水池边，在一张石凳上坐下，动手解开头巾。碧玉般黑的长发泻落到肩上。

桑格·莫阿尔特火热的喘息在黑暗里呼呼作响。

豹子安静地卧在女主人脚边，机灵的脑袋搭在前爪上。

阿兹拉尔接着从纤腰上解下明艳的波斯披巾，又准备褪下轻薄的袍子，朝一处凸出的岩石扶壁走了两步——那石头正好挡住窥视者的目光。

桑格·莫阿尔特就要冲上去跟。两个人好说歹说才拽住他。

“你疯了吗？”祖尔菲卡尔在他耳边低吼，“你想用你那要命的好奇心出卖我们？”

“这可怜虫爱上这姑娘了！”克莱门特悄声说。

就在这时，传来哗啦一声水响，像有人跃入水中，同波浪嬉戏。

桑格·莫阿尔特疯了一般从同伴们的臂弯里挣脱出来，狂吼一声，朝水池冲去。

听见这声吼，阿兹拉尔从她那水做的镜子里一跃而出，浑身带着摄人心魄的万种魔力，目光灼灼地瞪着这个放肆的狂徒，凶狠地喝道——

“奥格兰！抓住他！”

===Original===

The panther had hitherto remained motionless; but the moment his mistress called him to battle, he sprang up with a roar, seized the young Wallach, and threw him with a single jerk to the ground.

Sange Moarte did not think of defending himself against the savage beast, but stretched out his hands imploringly towards the odalisque; drank in her loveliness with thirsty looks; writhed closer to her, and, weeping and groaning, fell down at her feet, while Azrael stared wildly at him, threw her mantle hastily around her, and watched her darling panther tear to pieces the youth who had never loved anyone in his life in order that he might love her to the death.

“I’ll go and help him!” cried Clement, mad with horror, and drawing his sword.

“Softly! Don’t be a fool! Besides, we have something better to do. The iron gate remains open; let us creep in while the lady is otherwise engaged, and find out what there is here; that will interest our masters very much, especially mine.”

With that the two men crept through the iron door, groped their way along the narrow passage which seemed to have been cut out of the naked rock, and discovered at the end of it, by the light of a lamp hanging from the roof, several small doors to the right and left. They opened one door after the other, but only found empty rooms with no further outlets. At length a glimpse of the outer world reached them through one of the windows. They hastened forward in that direction, and coming upon a second iron door passed through it, and found themselves in a large courtyard surrounded by high walls, one of which they scaled, and beheld from the top of it the valley of the cold Szamos stretching far and wide before their eyes. Soon after they discovered a footpath which led them from the wall to the woodlands below, and off they set running, and never drew breath till they had safely reached the bottom. It was only then that the two men ventured to stop and look each other in the face. Clement fancied he still heard the wildly musical voice of the fair demoniac, the roaring of the panther, and the death-shrieks of the young Wallach.

“We may as well go on now,” remarked Zülfikar, “for to return the way we came without a guide is impossible, and we are bound to come out somewhere.”

And, indeed, they soon came upon two woodcutters, who were fastening their raft to the river’s bank.

===Chinese===

豹子本来一直不动；可女主人一唤它出战，它吼的一声跃起，扑住年轻的瓦拉几亚人，一甩就把他掠翻在地。

桑格·莫阿尔特没想到要反抗那猛兽，只朝姬妾伸出一双哀求的手；用饥渴的目光饮着她的美；一边朝她蠕动靠近，一边哭泣呻吟，倒在她脚下；阿兹拉尔狂乱地瞪着他，慌忙把斗篷裹上身子，眼睁睁看着她心爱的豹子把那青年撕碎——这青年一生从未爱过任何人，只为爱她爱到死。

“我去救他！”克莱门特惊骇得发疯，拔剑喊道。

“轻点！别犯傻！再说，咱们有更要紧的事。铁门还开着；趁那位夫人正有事忙着，咱们溜进去，看看这里面到底有什么；这可会让咱们的老爷们大感兴趣，尤其是我那位。”

说罢，两人溜过铁门，摸着黑沿一条仿佛是从岩壁里凿出来的窄道往前走，借着顶上吊灯的光，在尽头发现左右各有几扇小门。他们一扇扇打开，却只见一间间空屋，再无别的出口。最后，一线外面的天光从一扇窗里照进来。他们朝那个方向疾走，遇上一道第二铁门，穿过去，进了一座高墙环绕的大院；爬上其中一面墙头，只见冰冷的索莫什河谷在眼前铺展开去，一望无际。不久他们发现一条小径，从墙脚通到下面的林地；两人撒腿就跑，一口气跑到谷底才敢喘气。直到那时，两人才敢停下来，面对面看一眼。克莱门特恍惚还听见那美丽魔女野性而悦耳的声音、豹子的吼叫，和年轻瓦拉几亚人的惨死呼号。

“咱们不如接着走吧，”祖尔菲卡尔说，“没有向导，原路是回不去的，往前走总会走到个什么地方。”

果然，他们很快碰上两个伐木工，正把木筏拴到河岸上。

===Original===

“What is that castle yonder?” asked Clement.

The men stared at him.

“Where? What castle?”

Clement looked behind to show it to them, and behold! nowhere was anything to be seen with the remotest resemblance to a castle, nothing but rocks, each the counterpart of the other. The Wallachs laughed aloud.

“It were better not to mention it to them,” said Zülfikar. “They look as if they do not know what is going on under their very noses. But we’ll mark the place. Nothing but rocks are visible from the outside, the brushwood conceals the very opening through which we got into the open air.”

So the wanderers inquired their way; returned to Marisel, where they naturally did not stop to be questioned about Sange Moarte, but mounted their steeds and rode off.

Zülfikar wanted Clement to go on with him to Banfi-Hunyad. The Patrol-officer, however, declined to trespass on Denis Banfi’s domains, so the Turk went on alone to levy the new tax, though Clement prophesied that he would receive more kicks than halfpence.

---

Clement duly informed Ladislaus Csaky of what he had seen, and received one hundred ducats for his discovery, to say nothing of the green top-boots.

Zülfikar fared much more strangely.

On arriving at Grosswardein, he gave the tribute-money to Ali Pasha, informing him at the same time of all that he had found out about Azrael.

This girl, when only thirteen years old, had been carried off from Ali Pasha’s harem by Corsar Beg. Ali, her original possessor, had promised a reward of two hundred ducats to whomsoever should discover the whereabouts of his favourite.

Zülfikar on quitting the Pasha had in his hand a purse of two hundred ducats. This came to the ears of the Aga, Zülfikar’s superior officer, who straightway picked a quarrel with the renegade, and condemned him to one hundred strokes of the bastinado, unless he preferred redeeming each stroke with a ducat.

“I won’t do that,” returned Zülfikar, “but I’ll hand over to you the gift which Denis Banfi sent to Ali Pasha when I told him he was to pay the new tax. Give it to the Pasha, and I’ll wager he’ll so reward you that you’ll remember it all your life.”

The Aga greedily caught at the offer, took charge of the carefully-sealed casket which Zülfikar himself ought to have handed to the Pasha, and presented it to his Excellency with the following respectful salutation﻿—

===Chinese===

“那边那座城堡是什么？”克莱门特问。

两人直瞪着他。

“哪儿？什么城堡？”

克莱门特回头指给他们看——咦！哪儿还有什么城堡的影子，只有一座座一模一样的岩石。瓦拉几亚人大笑起来。

“还是别同他们提这事的好，”祖尔菲卡尔说，“瞧他们那样，自家鼻子底下的事都一概不知。不过咱们把地方记下了。从外面看只有岩石，咱们钻出来的那个口子，也叫灌木丛遮得严严实实。”

于是两个行路人问明了路径，回到马里谢尔；自然也没有停留让人盘问桑格·莫阿尔特的事，而是翻身上马，一走了之。

祖尔菲卡尔想拉克莱门特一起去班菲-洪纳德（Banfi-Hunyad）。巡官却不愿涉足德尼斯·班菲（Denis Banfi）的领地，于是土耳其人独自前去征收新税——克莱门特还预言，他挨的靴子会比到手的小钱多。

---

克莱门特把所见一五一十禀报了拉斯洛·恰基（Ladislaus Csaky），因这一发现得了一百达卡赏钱，更不必说那双绿色长统靴。

祖尔菲卡尔的遭遇就更离奇了。

到了大瓦尔德因，他把税款交给阿里帕夏，同时把自己查到的关于阿兹拉尔的一切都报告了。

这姑娘十三岁那年，被科尔萨尔贝伊从阿里帕夏的后宫里抢了走。她最初的主人阿里曾许下二百达卡的赏格，给任何能查出这位宠姬下落的人。

祖尔菲卡尔出得帕夏的门，手里已经攥着一袋二百达卡。这话传到了他上司阿加（Aga）的耳朵里；阿加立刻寻了个由头同这叛教者作对，判他一百杖笞刑——除非他肯每杖用一个达卡赎。

“赎我可不干，”祖尔菲卡尔答道，“不过我倒可以把德尼斯·班菲大人送的那份礼转交给你——我通知他缴新税时，他让我转呈阿里帕夏的。你拿去献给帕夏，我打赌，他会重重赏你，叫你一辈子忘不了。”

阿加贪婪地一口应承，接过了那只本该由祖尔菲卡尔亲手呈给帕夏的封得严严实实的礼匣，毕恭毕敬地把它呈到大老爷面前，致辞如下——

===Original===

“Behold, most gracious Pasha, I bring you that princely gift which Lord Denis Banfi has sent you in lieu of taxes.”

Ali Pasha seized the casket, cut through the silken cords, broke the seal, and took off the cover, when lo! a horrible, shrivelled pig’s tail fell out of it on to his kaftan﻿—the direst, most abominable outrage which can befall a Mussulman!

Ali Pasha in his fury sprang almost up to the ceiling, and throwing his turban to the ground, immediately ordered that the Aga, who stood rooted to the spot with horror, should be impaled outside the camp.

But Zülfikar went gaily on his way with the two hundred ducats in his pocket.

===Chinese===

“敬禀宽仁的帕夏：小人给您带来了德尼斯·班菲大人的厚礼，权当代缴的税银。”

阿里帕夏抓过礼匣，剪断丝绳，拆了封，揭开盖子——哎呀！一条干瘪瘪的猪尾巴掉了出来，正落在他的长袍上——这是穆斯林可能遭受的最凶最脏的奇耻大辱！

阿里帕夏暴怒得几乎蹦到天花板，把头巾掼在地上，当即下令：把吓得钉在原地的阿加拖到营外尖桩处死。

而祖尔菲卡尔揣着那二百达卡，兴冲冲地上路去了。

## III / 三
===Original===

An Hungarian Magnate in the Sixteenth Century

There was a great commotion at Bonczhida Castle. The lord of the manor, Denis Banfi, was expected home from Ebesfalva. The castle gates (on the midmost panel of which blazed a huge family coat-of-arms between the claws of two golden lions rampant) were overshadowed by green branches and bravely-coloured banners; in the street, the schoolchildren, in gala costume, were drawn up in a long line headed by their teachers; further back, with bright Sunday faces, stood the vassals; and, marshalled in front of the hillock which marked the bounds, the mounted gentry of the County of Klausenburg, some eight hundred horsemen or so, all of them stalwart, sturdy forms, armed with morning stars and good broadswords, had come out to meet their leader, the Marshal of the Nobility.

On the bastions are to be seen Banfi’s own soldiers, consisting of about six hundred mail-clad heroes, with long Turkish muskets and Scythian helmets. On the walls facing the Szamos six mortars are placed. A few yards further off a coal fire is burning, at which the cannoneers are heating the ends of their long iron staves so as to use them as linstocks.

At every gate, at every buttressed window, stand a couple of pages in crimson dolmans and tightly-fitting, cornflower-blue hose, richly garnished with silver-embroidered lace.

At the window of the highest donjon sits the castellan, ready to proclaim the arrival of his liege lord by the blast of a horn. Over his head the wind is wrestling with a gigantic purple banner, the huge dependent gold tassels of which it can only raise with difficulty.

Out of all the windows, inquisitive domestics and expectant knights and dames peep forth, or rather, out of all the windows but three, which are altogether bare of festal groups, for there nothing is to be seen but fragrant jasmines and quivering mimosas in snow-white porcelain vases, behind which one can dimly distinguish a pale and delicate form leaning dreamily on the embroidered window-cushions. This is Denis Banfi’s wife.

===Chinese===

十六世纪的匈牙利大贵族

邦奇达城堡里一片大忙乱。庄主德尼斯·班菲（Denis Banfi）要从埃贝什法尔瓦回来了。城堡大门（门板正中，两只金狮后爪立地、利爪之间是一枚巨大的家徽）掩映在青枝彩旗之下；街上，穿节日盛装的学童由教师领头，排成长长一列；再往后，是喜气洋洋如同过礼拜天的众佃户；而在界丘前排开的，是克劳森堡州的骑马乡绅，约莫八百骑，个个剽悍壮实，持晨星锤和阔剑，出迎他们的统领——那位大贵族（magnate）统领。

城堡棱堡上班菲自己的士兵，约六百名披锁子甲的好汉，持长长的土耳其火枪，戴西徐亚式头盔。面朝索莫什（Szamos）河的墙上架着六门臼炮。再往前几步，一堆炭火正旺，炮手们在火里烧着长铁钎的尖端，好拿它们当点火杆用。

每一处门口、每一处带扶垛的窗前，都立着两名穿绯红多尔曼（dolman）和紧俏矢车菊蓝裤袜的侍童，浑身缀满绣银花边。

最高的角楼窗前坐着城堡总管，只等一声号角宣告领主驾到。他头顶上，狂风正与一面巨大的紫旗角力，勉强才掀得动那垂坠的金穗。

所有的窗子里都有探头探脑的仆役和满心期盼的骑士贵妇们——不如说，除了三扇窗子而外。那三扇全然不见喜庆的人影，只看得见雪白瓷瓶里芬芳的素馨和颤动的含羞草，花后隐约可辨一个苍白娇弱的身影，正梦一般倚在绣花窗垫上。这是德尼斯·班菲的妻子。

===Original===

It might have been ten o’clock in the morning when the castellan, perceiving clouds of dust on the highway, announced the approach of his Excellency with a blast of his horn, whereupon the roar of the mortars scared everyone into his proper place; the priests and teachers reviewed their pupils, the officers marshalled their troops, and the trumpeters on the ramparts played the latest marches.

Shortly afterwards the Lord-Lieutenant arrived, escorted by the banderia of half-a-dozen counties. Before and behind him trotted squadrons of horsemen, whose arms and caparisons gleamed with all the colours of the rainbow. There were to be seen horses of every race and every hue﻿—Arabian stallions, Transylvanian full-bloods, little Wallachian ponies, slim English racers, and light-footed Barbary steeds. There were horses with flesh-coloured manes, jewelled bits, variegated reins, and embroidered schabracks. There were all the weapons with which the art of war was then familiar﻿—the slender Damascus blade, the toothed morning-star, the curved *csakany*,36 the serpentine crease, and those long, gorgeously-fashioned firearms which could seldom be discharged more than once; here and there, too, was visible a specimen of those three-edged, six feet long Turkish scimitars, which were just then coming into vogue.

Each squadron brought its banner, on which the arms of the respective counties were gaily embroidered, and sturdy standard-bearers bore them aloft on their saddlebows. In front of the martial bands rode their captain, George Veer, a muscular man of about forty, with a grey-speckled beard, stiffly waxed moustaches, and sunburnt face. A stately heron’s plume, fastened by an opal agraffe, waved from his marten-embroidered kalpag; his gorgeous bearskin was held together in front by a gold chain as broad as a man’s hand, set with gems. Chrysolites as large as filberts gleamed, instead of eyes, in the bear’s head looking over his shoulder; his body was encased in a coat of silver mail, sewn with gold stars, through which his dark-blue dolman was visible. His crooked scimitar with its golden hilt well became the hand which held it, and from his saddlebows peeped forth the menacing muzzles of a pair of pistols, the mechanism of which was about as simple as the mechanism of a modern steam-engine.

===Chinese===

上午十点钟光景，城堡总管望见大路上腾起烟尘，一声号角宣告大人老爷驾到；臼炮轰然齐吼，把每个人都吓得各归各位；神父和教师们整好学生，军官们排好队伍，壁垒上的号手吹起最新的进行曲。

不多时，州督大人在六个州的班德里亚骑兵护卫下驾临。他前后小跑着一队队骑兵，兵刃马甲虹霓般流光溢彩。马匹应有尽有，各个品种、各种毛色——阿拉伯牡马、特兰西瓦尼亚纯血马、瓦拉几亚（Wallachian）小马、苗条的英国赛马、轻捷的巴巴里骏马。有的马披着肉色鬃毛，衔着嵌宝嚼环，配着彩缰和绣花鞍褥。兵器也一应俱全，当时兵法所熟知的全在这里——大马士革细刃、带齿的晨星锤、弯柄*恰卡尼*（csakany）36、蛇形波纹短剑，还有那些奢华工巧的长火器——它们难得能连放两枪；四下里还偶尔可见三棱、六英尺长的土耳其弯刀（scimitar），那正是时新起来的式样。

每一队骑兵都打着各自的旗，旗上鲜艳地绣着本州的州徽，由健壮的旗手擎在鞍桥前。军乐队的最前面骑行着他们的统领乔治·维尔（George Veer），是个四十岁上下的精壮汉子，灰斑的络腮胡，蜡得笔硬的髭须，一张晒黑的脸。一枝气派的白鹭羽，用蛋白石饰扣（agraffe）别在绣貂的卡尔帕格（kalpag）帽上迎风招展；他那华丽的熊皮在胸前由一条手掌宽的嵌宝金链拢住。回首望他的那颗熊头上，两颗榛子大的贵橄榄石权当眼睛放着光；他身裹缀金星的银色锁子甲，甲下透出深蓝的多尔曼。那柄金柄弯刀正配握它的手，鞍桥旁还探出一对手枪阴森森的枪口，那枪机构造之"简单"，大约赶得上现代蒸汽机。

===Original===

The Lord-Lieutenant himself sat in an open carriage, drawn by five black horses, with rose-coloured, gilded harness; both panels of the carriage door bore the Banfi crest, gorgeously painted on a gold ground; behind stood two hussars with silver-embroidered mantles and white heron plumes.

With haughty dignity Denis Banfi sits back on the velvet cushions of his coach; all the pomp and splendour which surrounds him suits him well. His glossy locks leave bare his high forehead, which, together with his fine, frank eyes, bespeaks infinite good-nature, while the bold curve of the bushy eyebrows and the peculiar cut of the thin lips indicate a violent temper. The whole face seems to be constantly under the influence of these hostile emotions. At one moment it is mild, smiling, rosy; at another savage, grim, and suffused by a dark purple flush. The traces of noble enthusiasm and of unbridled fury are impressed upon his face side by side just as they are in his heart.

The martial squadrons present arms; the schoolchildren chant hymns; the vassals wave their hats; the music resounds from the battlements; the clergymen deliver addresses; and all the guests flutter their kerchiefs and their kalpags at him from the windows, and Banfi receives all these demonstrations of respect with his usual majestic dignity and condescension, with the air of a man who feels that all this sort of thing belongs to him of right. Meanwhile his eyes glance up at those three windows concealed behind the fragrant jasmines and the quivering mimosas, and his face grows graver and sadder when he perceives no one behind them.

From the window of another room there looks down a very tall old man in a long clerical surtout with small buttons. Since losing his teeth his chin has moved closer to his nose, which makes his nose look a long way from his eyes. He seems to be taking no part whatever in the general rejoicings. By his side leans a lady in mourning, wearing a black velvet *haube*; rage and contempt are unmistakably visible in her countenance. Near these two stands Master Stephen Nalaczi with folded arms, surveying the whole procession with a droll, sarcastic smile.

===Chinese===

州督大人自己坐在一辆敞篷马车里，五匹黑马拉车，玫瑰色描金辔挽；车门两边的板上，金底彩绘着班菲家的纹章；车后立着两名披银绣披风、插白鹭羽的骠骑兵。

德尼斯·班菲带着倨傲的威仪，仰坐在马车的天鹅绒软垫上；环绕他的一切富丽堂皇，与他相得益彰。油亮的鬈发露出他饱满的天庭，那天庭连同那双明朗坦率的眼睛，透出无限的厚道；而浓眉的遒劲弧线和薄唇的独特线条，又透出一副暴烈的性子。整张脸仿佛永远处在这两种敌对情绪的轮番支配之下：一时温和、含笑、红润；一时狰狞、阴沉，涨出一层紫黑的血色。高贵的热忱和狂纵的暴怒的印迹，在他脸上并肩铭刻着，正如在他心里一样。

尚武的马队列队持枪敬礼；学童们唱着圣歌；佃户们挥舞帽子；乐声在雉堞间回荡；神父们致着颂词；满窗的宾客朝他挥舞手帕和卡尔帕格帽。班菲以他惯有的君王般的尊严与谦和，接受这一切敬意，那副神情，仿佛觉得这一切本属他名下，天经地义。这中间，他的眼睛却瞟向那三扇掩在芬芳素馨和颤动含羞草后的窗子，见窗后空无一人，他的脸色便愈加凝重、愈加忧郁。

另一间屋的窗口，探出一个极高个子老头，穿一件小扣长牧师袍。自打掉了牙，他的下巴便向鼻子挪近了些，于是鼻子看起来离眼睛又格外远了。普天同庆，他似乎全然无心与闻。他身旁倚着一位穿孝的夫人，戴黑色天鹅绒*包发帽*（haube）；她脸上的怒容与轻蔑一望可知。这两人近旁站着斯蒂芬·纳拉奇老爷，抱着手臂，用一种滑稽而刻薄的微笑检阅着整个行列。

===Original===

“Just look, your Reverence,” says the lady in widow’s weeds to the grey-headed clergyman. “Did ever prince lord it with the pomp and splendour of this simple Baron? I have been at coronations, installations, inaugurations, triumphal ovations, but never, never have I seen anything like the homage paid to this private man. If they rendered it to a prince it might pass, but who, forsooth, is this Denis Banfi? Why, a simple nobleman﻿—just such a one as we are, except that he is full of arrogance and pretence. All this princely splendour does not belong to him de jure. Oh! well do I know the meaning of the word *jus*; for I have all my life been before the courts against greater lords than he.”

“How my reverend colleagues press forward to kiss his hand,” murmured Martin Kuncz (for that was the clergyman’s name). “Ei! ei! Look now, at my learned colleague Gabriel Csekalusi, how radiantly he hastens forward to assist his Excellency out of his carriage!﻿—and he is right, for Denis Banfi is the visible providence of the Calvinists. But for poor, vagabond Unitarian ministers like me, the place behind the door is good enough.”

“But just look! just look! how the worthy *armalists*37 raise him on high and carry him on their shoulders to the door. ’Tis well they do not set him on a litter like a sovereign prince﻿—as if, forsooth, feeding them at his table made him their lord and master!”

“Nay, but, Madame Saint Pauli, pray let the good people do him homage if they like,” interrupted Nalaczi with a sneer. “Wait a bit. The greeting I have in reserve for him will add salt to the soup! It will bring my lord to his senses, I warrant you!”

===Chinese===

"您瞧，神父大人。"穿孝的夫人对白头神父说，"哪有哪个君王，摆得出这位区区男爵这样的排场气派？加冕礼、登基礼、就职礼、凯旋礼，我都见识过，可给一个平头百姓行的这种大礼，我从来没有、从来没有见过。要是行给一位亲王，倒也罢了；可这位德尼斯·班菲，他算老几？哼，不过一介平常贵族——同我们一模一样的人，只是满身的傲慢与矫饰。这满堂亲王般的荣华，从法理上讲根本不属于他。哦！*jus*（法权）这个词是什么意思，我可太懂了；我这一辈子，一直在同比他大得多的老爷们打官司。"

"瞧我这班可敬的同道，挤着抢着去吻他的手。"马丁·孔茨（Martin Kuncz）——那位神父正是此名——咕哝道，"诶！诶！瞧我那位博学的同道加布里埃尔·切卡卢西（Gabriel Csekalusi），满面红光地抢上前去搀大人老爷下车！——他做得对，德尼斯·班菲是加尔文宗看得见摸得着的救星。可像我这样穷苦流浪的一神宗牧师嘛，门背后那个位置就满好了。"

"可你瞧！你瞧！那群好样的*军旗贵族*（armalist）37把他高高抬起，扛在肩上直送到门口。亏得他们没像抬国君亲王那样拿轿子抬他——倒像给他管几顿饭，他就成了他们的主子老爷似的！"

"得了，圣保利（Saint Pauli）夫人，好人们爱给他行礼，就由他们行去吧。"纳拉奇冷笑着插嘴，"稍安勿躁。我给他备下的一份见面礼，保管给这锅汤添滋味！保管让我这位老爷清醒清醒，我敢打包票！"

===Original===

Meanwhile Banfi is mounting the steps, and the crowd, pouring after him, forces its way in at the same time, and carries the Baron on its shoulders right up to the dais at the end of the room. The clergymen squeeze their way through the surging mob into their proper places, not without being mercilessly mauled on the way; while George Veer, with respect-inspiring elbows, carves a road for himself through the mob up to the very seat of the Lord-Lieutenant. The room is already crammed full with as many of the gentry as it will hold, the remainder block the corridors. The vassals remain, perforce, in the courtyard, and hear nothing of what is going on but the hubbub which reaches them through the windows, and seems to delight them amazingly.

===Chinese===

这当儿班菲正拾级而上，人群跟着涌入，也一同挤了进来，把男爵扛在肩上，一直送到厅堂尽头的台上。神父们在翻腾的人海里挤向各自的席位，一路上难免被狠狠踩挤；乔治·维尔则凭着两条令人肃然起敬的胳膊肘，在人海里给自己开路，一直杀到州督大人的座位跟前。厅里已经塞满了塞得下的全部乡绅，余下的堵在走廊里。佃户们没法子，只好留在院子里，里头的事什么也听不见，只听见从窗口传出来的喧哗——那喧哗似乎让他们乐不可支。

===Original===

“My noble friends,” said Banfi, when there was at last something like silence, and his eye had taken in everyone present, “it was not without good cause that I invited you to come to my house *armed*. You know right well from the past history of our poor fatherland, how much our nation has suffered because our Princes, either discontented with what they already had, or unable to guard it, have perpetually called in foreign troops. The historians have only recorded what has redounded to the glory of our Princes﻿—victories, battles, conquests; but they have forgotten to mention that in the year 1617, in consequence of the horrors of war, not a single child was born in the whole of Transylvania, for famine and flight killed them all in their mothers’ wombs. But we know it, for we have suffered with and for the people. Now, thank heaven! we are masters in our own homes. By the Peace of Saint Gothard, the Turkish Sultan and the German Emperor have covenanted not to march their troops through Transylvania, and by thus holding each other in check, have vouchsafed us a little breathing-space, inasmuch as we are no longer bound to take up arms for either of them, but can set about healing our country’s ancient wounds. A golden age is dawning upon us. The whole world is fighting and bleeding, we alone possess peace; in our land alone is the Magyar independent and his own master. True, ours is not a very large realm, but at any rate ’tis our own. We may be a very little people, but we recognize no greater anywhere. Now there are persons who would destroy this golden age. There are persons who do not care what an imprudently begun war may cost the country, provided their ambition, provided their greed is gratified thereby; and if he whom they attack chances to win, *they* do not perish with their country, but simply turn their coats, go over to the victors, and share the spoil with them.”

“That is a slander!” cried someone from the background. Banfi at once recognized Nalaczi’s voice.

The murmuring crowd turned towards the corner whence the interruption had proceeded.

===Chinese===

"我尊贵的朋友们，"待厅里总算安静下来、他的目光把在场众人一一扫过之后，班菲说道，"我请诸位*携武装*到舍下来，绝非无因。从我们可怜祖国的既往历史，你们深知我们这个民族吃了多少苦头——只因我们的亲王们或是不满于已有的东西，或是无力保有它，便一个劲儿招引外兵。史家们只记下给亲王们增光的东西——凯旋啊，会战啊，攻城略地啊；却忘了提一笔：一六一七年，因为兵罅的恐怖，整个特兰西瓦尼亚（Transylvania）没有一个孩子降生——饥馑和流亡把他们全杀戮在母亲的胎里了。可我们知道，因为我们同百姓一同受过苦，也替他们受过苦。如今，谢天谢地！我们成了自己家里的主人。凭圣哥达（Saint Gothard）和约，土耳其苏丹（Sultan）与德意志皇帝立约不再借道特兰西瓦尼亚行军，彼此这么一钳制，反倒赏了我们一口喘息的工夫：我们不必再为哪一方拔剑，可以着手医治祖国的多年沉疴了。一个黄金时代正在我们头顶破晓。全世界都在厮杀流血，唯我们独享太平；唯在我们的土地上，马扎尔人是独立的、自己作主的。诚然，我们的邦国不算大，但好歹是我们自己的。我们或许是很小的民族，可我们不承认天下还有更大的。如今偏有人要毁掉这个黄金时代。偏有人不管一场轻启的战端要让国家付出什么，只求他们的野心得到满足、贪欲得到餍足；倘若他们攻击的一方碰巧赢了，*他们*也不与国偕亡，只消把外套一翻，投到胜者那边，同人家分赃就是。"

"这是污蔑！"人堆后头有人喊。班菲立刻听出是纳拉奇的声音。

窃窃私语的人群朝插话的角落转过头去。

===Original===

“Let him alone, my friends,” cried Banfi; “some satellite of Master Michael Teleki’s, I suppose. Let him, too, have the benefit of freedom of speech! I, however, who am well acquainted with the upright sentiments of the Estates of the Realm, can tell you positively, that this thoughtless step can never be taken in a constitutional way, and if they attempt by secret intrigues or sudden violence to bring about what cannot be done by fair means, then too they will find me at my post. I wish to defend the realm *and* the Prince, but if it must be so, I will defend the realm against the Prince himself. Now listen to what the caballers have devised, so as to ensnare us once more in those meshes from which we have hardly withdrawn our heads. Despite the peace, Turks at one time, Tartars at another, cross our frontier, blackmail the people, burn the towns, in short, force their friendship upon us in every imaginable way. Eight days ago they ravaged Segesvar, and before that they made incursions into the Csika district. That, however, is not *my* business. It concerns the Governors of the Saxon land and the Captains of the Szeklers. It is true that the mouth of his Excellency, Ali Pasha, has long been watering for my domains, only he has not quite made up his mind how to pick a quarrel with me. A few days ago, however, his roving bands captured the Prince’s Patrol-officer, and proclaimed through his mouth to the whole district a fresh tax of a farthing per head. The poor peasantry rejoiced at getting off so cheaply, and hastened to pay the tax without first asking me whether it was lawfully levied. The artful Turk gained a double end thereby: in the first place, he got the people to recognize the tax, and in the second place, he found out exactly how many taxable persons resided in the district, and immediately afterwards levied upon them the fearful blackmail of two Hungarian florins per head!”

The multitude howled with rage.

===Chinese===

"别理他，朋友们。"班菲喊道，"多半是米哈伊·泰莱基（Michael Teleki）老爷的哪颗卫星。也让他享享言论自由的好处！不过我深知全国各等级诸公的正直心肠，可以明白告诉诸位：这步昏招绝不可能循宪政的路子走出；倘若他们想靠阴谋诡计或骤然之变，去成光明正大之途所不能成的事，那时他们也会发现我坚守在岗位上。我愿捍卫邦国*和*亲王；可事情若逼到那一步，我愿捍卫邦国，哪怕对手是亲王本人。现在听听那帮阴谋家又设下了什么圈套，要再把我们套进我们好不容易才探出脑袋来的那张罗网。和约墨迹未干，土耳其人一时、鞑靼人一时，轮番越境，敲诈百姓，焚毁城镇——总之，用一切想得出的法子把他们的'友谊'强加给我们。八天前他们蹂躏了谢什瓦尔，再往前还窜犯过奇卡（Csika）地区。不过，那不是*我的*事，那是撒克逊地方的总督们和塞克勒统领们的事。倒是阿里帕夏（Ali Pasha）大人那张嘴，觊觎我的领地已久，只是一直没拿定主意怎么同我寻衅。可不几天前，他那流窜的队伍捉住了亲王的巡察官，借他的嘴向全区宣示了每人一法新的新税。可怜的庄稼人只喜得这么便宜就脱了身，急忙把税缴了，也不先问问我这税征得合不合法。狡猾的土耳其人一举两得：第一，让百姓认下了这桩税；第二，把全区该纳税的人口查了个一清二楚，紧接着便向他们勒索了每人两匈牙利弗罗林的骇人巨款！"

人群怒吼起来。

===Original===

“I immediately forbade all further payments. This tax does not indeed fall upon our shoulders, for we are nobles; but it is just because we are the peasants’ masters that we are bound to save them from being fleeced, and defend them at all hazards. The only answer I sent to his Turkish Excellency was a pig’s tail, and if he comes to levy the tax in person, I swear by the living God, I’ll give him a buffet he won’t forget as long as he lives.”

“We will cut him to pieces!” roared the mob, striking their scabbards, and waving their morning-stars in the air.

“And now, my faithful friends, return to your tents. My seneschals will provide for your entertainment. If we must fight, I’ll tell you when.”

The excited nobility then withdrew with rattling weapons and boisterous approbation; only a few petitioners remained behind.

The Klausenburg professors invited their patron to the public examinations. Banfi promised to come, and distribute rewards to the best scholars.

As they retired Banfi beckoned to the remaining suppliants to approach one by one. The first he turned to was Master Martin Kuncz, the Bishop of the Klausenburg Unitarians.

“How can I serve your Reverence?”

“I have a complaint to make, gracious sir,” returned Kuncz, with a bow and a scrape. “The Klausenburg town-council has forcibly removed the market booths belonging to the Unitarian Church. I beg you to help us to regain possession.”

“I am very sorry I cannot help your Reverence,” returned Banfi, whistling through his teeth and buttoning up his coat. “That is a constitutional affair, and concerns the Prince. The land indeed is mine, but the cause belongs to his Highness’s Courts.”

“The Prince gave me exactly the same answer, only reversed﻿—‘The cause indeed belongs to my Courts, but the land is Banfi’s, go to him.’ ”

Banfi laughed good-humouredly, but Kuncz did not seem to regard the matter as particularly entertaining.

“Then, although my right is as clear as noonday, I can turn nowhither?”

Banfi shrugged his shoulders and stroked his beard.

“Because your Reverence has right on your side, it by no means follows that you will get justice.”

“Then his case is exactly the same as mine,” interrupted someone, and Banfi, looking round, beheld Dame Saint Pauli making towards him.

===Chinese===

"我当即禁止再缴一文。这税固然落不到我们肩上，因为我们是贵族；可正因为我们是庄稼人的主子，才更有责任不叫他们被剪毛剥皮，赴汤蹈火也要护住他们。我给那位土耳其大人阁下的全部答复，就是一条猪尾巴；他若亲自来收税，我凭永生的上帝起誓，定赏他一记终生难忘的耳光。"

"把他剁成肉泥！"人群吼道，拍着刀鞘，把晨星锤拤得呼呼生风。

"现在，我忠实的朋友们，回你们的营帐去吧。我的司务官自会款待诸位。倘要开战，我自会告知时日。"

激动的贵族们于是刀枪铿锵、喧腾叫好地退了出去；只留下几名请愿人。

克劳森堡的教授们邀请他们的恩主出席公开考试。班菲答应前往，并给最优秀的学生颁赏。

教授们退下后，班菲招手让余下的请求者一个个上前。他先转向的是马丁·孔茨老爷，克劳森堡一神宗的主教。

"我能为神父大人效什么劳？"

"我有一桩冤情要诉，仁慈的老爷。"孔茨打躬作揖地答道，"克劳森堡市政会强行拆走了一神宗教会的集市货棚。恳求老爷帮助我们夺回来。"

"很抱歉，我帮不上神父大人的忙。"班菲从牙缝里吹着口哨，扣上外套答道，"那是宪制事务，归亲王管。地界确实是我的，案子却归殿下的法院。"

"亲王给过我一模一样的答复，只是倒了个个儿——'案子确实归我的法院，可地界是班菲的，找他去。'"

班菲开怀大笑，孔茨却似乎不觉得这事有多可乐。

"那么，尽管我的权利亮得如同正午，我竟是告天天不应、告地地不灵？"

班菲耸耸肩，捋着胡子。

"神父大人有理在身，可远不等于就讨得到公道。"

"那么他这案子跟我的一模一样。"有人插嘴道。班菲回头一看，圣保利夫人正朝他走来。

===Original===

The magnate pretended he did not see the widow, and nonchalantly adjusted the gold and diamond chain of his mente; but the widow thrust herself right under his nose, and thus began﻿—

“Vainly do you condescend to ignore me, my lord. I am here though uninvited.”

Banfi looked at her without saying a word, half amused and half annoyed.

“Or perhaps your lordship has forgotten my name?” continued the lady sharply, smiting her breast and exclaiming﻿—“I am the noble, highborn﻿—”

“And worshipful,” added Banfi, laughing.

“Dowager Lady George Saint Pauli,” continued the lady imperturbably, “every scion of whose family is as noble and illustrious as the Prince himself. I too have never forgotten what name I bear, but have proudly confessed it before princes and generals﻿—yea, even before greater men than your Excellency.”

“Well, well, your ladyship. All that I know by heart, for I have heard it from your own lips twenty times before. Come, tell me quickly what you want.”

“Quickly, forsooth! Perchance your Excellency imagines that it is possible to tell in a few words why the suit between us has lasted four years already, and why the suit between the town of Klausenburg and my family has been pending for three-and-sixty years?”

“To cut matters short, I will tell you the whole story myself,” interrupted Banfi; “your ladyship can make your comment afterwards. Your ladyship possesses a ruinous den in the midst of the Klausenburg marketplace﻿—”

“I beg your pardon﻿—a manor-house just as good as your lordship’s own castle.”

“This shanty has for a long time disfigured the marketplace. In vain has the town-council negotiated with and sued your family in order to have the house pulled down.”

“And we have not surrendered it. Quite right. A genuine nobleman never sells property which he has purchased with his blood. It belongs to me, and within my four walls neither Prince nor Diet has the right to command. No, nor you either, my Lord-General.”

“My good lady, I never asked you to give me this venerable ruin for nothing. I offered you ten thousand florins for it. For that sum I could have bought up the whole gipsy quarter, though there is no such dilapidated house there as yours.”

===Chinese===

大贵族装作没看见这位寡妇，漫不经心地摆弄他门特披风上嵌金钻的衣链；寡妇却一头拱到他鼻子底下，开了腔——

"老爷枉自屈尊对我视而不见。我不请自来，就在这儿。"

班菲望着她，一言不发，半觉好笑，半觉恼火。

"莫非老爷忘了我的名姓？"夫人尖刻地接着说，捶着胸口朗声宣布——"本人乃高贵名门的——"

"暨可敬的。"班菲笑着补了一句。

"乔治·圣保利太夫人。"夫人不动声色地接着说，"这一门的每一根苗裔，都同亲王本人一样高贵显赫。我也从不曾忘了我姓甚名谁，在亲王和将军们面前——是的，在比老爷您更伟大的人物面前——我都骄傲地自报过家门。"

"好了好了，夫人。这一套我倒背如流，亲耳听您念叨过不下二十遍了。来，快说说您要什么。"

"快说说！倒是想得美！老爷莫非以为，你我之间的官司已经打了四年，克劳森堡城与我家族之间的官司已经悬了六十三年，这里头的情由，三言两语就说得清？"

"长话短说，我自己替您讲。"班菲打断她，"您再补充好了。夫人在克劳森堡市场正当中，有一处塌了半边的破窝——"

"恕我不敢苟同——那是一座庄宅，同老爷您自家的城堡一样体面。"

"这座破棚子败坏市容已久。市政会又是商量又是兴讼，要拆掉此屋，全属徒劳。"

"我们就是不给。给得对。真正的贵族，绝不卖掉用血换来的产业。它是我的；在我这四堵墙里，亲王也罢，议会（Diet）也罢，都无权发号施令。老爷您，将军阁下，也一样不行。"

"我的好夫人，我从没白要您这座年高德劭的破屋。我出过一万弗罗林。这笔钱够把整个吉卜赛街区买下来——那里面也没有一栋像您这栋这样破的。"

===Original===

“Keep your money, sir. I’ll not give up my house. My seven-and-seventieth ancestor bought it two centuries ago, and therefore I’ll not barter it away. In it I was born; in it died my father and my mother. If it offends your Excellency’s eye to look down upon my beggarly house from your splendid mansion, pray look the other way; but at least do not grudge me the poor pleasure of spending the remainder of my days in the room where my poor husband breathed his last sigh; and let me tell you, sir, that I wouldn’t take a palace in exchange for it.”

The widow’s sobs at the recollection of her deceased husband here enabled Banfi to put a word in, and he replied with passionate vehemence﻿—

“What I have said shall be done. The masons are already on their way to pull down the house. The ten thousand florins you can have on application to the town-council.”

“I don’t want them. Throw them to your dogs,” cried the woman furiously. “Am I a peasant that you turn me thus out of my property? Whoever dares to step across my threshold shall be driven out with a broomstick like a cur. I have appealed to the Prince and to the Estates, and there you have the sealed mandate in which the Diet forbids all and sundry to invade my property. I’ll nail it upon the gate﻿—’tis engrossed in a good, legible hand﻿—and then I’ll see who dares to break into my house.”

“And I tell you that tomorrow your house will be razed to the ground, even if it be surrounded by *armalists*, and then the Diet may build you a new one if it is so disposed.”

And with that Banfi turned away in high dudgeon, and almost ran into Nalaczi.

The two men greeted each other with constrained politeness; and while Dame Saint Pauli went off cursing, Nalaczi, after drawing a long breath, began in the sweetest of tones﻿—

“His Highness the Prince desires to bring a very unpleasant matter to the notice of your Excellency.”

“I am all attention.”

“The Turk has thrice this year extorted gifts from us under various pretexts.”

“You ought not to give them to him.”

“If we don’t he will force upon us as Prince the refugee Nicholas Zolyomi, now under the protection of the Porte.”

“Let him come! We will kick him out again.”

“Bravely spoken! But the Prince, weary of so much discord, and somewhat fearful besides, has resolved to amnesty Zolyomi and allow him to return.”

“In God’s name let him do so then!”

===Chinese===

"收起您的钱吧，先生。我不交我的房子。我的第七十七代祖上两个世纪前买下它，所以我决不出让。我生在这屋里；我的父亲、我的母亲都死在这屋里。倘若您从华厦里俯望我这寒舍碍了老爷的尊目，就请把眼睛望向别处；可至少别剥夺我这点点可怜的乐趣——在我可怜的丈夫咽下最后一口气的屋子里了却残生。还告诉您，先生，拿一座宫殿来换，我也不换。"

寡妇想起亡夫，呜咽起来，班菲这才得着插话的空当，他怒气冲冲地答道——

"我说的就要照办。泥水匠已经在拆屋的路上了。那一万弗罗林，您找市政会去领。"

"我不要。扔给您的狗去吧。"妇人怒骂道，"我是庄稼人，由得你们这样把我撵出自己的产业？谁敢跨进我的门槛，我就拿扫帚把他当疯狗一样轰出去。我告到亲王和各等级面前，啾，这就是盖了印的诏令，议会明令禁止任何人侵犯我的产业。我要把它钉在大门上——它誊写得工整清楚——然后我倒要看看，谁敢闯我的家。"

"我也告诉你：明天你的房子就要夷为平地，哪怕围满了*军旗贵族*，也拦不住；到时候议会若有兴致，自会给你盖新的。"

说罢班菲怒冲冲地转身就走，差点一头撞上纳拉奇。

两人强作礼数地互相见礼；圣保利夫人口中咒骂着走远了，纳拉奇深吸一口气，用最甜的声调开了口——

"亲王殿下有一件极不愉快的事，想让大人知晓。"

"洗耳恭听。"

"土耳其人今年已三次借口种种，向我们勒索礼金。"

"你们本不该给。"

"不给，他就要把如今受奥斯曼朝廷庇护的流亡者尼古拉·佐约米（Nicholas Zolyomi）强塞给我们当亲王。"

"来就来！我们再把他踢出去就是。"

"好豪气！只是亲王苦于纷争太久，又兼有几分畏惧，已决意赦免佐约米，准他回来。"

"那就奉上帝的名让他赦好了！"

===Original===

“Right, quite right! But your lordship knows very well that Zolyomi’s estates are now in your lordship’s possession; the Prince therefore finds himself compelled to request your lordship to surrender these estates to the returning Zolyomi, if it would not greatly inconvenience your lordship.”

Nalaczi had been a little too curt in the delivery of his message, although he had done his best to sugar it with respectful epithets.

“What!” cried Banfi, stepping back, “do you really suppose that I will give up these estates? The Diet gave them to me with the onerous condition of equipping at my own cost twelve regiments for the defence of the country. That onerous condition I have faithfully fulfilled, and now you fancy that I shall surrender the estates merely because there is to be one fool the more in the land? Preposterous!”

“But if the Prince wishes it!”

“I’ll not give them up whoever wishes it.”

“And that is the answer I’m to take back?”

“You’ll please take back these two words,” said Banfi, emphasizing each syllable﻿—“I won’t!”

“Your most obedient servant,” said Nalaczi, and with an ironical obeisance he turned upon his heel.

“Servus,” replied Banfi contemptuously, as if he were throwing a bone to a dog; and then he looked out into the corridor, and seeing some of his vassals waiting there, hat in hand, roughly asked them what they wanted.

When the good people saw that their liege lord was in a villainous humour, they held back, but the steward pushed them in.

“We ought to have brought the tithes,” began the oldest peasant, with a whining voice and downcast eyes, “but it was impossible.”

“Why?”

“Because we have nothing, my lord. There has been no rain; the crops are a failure; we have not even seed enough to sow our fields. In the village the people are living on chance roots and fungus, and when these are all gone, God only knows what will become of us.”

“Look now,” cried Banfi, “another visitation of God, and yet we must needs have a war to boot! Steward, open at once the demesne granaries, and distribute seed to the vassals, that they may sow their fields. See too that the poor people have enough corn to feed them through the winter.”

The poor peasants would have kissed Banfi’s hands, but he would not suffer it. A tear stood in his eye.

===Chinese===

"对，对得很！只是大人知道，佐约米的产业如今正在大人手中；亲王因此不得不恳请大人把这些产业交还给归来的佐约米——倘若这不至于给大人添太大的不便。"

纳拉奇传这番话未免太简促了些，尽管他已竭力拿恭敬的头衔给它裹糖衣。

"什么！"班菲倒退一步，喊道，"你们当真以为我会交出这些产业？议会把它们给了我，附带的重担是：由我自掏腰包装备十二个团来保卫国家。这副重担我一字不差地扛了下来，如今你们倒想，只因为国里要多一个蠢材，我就得把产业拱手交出去？妄想！"

"可亲王有此意愿！"

"谁有此意愿我也不交。"

"这就是叫我带回去的答复？"

"那就请你带回这两个字。"班菲一字一顿地说，"不、交！"

"卑职告退。"纳拉奇说着，行了个讥诮的礼，转身便走。

"回见。"班菲轻蔑地答道，像扔骨头给狗似的；随后他朝走廊里张望，见几个佃户手拿帽子候在那儿，便粗声粗气地问他们要什么。

好人们见领主心情恶劣，都缩着不敢上前，总管把他们推了进去。

"我们本该把什一税送来的。"最年长的庄稼汉开口了，哭腔哭调，眼皮耷拉着，"可实在办不到。"

"为什么？"

"因为我们一无所有了，老爷。一直没下雨；庄稼绝了收；连来年下种的种子都不够了。村里人如今靠野菜草根和蘑菇过活，等这些也吃光了，只有天知道我们会落个什么下场。"

"瞧吧，"班菲喊道，"天灾又降一场，偏还要再打一场仗！总管，立刻打开庄园粮仓，给佃户们分发种子，让他们下田播种。再看着点，别让穷人们过冬的口粮不够。"

穷庄稼人要吻班菲的手，他不许。他眼里噙着一滴泪。

===Original===

“For what am I your lord if not to lighten your burdens when you are in need? My stewards will carry out my orders. If my own storehouses fall short, you shall have corn for ready money from Moldavia.”

And with that he retired into the adjoining chamber.

---

Banfi’s wife with a beating heart heard his familiar footsteps drawing nearer.

There she sits behind the fragrant jasmines and the quivering mimosas, herself as pale as the jasmine flowers and as tremulous as the mimosas.

Around her is nothing but pomp and splendour. On the walls hang cut Venetian mirrors in gold frames, portraits of kings and princes, the handsomest among which is John Kemeny’s, painted while he still held with the Turk and wore close-cropped hair and a long beard in the Turkish fashion, so much affected by the magnates of those days.

On one side of the room is a wardrobe with countless drawers, a masterpiece of art, inlaid with tortoiseshell, lapis lazuli, and mother-of-pearl. In the centre of the room stands a variegated table surmounted by silver candelabra of exquisite workmanship. Within glass almeries the family treasures are piled up in gorgeous heaps: pocals encrusted with gems; gold-enamelled stags, whose heads can be screwed off and on; large silver filigree flower-baskets, each scarcely heavier than a crown-piece, filled with posies of precious stones of every hue, artistically disposed in dazzling groups, with here and there a butterfly poising above them with delicate wings of transparent gold.

Heavy red silk curtains fall down from the lofty windows to the floor, and the windowsills are covered with the most gorgeous of the flowers then in vogue, among which the shining, velvety, amaranthine cock’s-comb, the liriodendron with its dependent, tulip-like calices, and the mesembryanthemum, with its leaves like dewy pearls, are the most conspicuous.

===Chinese===

"你们遭了难，我不替你们卸担子，还做什么你们的老爷？我的管事们自会照我的吩咐办。我自家的粮仓若是不够，就现钱去摩尔达维亚给你们籴粮。"

说罢，他退进隔壁的屋子。

---

班菲的妻子心怦怦跳着，听着那熟悉的脚步声越来越近。

她坐在那里，藏在芬芳的素馨与颤动的含羞草后面，人同素馨一般苍白，同含羞草一般颤抖。

她四下里无非富贵气象。墙上挂着金框的威尼斯琢磨镜、列王与亲王的画像，其中最俊的一幅是约翰·凯迈尼（John Kemeny）的，画于他尚与土耳其人一气的时候：剃短的头发，土耳其式的长须——那是当年大贵族们最讲究的打扮。

房间一侧立着一口有无数抽屉的大衣柜，嵌着玳瑁、青金石和珠母，堪称艺术杰作。屋子中央是一张五彩石面桌，桌上摆着工艺绝伦的银烛台。玻璃柜里，家藏珍宝堆得琳琅满目：嵌满宝石的高脚杯；金珐琅的牡鹿，鹿头可以旋下旋上；大件的银丝细工花篮，每只几乎不比一枚克朗重，篮里插满各色宝石扎成的花束，布置得错彩镂金，其间还有蝴蝶点着透明的金薄翅悬在上面。

深红的绸帷从高窗直垂到地，窗台上摆满了当时最时髦的奇花，其中最惹眼的是绒亮的天鹅绒紫鸡冠花、垂着郁金香状花萼的鹅掌楸，还有叶片如同露珠的日中花。

===Original===

Of all these flowers only the trembling mimosa and the pale jasmine harmonized with the lady of the house, whose face contrasted so sadly with the gorgeous abode. The tiny, delicate figure seemed almost lost in the lofty arched room. She could not even have moved one of the massive morocco armchairs, nor have raised one of the huge heavy candlesticks, nor have pulled aside one of the heavy atlas curtains. Everything around her seemed to remind her of her feebleness. Every sound made her nervous, and when the well-known footsteps reached her threshold, all the blood rushed to her face. She was about to leap up when the door opened, and immediately she was as pale again as ever, and incapable of rising from her seat.

Banfi hastened, with expansive joy, towards his trembling wife, who could not for the moment find words to welcome him, seized both her delicate hands, and looked kindly into her dreamy eyes.

“So pretty and yet so sad!”

The lady tried to smile.

“And how sad that smile is too,” remarked Banfi, gently embracing the sylphlike lady.

Lady Banfi laid her head on her husband’s bosom, threw her arms round his neck, drew down his face to hers, and kissed it.

“That kiss too, how sad it is!”

She turned away to conceal her tears.

“What is it?” asked Banfi, stroking his wife’s forehead. “What is the matter? Why are you so pale? What do you want?”

“What do I want?” returned Lady Banfi, turning her streaming eyes up to her husband and sighing deeply. Then she dried her eyes, placed her arm in his, and as if to give another turn to the conversation, led him to her flowers.

“Look at that passionflower, how withered it is, and yet it is planted in a porcelain vase, and I water it every day with distilled water. But once I forgot to draw up the blinds, and now look how the poor thing has faded. It wants nothing﻿—but sunshine.”

“It seems,” said Banfi, in a low voice, “as if we were to address each other in the language of flowers.”

“What do I want?” repeated Lady Banfi, and leaning on her husband’s neck, she burst forth sobbing. “I want my sunshine﻿—your love.”

Banfi at that moment looked very uncomfortable. He sat down on his wife’s chair, took her gently upon his knee, and asked her in a kind tone, but not without a touch of temper too﻿—

“Am I less able to show you my love now than heretofore?”

===Chinese===

这满室的繁花中，唯有颤动的含羞草和苍白的素馨，同这宅子的女主人相配；她的面容，在这满堂华彩中间显得何等凄清。那纤小的身影，在高旷的穹顶屋里几乎要被淹没。她连一张沉重的摩洛哥皮扶手大椅都挪不动，连一座敦实的重烛台都举不起，连一挂厚实的缎纹窗帘都拉不开。身边的一切似乎都在提醒她的孱弱。一点声响都叫她心惊，等那熟悉的脚步到了门槛，血一下子全涌上她的脸。门开时她正要跳起来，随即又苍白如故，连身子都抬不起来了。

班菲怀着舒展的欢欣，快步走向发颤的妻子——她一时竟找不出迎他的言语；他握住她一双纤手，慈和地望着她那双梦似的眼睛。

"这么美，却又这么愁！"

夫人勉强笑了笑。

"这笑也这么愁。"班菲说着，轻轻拥住这窈窕如仙的女子。

班菲夫人把头贴上丈夫的胸膛，双臂环住他的脖颈，把他的脸拉向自己，吻了上去。

"这一吻，也这么愁！"

她转过脸去，掩藏眼泪。

"怎么了？"班菲抚着妻子的额头问，"出什么事了？你怎么这样苍白？你想要什么？"

"我想要什么？"班菲夫人抬起泪汪汪的眼睛望着丈夫，深深叹息。然后她拭干眼睛，挽起他的手臂，像是要把话头岔开，引他去看她的花。

"瞧那棵西番莲，蔫成什么样了；明明栽在瓷盆里，我天天拿蒸馏水浇它。可有回我忘了拉起百叶帘，瞧瞧，这可怜的东西就憔悴了。它什么都不要——只要阳光。"

"看来，"班菲低声说，"我们是要用花语来交谈了。"

"我想要什么？"班菲夫人又问了一遍，倚着丈夫的脖颈，放声呜咽起来。"我要我的阳光——你的爱。"

班菲这时显得极不自在。他在妻子的椅子上坐下，轻轻把她抱上膝头，用温存的口吻问——只是里头也带着一点火气——

"比起从前，难道我现在更没有本事向你证明我的爱了？"

===Original===

“Oh, no!﻿—not less! But I see you so seldom. You have been away these six weeks, and you would not let me come to you.”

“What, my lady! Have you suddenly become ambitious? Would you shine at the court of the Prince? Believe me, your court is much more splendid than his, and not nearly so dangerous.”

“Oh, you know right well that I neither seek splendour nor fear danger. When our only shelter was a rude simple hut, nay, sometimes only a tent, half buried in the snow, then you made me lay my head upon your breast, covered me with your mantle, and I was so happy, oh, so happy. Oftentimes the din of battle, the thunder of the cannon, scared sleep from our eyes, and yet I was so happy. You mounted your horse, I sank down in prayer; and when you came back blood- and dust-stained, but unhurt, how happy I was then!”

“Heaven grant that you may always be so. But there is a happiness which stands higher than domestic happiness; there are matters where the mere sight of you would be to me a hindrance and an obstacle.”

“Oh, I know what they are﻿—sweet adventures, lovely women, eh?” returned Lady Banfi, with an arch voice but perhaps a bleeding heart.

“You are mistaken,” cried Banfi, springing hastily from his chair. “I was alluding to the commonweal,” and he began to pace angrily up and down the room.

When a husband takes umbrage at such jests, it is a sure sign that he feels himself hit.

At last Banfi unknitted his bushy brows and stood stock still before his trembling wife, who, ever since her husband entered the room, had been the prey of the most conflicting emotions; joy and grief, fear and rage, love and jealousy, still struggled for the mastery in her agitated breast.

“Margaret,” he began, in an unsteady voice, “Margaret, you are jealous, and jealousy is the first step towards hatred.”

“Then hate me rather than forget me!” cried the lady with a sudden outburst, which she instantly regretted.

===Chinese===

"哦，不！不是更少！可我太少见到你了。你一走就是六个星期，又不许我到你那儿去。"

"怎么，夫人！您忽然生出野心来了？想在亲王的宫廷里争光？相信我，您的宫廷比他的排场得多，也远没有那么危险。"

"哦，你明知道我不求排场，也不怕危险。当年我们唯一的栖身之处不过一间粗陋的茅屋——不，有时只是一顶半埋在雪里的帐篷——那时你让我把头枕在你胸上，把披风盖在我身上，我多幸福啊，哦，多幸福。多少回厮杀的喧嚣、大炮的轰鸣吓得我们合不上眼，可我还是那么幸福。你跨上战马，我跪下祈祷；等你浑身血污尘土、却毫发无伤地回来，那时我又是多幸福啊！"

"上天保佑你永远这样。可有比闺房之福更高一级的幸福；有些事情，单是你一露面，对我就是妨碍和牵绊。"

"哦，我知道是什么——香艳的奇遇，可人的美人儿，嗯？"班菲夫人答道，声音俏皮，心里也许在滴血。

"你猜错了。"班菲喊道，急忙从椅子上跳起来，"我说的是国家大事。"说罢他开始在屋里怒冲冲地来回踱步。

做丈夫的若被这类玩笑戳得跳起来，那准是被说中了。

末了，班菲舒开紧锁的浓眉，直挺挺立在发颤的妻子面前。自打丈夫进屋，她便一直被最矛盾的情感轮番撕扯；欢喜与悲伤、恐惧与愤怒、爱与嫉妒，仍在她激荡的胸中争夺主导。

"玛格丽特（Margaret），"他声音不稳地开口了，"玛格丽特，你在吃醋，而吃醋是通向仇恨的第一步。"

"那就恨我吧，只别忘了我！"夫人蓦地迸出这句，话一出口就悔了。

===Original===

“But what do you want me to do? Have you a single reason for suspecting me? Perhaps you want me to render you an exact account of how many miles I’ve travelled, how many people I’ve spoken to, like that blockhead Gida Bertai, for instance, who takes a diary with him every time he leaves the house, and reports to his better-half every half-hour? To hear you speak, one would fancy that I keep you under lock and key, like Abraham Thoroczkai keeps his wife, who, whenever he goes from home, puts a padlock on his wife’s chamber, and on his return exacts an oath from all his neighbours that no one has spoken to her in the meantime.”

Lady Banfi laughed, but it was a laugh which ended in a sigh.

“You evade the question with a jest. I certainly do not accuse you. I do not watch you, and if you were to deceive me I should be none the wiser. But look! there is that in a woman’s heart (a sort of sixth sense) which smarts she knows not why, and whereby she can tell instinctively whether her beloved’s love is on the wax or wane. I know not, nor wish to know, whence you come and whither you go; but this I do know﻿—you stay away a long time, and do not make much haste in coming back. Banfi, I suffer﻿—I suffer more than you can think.”

“Madame!” cried Banfi, turning upon his wife with a flushed face, “in this country divorce suits do not last very long!”

Lady Banfi fell back into her chair, pressed her hands to her heart, and gasped for breath. She uttered one sharp, plaintive cry, but no other sound came from her parted lips. It was as though someone had suddenly severed the strings of a harp with a sword.

Half fainting, the wife looked up at her husband, as if to make sure whether after all it was not a mere jest, though certainly a very ghastly one.

“You are unhappy,” continued Banfi, “and I cannot help you. You are so romantic, and I’m not given that way at all. Perhaps my heart wounds yours, and I’m sorry for it; but your heart certainly wounds mine, and I won’t stand it. I recognize no tyrant over me, not even in love, and I’ll not endure persecution﻿—no, not even the persecution of a woman’s tears. Let us rend our hearts asunder. Better do it now while they will still bleed from the rupture than wait till they drop away of their own accord. Let us rather part while we still love one another, than wait till we have learned to hate.”

===Chinese===

"可你要我怎么办？你可有一丝一毫疑我的凭据？莫非你要我给你报一份流水账——走了多少里路、同多少人说过话——像吉达·贝尔陶伊（Gida Bertai）那个蠢货似的，每回出门都揣一本日记，每隔半个钟头向老婆大人汇报一回？听你说话的口气，倒好像我把你锁进柜子里了，像亚伯拉罕·托罗茨考伊（Abraham Thoroczkai）看管他老婆那样——那家伙每次出门，都给老婆的房门挂上一把挂锁，回来还要左邻右舍人人宣誓，说这中间谁也没跟她说过话。"

班菲夫人笑了，可那笑声以叹息收了尾。

"你拿玩笑搪塞问题。我固然不指控你。我不监视你；你就是骗了我，我也无从知晓。可是你瞧！女人心里有一种东西（算是一种第六感吧），不知为何就隐隐作痛，凭它她本能地知道爱人的爱是渐盈还是渐亏。你从哪里来、往哪里去，我不知道，也不想知道；可这一点我知道——你一走许久，也不急着回来。班菲，我在受苦——我受的苦，你想都想不到。"

"夫人！"班菲涨红着脸冲妻子喝道，"在这个国家，离婚的官司可拖不了多久！"

班菲夫人跌回椅中，双手按住心口，喘不上气来。她发出一声尖利的哀鸣，张开的唇间却再吐不出别的声音——仿佛有人挥剑，蓦地割断了竖琴的琴弦。

半晕过去的妻子仰望丈夫，仿佛要确认这到底是不是一句玩笑——尽管是句狰狞至极的玩笑。

"你不幸福，"班菲接着说，"我帮不了你。你太多情善感，我天生不是这种性子。也许我的心伤了你的心，我很抱歉；可你的心也确实在伤我的心，我忍不下去了。我不承认头上有暴君，爱情里也不承认；我不愿受逼迫——不，哪怕是女人眼泪的逼迫。让我们把两颗心撕开吧。趁撕开还会流血的时候撕，总好过等到它们自己枯落。趁我们还在相爱的时候分手，总好过等到我们学会相恨。"

===Original===

During the whole of this cruel speech the lady panted convulsively for breath, as if a heavy nightmare were pressing upon her bosom and depriving her of speech, till at last her emotion found an escape, and she uttered a piercing scream.

“Banfi! you are killing me!”

Banfi himself seemed aghast at this cry, and turning round in the very act of quitting the room, cast a glance at his wife.

He did not perceive that at that moment the door opened and someone entered; he only saw that his wife’s agonized countenance was suddenly distorted by an unspeakably painful smile. A forced smile on those convulsed features was something too terrible. Banfi thought at first that his wife had gone mad.

The next instant Dame Banfi rose impetuously from her chair, and exclaiming, “Anna! my darling Anna!” rushed towards the door.

It was then that Banfi turned round, and saw before him Anna Bornemissa, the consort of Michael Apafi. That lady’s sharp eyes instantly detected the agitation of the consorts, though they both did their best to hide it, and not without success. But she made as though she saw nothing, and drawing Margaret to her breast, kindly held out her hand to Banfi.

“I heard your voices outside,” said she, “so I came in without waiting to be announced.”

“Ah, yes﻿ ﻿… we were﻿ ﻿… laughing,” said Dame Banfi, covertly wiping her eyes with the corner of her pocket-handkerchief.

“And to what circumstances do we owe this extraordinary piece of good fortune?” asked Banfi, concealing his embarrassment behind an exaggerated courtesy.

“As you did not bring my sister to see me,” returned the Princess, with a reproachful smile, “I thought I would just visit my poor exiled Hungarian kinswoman myself.”

Banfi felt the sting of these last words, and murmured as he stroked his beard﻿—

“Here my fair sister-in-law may do with me what she will. She may make me the butt of her sparkling wit; she may overwhelm me with her playful sallies. In the Hall of the Diet, before the throne of the Prince, we stand, face to face, as foes; but here you may command me, here I am only your most devoted servant, who delights to do homage to your charms, and is beside himself for joy to have you as his guest.”

With these words Banfi embraced the majestic lady with easy familiarity; then, turning to his wife, added, not without a touch of malice﻿—

“I hope you will not be jealous of Anna?”

===Chinese===

这篇残酷的话从头到尾，夫人都痉挛地喘息，仿佛一个沉重的梦魇压在胸口，夺去了她的言语；末了，郁积的情感终于找到了出口，她迸出一声刺耳的尖叫。

"班菲！你要杀死我了！"

这声喊叫让班菲自己也骇然了；正要出门的他转过身，朝妻子望了一眼。

他没有察觉门正巧开了，有人走了进来；他只看见妻子那张痛苦的脸，忽然被一个说不出的惨笑扭曲了。在那张抽搐的脸上硬挤出的笑，实在太可怕了。班菲起先以为妻子疯了。

下一瞬，班菲夫人猛地从椅子上站起，喊着"安娜！我亲爱的安娜！"朝门口扑去。

直到这时班菲才转过身，看见面前站着安娜·博尔内米莎，米哈伊·阿帕菲（Michael Apafi）的夫人。那位夫人锐利的眼睛霎时就察觉了这对夫妇的激动——尽管两人都竭力掩饰，而且掩饰得不算不成功。可她只作什么也没看见，把玛格丽特搂到怀里，又和颜悦色地向班菲伸出手去。

"我在外头听见你们说话，"她说，"便不等通报，径直进来了。"

"啊，是啊……我们正……笑呢。"班菲夫人说，用手帕一角悄悄拭着眼睛。

"是何等机缘，让我们得享这般难得的福气？"班菲问，把狼狈藏在一副夸张的殷勤后面。

"既然您不肯带我妹妹来看我，"亲王夫人带着嗔怪的微笑答道，"我想，就由我自己来看看我这个流亡的可怜匈牙利亲戚吧。"

最后这几个字扎得班菲生疼，他捋着胡子咕哝道——

"在这里，我这位美丽的姻妹要拿我怎样便怎样。她尽管拿我当她妙语的靶子；尽管用她的俏皮话把我淹没。在议会大厅里、在亲王座前，我们面对面站着，是仇敌；可在这里，您尽可差遣我，在这里我只是您最恭顺的仆人，乐于拜倒在您的风致之下，为您做客而喜得忘形。"

说罢，班菲亲亲热热、不拘礼数地拥抱了这位雍容的夫人；然后转向妻子，添上一句，不无一点恶意——

"我想你不会吃安娜的醋吧？"

===Original===

The Princess hastened to reply instead of Margaret.

“Methinks you fear me too much to make love to me.”

“I might perhaps if you were my wife. Yet we were near being wedded once. There was a time when I wanted to make you my bride.”

“But it went no further than wishing,” returned the Princess, laughing.

“We soon learned to know each other,” continued Banfi. “There would have been no room in one house for two such heads as ours, which find one realm too small to hold them both. We both of us love to rule. We should have been hard put to it if one had been obliged to obey the other. Things fell out for the best. We have found our corresponding halves﻿—you Apafi; I Margaret﻿—and we are both contented.”

With these words Banfi tenderly kissed his wife’s hand and departed, leaving the sisters alone.

Anna, with noble gravity, placed her hand on the shoulder of her sister, who looked up to her with a soft smile like an innocent child regarding its guardian angel.

“You have been weeping,” began the Princess; “ ’tis in vain that you try to put a good face on it.”

“I have not been weeping!” returned Margaret, keeping her countenance with wonderful self-control.

“Well, well; I’m glad you conceal it. That shows you love him; and if ever there was a time when your husband needed your love, your watchfulness, and your protection, it is now.”

“Your words alarm me! You have something extraordinary to tell me!”

“My coming here at all must have been enough to have alarmed you. You may well suppose that I would not come to your castle for nothing. We have both equal cause to fear a certain person, and if we do not quickly come to an understanding, one of us may lose what she prizes most in the world.”

“Speak! oh, speak!” cried Dame Banfi, trembling, and making her sister sit down beside her on the sofa.

“Our husbands have hated each other from the first. They were always of different opinions, belonged to opposite parties, and early became accustomed to regard each other as foes. Woe betide us if this hatred should turn to open strife, and we should see our loved ones compass each other’s ruin.”

“Oh, I can positively assure you that Banfi nourishes no hostile feeling against your husband.”

===Chinese===

亲王夫人抢在玛格丽特前头答了话。

"依我看，你怕我还怕不过来呢，哪里敢向我求爱。"

"你若是我妻子，我或许还敢。想当年，我们差一点成了亲。有一阵子，我一心想娶你做新娘。"

"可也就止于想想罢了。"亲王夫人笑着答。

"我们很快就认清了彼此。"班菲接着说，"我们这两颗脑袋，一个王国都嫌小，容不进一间屋子。我们俩都爱发号施令。真要一个服从另一个，那可就难为我们了。结果倒落得两全。我们各找到了自己的另一半——你配了阿帕菲；我配了玛格丽特——我们俩都心满意足。"

说着，班菲温存地吻了妻子的手，退了出去，留下姐妹俩独处。

安娜带着高贵的端凝，把手搭在妹妹肩上；妹妹仰起脸朝她柔柔地笑，像天真的孩子仰望守护天使。

"你哭过了。"亲王夫人开口道，"想装作没事，也是枉然。"

"我才没哭！"玛格丽特答道，以惊人的自制绷住面孔。

"好吧，好吧；你肯瞒着，我倒高兴。这说明你还爱他；而倘若真有一个时候，你丈夫最需要你的爱、你的警醒、你的庇护，那就是现在。"

"你的话叫我心惊！你一定有要紧事要告诉我！"

"单是我到这儿来，就够叫你心惊了。你尽可想见，我绝不会无缘无故登你城堡的门。我们俩有同等的缘由惧怕某一个人；若不赶紧同心合计，我们中的一个，就可能失去她在这世上最珍视的东西。"

"说呀！哦，说呀！"班菲夫人喊着，浑身发抖，拉姐姐在身边的沙发上坐下。

"我们的丈夫从一开始就互相仇恨。他们意见总是相左，党派素来相对，早早惯于把对方当作仇敌。这仇恨若化作明火执仗的争斗，眼睁睁看着我们心爱的人把彼此逼上绝路，那我们可就惨了。"

"哦，我可以向你担保，班菲对你丈夫绝无半点敌意。"

===Original===

“I do not apprehend Apafi’s fall, but your husband’s. The throne upon which he was placed by force has quite changed Apafi’s character. I perceive, to my consternation, that he has begun to grow jealous of his authority. Why, even at Érsekújvár, when he first became Prince, he expressed his anxiety to the Grand Vizier that Gabriel Haller was plotting for the diadem, whereupon the Grand Vizier had poor Haller beheaded there and then without my husband’s knowledge; but Apafi still recollects the message your husband sent him on that occasion, namely, that ere long he would tear from his shoulders the green velvet mantle, the symbol of the princely dignity.”

“Oh, my God! what must I not fear?”

“Nothing, so long as I do not lose my husband’s favour. While you are securely sleeping, I am watchfully guarding against his passionate outbursts, and hitherto God has given me strength to fight against the monsters who would make of his reign a bloody memorial. But there is a certain condition of mind to which my husband is liable when my influence over him loses all its talismanic power; when, revolting against his own nature, his gentleness turns to ravening savagery; when his eyes, usually so ready to weep at the death of his lowliest vassal, seem to thirst for blood; when he throws off his habitual circumspectness and becomes wildly reckless. And this condition﻿—I blush to confess it﻿—is drunkenness. I do not bring it against him as an accusation. He whom we love has no fault in our eyes.”

“Except one thing﻿—his infidelity to us,” interrupted Margaret.

“That too, yes, that too must be forgiven when it becomes a question of saving his life,” replied the Princess.

“Oh, Anna!” cried Margaret, “you make me suspect mysteries which you will not reveal to me.”

===Chinese===

"我担心的不是阿帕菲倒台，是你丈夫的下场。那把他被强按上去的王座，把阿帕菲的性子全变了。我惊恐地看出，他已开始对自己的权柄生出妒心。就说在埃尔谢克乌伊瓦尔吧，他刚当亲王那阵，就向大维齐尔表过忧心，说加布里埃尔·哈勒在图谋王冠；大维齐尔当即——瞒着我丈夫——把可怜的哈勒砍了头；可你丈夫当时捎给他的那句话，阿帕菲至今记得：不日便要把那件亲王尊位的象征——绿天鹅绒披风——从他肩上撕下来。"

"哦，我的上帝！还有什么是我不用怕的？"

"什么都不用怕——只要我一天不失我丈夫的欢心。你们安然酣睡的时候，我正彻夜提防着他的暴怒发作；到如今，上帝一直赐我力气，同那些想把他的朝政变成血腥纪念碑的妖魔搏斗。可是我丈夫有一种状态——我说出来都脸红——在那状态里，我对他的影响便失去一切符咒之力；那时他连自己的天性都反了，温厚化作噬人的凶暴；那双平素连最卑贱的佃户死了都要落泪的眼睛，仿佛渴望起血来；那时他抛开素日的审慎，狂放得不顾一切。这状态，就是醉酒。我说这个，不是控诉他。我们所爱的人，在我们眼里没有过错。"

"除了一样——他对我们不忠。"玛格丽特插嘴道。

"那一样，是的，一旦关系到救他的性命，那一样也须宽恕。"亲王夫人答道。

"哦，安娜！"玛格丽特喊道，"你叫我疑心有什么隐情，可你又不肯说给我听。"

===Original===

“What you ought to know you shall know. A little while ago your husband, with haughty presumption, opposed himself to a mighty faction which has kings for its confederates and kings for its antagonists; he might just as well have opposed Destiny herself. He is too proud to calculate the dangers which he thus draws down upon his head; or does he really think that they who sharpen their swords against a reigning monarch would suffer for an instant one of their own subjects to raise his head against them? And Banfi has threatened, mocked, insulted them, and entangled the meshes of their well and widely laid plans﻿—nay, more, he has encountered and browbeaten them in the very presence of the Prince.”

Dame Banfi folded her arms in timid resignation.

“I see the storm which is gathering over Banfi’s head. In his drunken fits, Apafi has let fall hints which have filled my soul with terror, and I don’t wish Apafi’s to be the hand to strike down Banfi for the sake of others. They will try to catch him at every turn, but we two will watch over him. I will endeavour to keep back the stroke, yet should it fall, ’tis for you to ward it off. We must both possess the entire love and confidence of our consorts, so as to be able to intervene energetically and decisively should they come to blows. For would it not be frightful if one fell by the other’s hand, and one of us were the cause of the other’s misery?”

Margaret timidly pressed Anna’s hand.

“What am I to do? Oh, my God! what can I do? How can I intervene? I have no power.”

“Your power lies in your love, watchfulness, and self-sacrifice,” returned Dame Apafi with an exalted look, striving to inspire her weaker sister with something of her own strength.

At that moment the fate of two men was in the hands of two angels, and the fate of those two men was one with the fate of Transylvania.

===Chinese===

"你该知道的，就会知道。不久前，你丈夫倨傲自负，挡在了一个强横派系面前——那派系的盟友是国王，对头也是国王；他这样干，无异于同命运女神本人作对。他太高傲，不屑掂量这样给自己招到头上的危险；还是他当真以为，那些磨刀霍霍对付在位君王的人，会容自己的一个臣民对他们昂首挺胸？何况班菲威胁过他们，讥笑过他们，辱骂过他们，搅乱了他们精心布置、四通八达的罗网——还不止呢，他当着亲王的面，同他们顶撞过、压制过。"

班菲夫人怯怯地认了命，交叠起双臂。

"我看见风暴正在班菲头顶聚集。阿帕菲醉后漏过的话，把我的灵魂灌满了恐怖；我不愿为了别人的缘故，让班菲倒在阿帕菲手里。他们会在每个转弯处设法逮他，可我们两个要守护他。我会尽力拦住那一击；万一它落下来，就要靠你去挡。我们俩都必须握有丈夫全部的爱与信任，好在他们动起手来的时候，能够有力地、决断地插进去。倘若一个死在另一个手里，我们中的一个成了另一个的祸源，那岂不骇人？"

玛格丽特怯怯地握了握安娜的手。

"我该怎么办？哦，我的上帝！我能做什么？我拿什么去插手？我一点权柄也没有。"

"你的权柄，就在你的爱、你的警醒、你的舍己里。"阿帕菲夫人答道，神色崇高，竭力把自己的一部分力量灌输给孱弱的妹妹。

那一刻，两个男人的命运握在两位天使手里；而那两个男人的命运，与特兰西瓦尼亚的命运休戚与共。

## IV / 四
===Original===

The Midnight Battle

As Denis Banfi, after quitting his wife’s chamber, was descending the spiral staircase which led to the hall, he saw a young horseman come galloping at full speed into the courtyard.

The horseman was covered with blood and foam. As he sprang from his horse the beast collapsed altogether; but the rider rushed pell-mell towards Banfi, who, recognizing in him one of his captains, Gabriel Benkö, went to meet him, and asked him what was the matter.

“Sir,” began the gasping knight, catching his breath, “Ali Pasha is attacking Banfi-Hunyad.”

“Is that all?” said Banfi gruffly, not displeased that Fate had given his irritated temper something to rend and tear. “Send Veer hither!” he cried to his retainers; “and you, when you have got your breath, just tell me how the matter went.”

“I must be brief, my lord. I come from the thick of the fight. Yesterday a troop of Kurdish freebooters appeared before Banfi-Hunyad. Your lordship’s captain, Gregory Söter, anticipating that they had come to levy blackmail, went out against them with the castle bands, engaged in combat with them, drove them from beneath the walls after a sharp contest, and, following up his advantage, sounded a charge and pursued the fugitives in the direction of Zenlelke. We were still pursuing the Kurds, who fled headlong, when suddenly we saw ourselves attacked in flank; and in a trice the whole plain was swarming with Turkish horsemen, who overran us like ants. I cannot exactly tell their numbers, but I saw three horsetail standards with my own eyes, which proves that the Pasha himself was with the expedition. Söter had no time to make good his retreat to Banfi-Hunyad.”

“The devil!” cried Banfi.

“Every one of us had to do with two or three of them. Söter himself seized a morning-star with one hand and a broadsword with the other, and cried to me﻿—I was by his side﻿—‘My son, leave the battlefield, cut your way through! Fly to Bonczhida and tell the news!’ I heard no more. The surging masses parted us; so I threw my shield over my shoulders, bowed my head deep down over my saddlebow, gave my nag the spur, and galloped out of the fight. About one hundred horsemen pursued me, the darts fell like a hailstorm on my shield; but my good horse, well aware of the danger, redoubled his speed, and so the pursuers lost trace of me.”

“Did you come direct to Bonczhida?”

===Chinese===

午夜之战

德尼斯·班菲（Denis Banfi）离开妻子的房间，正顺着通往大厅的螺旋楼梯往下走，忽见一个年轻骑士纵马全速冲进院子。

骑士浑身是血和汗沫。他跳下马，那牲口当场瘫倒在地；骑手却径直朝班菲奔来。班菲认出他是自己的一名队长加布里埃尔·本克（Gabriel Benkö），便迎上前去，问他出了什么事。

“老爷，”喘着气的骑士缓过一口气来说，“阿里帕夏（Ali Pasha）在攻打班菲-洪纳德（Banfi-Hunyad）。”

“就这事？”班菲粗声说。命运给他那正没处发泄的火气送来了撕咬的对象，他倒并不恼。“叫维尔（Veer）来！”他朝家臣们喊道，“你，等气儿喘匀了，把事情原原本本讲给我听。”

“长话短说，大人。我是从战阵最酣处来的。昨天，一队库尔德匪骑出现在班菲-洪纳德城下。大人的队长格雷戈里·舍特尔（Gregory Söter）料定他们是来勒索买路钱的，便带城堡兵丁出城迎击，同他们交上了手，一场恶战把他们从城下赶走，又趁胜吹起冲锋号，朝曾莱尔克（Zenlelke）方向追击溃敌。我们正追赶着抱头鼠窜的库尔德人，忽然发现侧翼受袭；眨眼间整片平原上黑压压涌满了土耳其骑兵，像蚂蚁一样漫过我们。他们的数目我说不准，但我亲眼看见三面马尾旗——这就证明帕夏本人随军而来。舍特尔已经来不及退回班菲-洪纳德了。”

“见鬼！”班菲喊道。

“我们每个人都得对付他们两三个。舍特尔本人一手捙狼牙棒、一手挥大砍刀，冲我喊——我就在他身边——‘孩子，离开战场，杀出去！飞奔邦奇达（Bonczhida）报信！’后面的话我就听不见了。人潮把我们冲散了；我把盾往背上一背，头深深伏到鞍头前，狠刺了坐骑几刺，就冲出了战团。大约一百名骑兵追我，飞镖像冰雹一样砸在我的盾上；可我的好马知道危险临头，跑得愈发飞快，追兵终于把我丢了。”

“你直接到的邦奇达？”

===Original===

“No; I made a side-spring to Banfi-Hunyad, to warn the people there of their danger, so that they might have time to escape to the mountains.”

“You did wisely. Then the people have escaped?”

“By no means. It was in front of Dame Vizaknai’s house that I told the news to the people. Their faces turned pale, when all at once the lady of the house appeared with a drawn sword in her hand, and as if possessed by the spirits of a hundred warriors, stood among the people with sparkling eyes and thus addressed them﻿—

“ ‘Are ye men? If so, seize your weapons, go out upon the ramparts, and show the world that you can defend the place where your children were born and your fathers lie buried. But if ye are cowards, then fly whither you will; but the women will remain behind here with me, to show the savage foe that none is too weak to fight for hearth and home.’ ”

Banfi, with a hoarse voice, called to his armourers to bring him breastplate, spear, and helmet, and beckoned to the panting messenger to go on with his story.

“At these words the people uttered a loud and furious cry. The women, like so many Bacchantes, ran in search of weapons, and mounted the ramparts by the side of their husbands, whom the determination of their wives had turned into veritable heroes. Everyone seized the first thing that came to hand﻿—scythes, spades, flails. Meanwhile, Dame Vizaknai was everywhere at once, marshalling and haranguing the combatants, barricading the church, breaking down the bridge, so that when I left the town, it was already in a fair state of defence. Thereupon I swam the Körös, to avoid making a long circuit, and came hither through the woods and byways.”

During the latter part of this narrative Banfi seemed to be nearly beside himself. He waited now for neither armour nor helmet, but roared for his horse; and as he sprang into the saddle, cried to Veer, who was hastening up﻿—
