“After me to Banfi-Hunyad! March day and night. The infantry must go round by the Gyalyui Alps. The cavalry will follow me to Klausenburg. Light beacons in the mountains as you approach, that I may attack the foe simultaneously with the vanguard.”

“Would it not perhaps be better if your Excellency remained behind with the main army?” said George Veer, with an anxious face.

===Chinese===

“不；我先绕到班菲-洪纳德，向那里的百姓报个险信，好让他们来得及逃进山里。”

“你做得对。人都逃了？”

“哪儿啊。我是在维扎克奈夫人（Dame Vizaknai）的宅前向众人报的信。众人脸都白了，不料女主人忽然手提出鞘的剑走出来，仿佛附着一百个勇士的英魂，目光灼灼地立在人群当中，对他们说道——

‘你们还算男人吗？是男人，就拿起兵器，上壁垒去，向世人证明：你们守得住生养你们儿女、埋葬你们父辈的地方。你们要是懦夫，那就随你们往哪儿逃；女人可要留下来陪我，让野蛮的敌人看看：保卫家园炉灶，没有一个人弱得不能一战。’”

班菲哑着嗓子喊人取胸甲、长矛和头盔来，又示意气喘吁吁的信使接着讲。

“这话一出，众人发出一声震天的怒吼。女人们一个个像酒神祭司般跑去寻兵器，同丈夫并肩上了壁垒——妻子的决绝把丈夫们变成了真正的英雄。人人抄起手边最顺手的家伙——镰刀、铁锹、连枷。维扎克奈夫人同时处处现身，调度战士、激励士气，封堵教堂，拆毁桥梁；到我离城的时候，城防已然像模像样。随后我泅过克勒什河（Körös），免得绕大远路，穿林子抄小道赶到了这里。”

讲到后半段时，班菲几乎按捺不住了。这会儿他甲也不要盔也不戴，只吼着要马；翻身上鞍时，朝赶来的维尔喊道——

“随我去班菲-洪纳德！日夜兼程。步兵绕道贾柳伊（Gyalyui）山地走。骑兵跟我去克劳森堡。你们临近时在山里点起烽火，好让我同前锋同时夹击敌人。”

“大人留在主力部队里，岂不更稳妥？”乔治·维尔（George Veer）面有忧色地说。

===Original===

“Do what I bid you, sir!” was Banfi’s reply; and giving his horse the spur, he dashed off, followed by about half-a-dozen of his suite.

“What ails him then, that he will neither wait for us, nor inform his wife and the Princess of what has happened?”

“He was aghast when I told him that Dame Vizaknai was defending Banfi-Hunyad,” said Benkö apologetically. “She is an old flame of his whom he has long forgotten; but his youthful affection seemed to revive him when he heard of her heroic audacity.”

George Veer, satisfied with this explanation, ordered his squadrons to take horse forthwith; and after previously informing Lady Banfi that he was off on a petty raid, departed for Klausenburg, leaving the command of the infantry to Captain Michael Angel, who did not break up till evening, the road along the Snow Mountains being much the shorter way.

Just as they were about to start, a tattered young Szekler, with pale cheeks but strong arms, stepped forth. His companions had pushed him into the front ranks.

“Come, sing us a battle-song!” they cried.

It was the rude, popular poet, Ambrose Gelenze.

Drawing from the pocket of his tunic his Bible, on the inside of the parchment covers of which he used to jot down his improvised war-songs, he placed himself in front of the host, and began to sing the following simple lay, the whole of the Transylvanian gentry repeating it word for word as they marched after him﻿—

“Now dawns serene the morning sheen,

The wonted hour hath come;

Sounds bold and free the merry march,

Nor bush nor brake is dumb!

Then up! to horse! and scale the height,

Bold Magyar! Szekler steeled in fight!

And sturdy Saxon hind!

A laggard he who doth not hie

When straight before the road doth lie;

And where there is no road to go, then climb, nor look behind!”

This song, sung by thousands and thousands of warriors, gradually died away in the distance.

---

George Veer, on reaching Klausenburg, no longer found Banfi there. The Lord-Lieutenant with two hundred horsemen had departed an hour before.

Veer, after allowing his men a brief halt, followed Banfi all night long without being able to overtake him; the Baron had always the start of him, though sometimes only a few minutes.

===Chinese===

“照我吩咐的做，先生！”班菲答道，一磕马刺，飞驰而去，身后跟着六七名随从。

“他是怎么了，既不等我们，也不把出的事告诉夫人和王妃？”

“我一说到维扎克奈夫人正在守班菲-洪纳德，他就变了脸色，”本克替主人分辩道，“那是他的一门旧情，早已淡忘了；可一听她的英风侠胆，青年时代的情火仿佛又把他点着了。”

乔治·维尔对这个解释还算满意，当即命令各队上马；先向班菲夫人禀报说大人出去打个小仗，便动身去了克劳森堡，把步兵交给米哈伊·安吉尔（Michael Angel）队长统领——因走雪山路近得多，步兵到傍晚才开拔。

正要出发时，一个衣衫褴褛的年轻塞克勒人（Szekler）站了出来，脸色苍白，胳膊粗壮。同伴们把他推到了前排。

“来，给我们唱支战歌！”他们喊。

这是粗通文墨的民间诗人安布罗斯·格伦泽（Ambrose Gelenze）。

他从短上衣口袋里掏出他那部《圣经》——羊皮封里常记着他即兴吟成的战歌——走到队伍前头，唱起下面这支朴素的歌谣；特兰西瓦尼亚的绅士们跟在他身后行军，一字一句地应和着——

“明媚晨光熹微亮，

约时已来到；

行军号角多欢畅，

草树皆喧闹！

上马！去把高峰登，

勇敢马扎尔！百炼塞克勒！

还有壮实的萨克森庄稼汉！

大路笔直在眼前，

磨磨蹭蹭是懒汉；

若无路可走，便攀上去，莫回头看！”

千千万万战士唱着这支歌，歌声渐渐消逝在远方。

---

乔治·维尔赶到克劳森堡时，班菲已经不在了。总督带着两百骑兵，一个钟头前就开拔了。

维尔让部下稍歇片刻，便连夜追赶班菲，却始终追不上；男爵总抢在他前头，尽管有时只差几分钟。

===Original===

It was already late in the night when Banfi with his two hundred horsemen reached the point where the Körös intersects the woody dale; just where a bridge crosses the stream the Turk had pitched his camp. Watchful Bedouins lay stretched on their bellies there, with their long muskets in their hands. It was impossible to surprise them.

In the direction of Banfi-Hunyad a red glow illuminated the sky, alternately waxing and waning.

Leaving his horsemen in ambush on the opposite shore, Banfi with four companions descended to the stream to seek for a ford. The Körös is there so rapid that it can unhorse the firmest rider. Fortunately it had fallen so much in consequence of the summer drought, that Banfi soon found a place where the water flowed more calmly, and waded successfully through it with his escort. One of them he sent back to fetch the rest, but he himself with the other three remained on the opposite bank looking steadily in the direction of the fire.

Meanwhile a patrol of Bedouin horsemen, who were keeping watch on the bank, perceived the three riders and their leader, and challenged them.

Banfi would have fallen back, but three of the Bedouins charged upon him forthwith, while the three others with couched lances fell upon his comrades.

“Bend your heads down over the necks of your horses, and seize their lances with your left hands!” cried Banfi to his companions; and with that they all four drew their swords, went at full tilt against the foe, and collided beneath the dark shadows without another word.

Banfi was in the centre. The lances of the three Bedouins whizzed through the air simultaneously, and Banfi’s comrades fell on both sides of him, transfixed, from their horses, while he with his left hand skilfully disarmed one of the spearmen, at the same time dealing him a blow with his right hand which cleft his skull. He then turned single-handed upon his two nearest assailants, and cut down one with his lance and the other with his sword.

But now the three remaining horsemen fell furiously upon him.

===Chinese===

夜已深，班菲带着两百骑兵赶到克勒什河穿过树林山谷的地方；桥跨河处，土耳其人扎下了营。警醒的贝都因人趴在地上，手里握着长枪。想偷袭他们是不可能了。

班菲-洪纳德方向，红光映天，忽明忽暗。

班菲把骑兵埋伏在对岸，自己带四个同伴下到河边找渡口。克勒什河在这里水流湍急，最稳的骑手也能被掠下马。所幸夏季大旱，河水落了许多，班菲很快找到一处水势平缓的地方，同随从们蹚了过去。他派一人回去接应大队，自己带着另外三人留在对岸，紧紧盯着火光的方向。

这时，一队在岸上巡哨的贝都因骑兵发现了三名骑手和他们的首领，喝问口令。

班菲本想退走，但三名贝都因人当即扑向他，另外三人挺矛直刺他的同伴。

“把头伏到马颈上，用左手接他们的长矛！”班菲朝同伴们喊。说罢四人一齐拔剑，全速冲向敌人，在黑影里一言不发地撞在了一起。

班菲居中。三名贝都因人的长矛同时破空而来，班菲左右两边的同伴都被穿透，跌下马去；他却左手一巧劲夺下一支矛，右手同时一刀劈开了那人的头颅。随后他只身迎战逼得最近的两名敌兵，一个用夺来的矛捯翻，一个用剑砍倒。

可剩下那三名骑兵又狂怒地扑了上来。

===Original===

“Come on then!” shouted Banfi, gnashing his teeth; and with that terrible humour peculiar to certain warriors in the hour of danger, he added﻿—“I’ll teach you how to wield the spear, my boys!” and setting his back against a clump of trees, he stuck his sword into its sheath, seized his spear with both hands, and not three minutes had elapsed before all three Bedouins had fallen from their horses to the ground.

Then he looked around to see if any more were coming, and was delighted to observe that the Turks at the bridge had heard nothing of the tussle, while his two hundred horsemen had come down to the riverside and were noiselessly crossing to the opposite bank.

Some of the fallen Bedouins were still moaning and groaning.

“Smash their skulls in, that they may not betray us with their cries!”

“Ought we not to await Veer’s troops?” asked one of the captains.

“We cannot. We haven’t time!” replied Banfi, with his eyes fixed upon the ruddy horizon, and the little band proceeded covertly through field and forest.

Soon a distant hubbub struck upon their ears, and when they had climbed to the top of a little hill, Banfi-Hunyad emerged before their eyes.

Banfi gave a sigh of relief. It was not the town that was burning, but the haystacks. The roofs of the houses had been taken off beforehand by the inhabitants themselves to prevent the enemy from setting them on fire. Even the church and castle were roofless, and the Turkish host could be seen swarming round them by the light of the conflagration, whilst from the battlements a fiery rain of sulphur and pitch, occasionally intermingled with heavy beams, poured down upon the besiegers, and drove them back from the walls.

Ali Pasha had not waited for his artillery﻿—it had stuck fast in the wretched roads﻿—imagining that he could easily storm a place defended only by women and peasants. But it is notorious that despair makes everyone a soldier, and that even scythes and axes are good weapons in resolute hands.

At this spectacle Banfi’s features grew flaming red. He fancied he saw a white female form on the pinnacle of the tower, immediately gave his horse the spur, and rushed forward like a whirlwind, crying to his horsemen﻿—

===Chinese===

“那就来吧！”班菲咬牙切齿地吼道，又带着某些武人在险境中特有的那种可怕的诙谐补了一句——“小子们，我教教你们怎么使矛！”他把背靠上一丛树，剑插入鞘，双手攥住长矛——不出三分钟，三个贝都因人全都从马上栽到了地上。

随后他环顾四周，看还有没有人来，高兴地发现桥头上的土耳其人对这场厮杀毫无察觉，而他的两百骑兵已经下到河边，正无声地渡向对岸。

倒地的贝都因人中有几个还在呻吟哼哼。

“把他们的脑袋砸烂，别叫哭声出卖了我们！”

“咱们不该等等维尔的部队吗？”一名队长问。

“不能。没工夫了！”班菲答道，眼睛死盯着泛红的天际。这支小队人马便穿田越林，隐秘行进。

不久，一片遥远的喧嚣传进耳朵；等他们爬上一座小丘的丘顶，班菲-洪纳德便出现在眼前。

班菲松了口气。烧的不是城，是干草垛。房屋的屋顶，百姓们事先自己拆了，免得敌人放火烧着。连教堂和城堡都光了顶；大火的光亮里，望得见土耳其大军围着它们蚁聚蜂屯；而城垛上，硫磺和沥青的火雨，间或夹着粗大的梁木，泼向围城者，把他们从墙根逼退。

阿里帕夏没等他的炮队——炮车陷在烂路里动弹不得——他以为，一座只靠女人和庄稼人守的镇子，一冲就垮。可是尽人皆知，绝望能把每个人都变成兵，镰刀斧头到了决绝的人手里，也是好兵器。

看到这幅景象，班菲满脸涨得通红。他恍惚看见塔尖上有一袭白色的女子身影，当即催马狂奔，旋风般冲了出去，一边朝骑兵们高喊——

===Original===

“Don’t count the enemy now; we shall have time enough for that afterwards, when we have cut them all down!” and in a quarter of an hour the little band had reached the camp before the town.

There everyone was slumbering. Whilst one half of the host was storming the town the other found time to repose. Even the heads of the sentries hung drowsily down. There they lay, close to their horses, and only awoke out of their dreams when Banfi was already charging through their ranks.

The Baron, who seemed bent upon relieving the besieged single-handed, cut down everything that came in his way; while the Turks, scared out of their slumbers, blindly snatched up sword and spear, and began massacring each other, despite all the efforts of the Tsahusz’s to restore order.

Meanwhile Banfi was madly forcing his way through the Turkish host surrounding the church. The foremost rows fled back aghast at this unexpected onslaught; but a brigade of Ali Pasha’s picked Mamelukes rode forward and arrested the flight.

A gigantic Moor stood at the head of this troop. His horse too was an extraordinarily big beast, a stallion sixteen hands high. The protuberant, swelling muscles of the dusky giant’s naked arms shone like steel in the hellish glare of the burning haystacks, his broad mouth was bleeding from the blow of a stone, and the whites of his eyes gleamed ghostlike out of his dark countenance.

“Halt, Giaour!” roared the Moor, with a voice which rose above the din of battle, and he went straight for Banfi. In his enormous fist sparkled a sabre as broad as a man’s hand; it appeared too heavy even for him.

Two hussars riding in front of Banfi fell right and left before two blows from the monster, one without his head, the other cleft to the shoulder. Throwing back his arm for a third stroke, the Moor rose in his stirrups, and exclaimed with a voice of thunder﻿—

“I am Kariassar, the invincible! Thank thy God that thou diest by my hand!” and with that he swept his sword backwards, and dealt a tremendous blow at Banfi’s head.

The Baron, with the utmost sangfroid, brought his sword in front of his face, and at the very moment when Kariassar let fly at him, made with lightning-like swiftness a dextrous lunge at the Moor’s fist﻿—it was what fencers call *an inner cut*﻿—striking off Kariassar’s four fingers, so that the heavy scimitar fell clashing out of the fingerless hand.

===Chinese===

“现在别数敌人有多少；等把他们全都砍倒，有的是工夫数！”一刻钟后，这支小队人马已经冲到城前的敌营。

营里人人都在酣睡。大军一半攻城，另一半正好歇息。连哨兵的脑袋都瞌睡得耷拉下来。他们躺在马旁，直到班菲已经冲进他们的队列，才从梦里惊醒。

男爵一副要单枪匹马解围的架势，逢人便砍，挡路的统统放倒；土耳其人从睡梦中惊起，胡乱抓起刀矛，竟自相残杀起来，任凭恰乌兹（Tsahusz）军士们声嘶力竭地弹压也无济于事。

与此同时，班菲发疯似的往围攻教堂的土军里硬凿。前排的人被这突如其来的冲击吓得倒退；但阿里帕夏的一支马穆鲁克（Mameluke）精兵拍马向前，稳住了溃势。

这支队伍前头立着一名摩尔巨人。他的马也大得异乎寻常，是一匹十六掌高的公马。燃烧的草垛映出地狱般的光，照着黑巨人赤裸双臂上虬结暴凸的筋肉，亮得像钢；他阔嘴上淌着石头砸出的血，浓黑的面孔上，眼白幽灵似的闪着。

“站住，异教徒（Giaour）！”摩尔人的吼声压过了战场上的喧嚣，径直朝班菲冲来。他那巨大的拳头里挥着一把手掌般宽的弯刀；那刀看上去连他使着都嫌沉。

班菲马前的两名骠骑兵，在这怪物两刀之下左右分跌：一个没了脑袋，一个从肩劈到腰。摩尔人抡起胳膊要劈第三刀，人立在马镫上，声若奔雷地大喝——

“我乃卡里亚萨尔（Kariassar），战无不胜！感谢你的神吧，你能死在我手上！”说罢他挥刀后抡，朝班菲的头猛劈下来。

男爵以极度的冷静把剑竖到面前，就在卡里亚萨尔挥刀劈落的一瞬，闪电般朝摩尔人的手腕疾刺一记——剑手们管这招叫“里门斩”——削掉了卡里亚萨尔四根手指，沉重的弯刀当啷一声从没了指头的手里掉了下去。

===Original===

The black’s face grew pale from rage and pain. With a frightful howl he instantly threw himself on Banfi, and disregarding fresh wounds on his face and shoulders, seized Banfi’s right hand with his left, and must have dragged him from his horse by sheer brute force if the Baron had not had an uncommonly firm seat.

It seemed as if the Moor were capable of crushing him with only one hand. But Banfi was a good rider, and now he pressed his horse tightly with his knee, whereupon the noble beast reared and plunged; and while the giant was struggling with his master, and tearing at his lacerated arm with a lion’s strength, the warhorse turned suddenly on the Moor, struck him a blow on the thigh with its front hoof, bit his brawny breast with foaming mouth, and shook the bitten part between its teeth.

Kariassar yelled aloud, and suddenly relinquishing the Baron, grasped his poniard with his left hand, and writhing with pain, drew it from its sheath; but at the selfsame moment Banfi dealt a rapid stroke at the giant’s neck. The huge head rolled suddenly to the ground, and while the blood shot up in a threefold jet from the severed neck, the headless figure remained for an instant swaying on its horse, and spasmodically waving its poniard﻿—a fearful spectacle to friend and foe.

At the sight of their leader’s fall the terrified Mamelukes scattered in all directions, trampling one another down in their panic-flight. At the same time the defenders of the church threw down their barricades and made a sortie, Dame Vizaknai at their head with a drawn sword, and close behind her the priests as standard-bearers with the church’s banners. The great besieging host, thus caught between two fires, was cut in two, leaving a free space on one side for the scythes of the peasants, and on the other for the csakanys of the hussars.

The csakany, by the way, is a mighty weapon in the hands of those who know how to use it. Its strokes are almost unavoidable. Its long, pointed beak smites down with such force as to crush shield and helmet to pieces, and a sword is no defence against it.

Step by step the besieged and the relief party drew nearer to each other, driving before them the Janissaries, who contested every inch of ground, and even when lying on the ground half-dead, aimed with their daggers at the feet of the horses which trampled them down.

===Chinese===

黑巨人的脸因暴怒和疼痛而发白。他一声骇人的咆哮，当即扑向班菲，全然不顾脸上肩头新添的伤口，左手死死抓住班菲的右手——若不是男爵骑功非凡、坐得极稳，单凭那股蛮力就要把他拽下马来。

那摩尔人仿佛单手就能把他捏碎。但班菲骑术精绝，此刻用膝盖猛一夹马腹，那骏马当即人立腾跃；正当巨人同主人扭作一团、以狮子般的膂力撕扯他血肉模糊的手臂时，战马突然调头扑向摩尔人，前蹄猛击其大腿，口吐白沫咬住他健硕的胸膛，咬着那块肉在齿间摇晃。

卡里亚萨尔惨叫一声，猛地撒开男爵，左手抓起匕首，忍着剧痛往鞘外拔；就在这同一瞬间，班菲朝巨人的脖颈疾挥一刀。那颗大好的头颅骨碌碌滚落在地，断颈的血三股激射，无头的躯体在马上晃了一晃，痉挛着挥舞匕首——敌我双方见了，无不胆寒。

马穆鲁克们见统领阵亡，吓得四散奔逃，慌乱中自相践踏。

与此同时，守教堂的人推倒街垒，杀出城来；维扎克奈夫人手提出鞘的剑一马当先，身后紧跟着扛教堂旗帜当旗手的教士们。

围城大军这样腹背受敌，被拦腰截成两段：一边留给庄稼人的镰刀，一边留给骠骑兵的恰卡尼（csákány）战斧，杀了个痛快。

顺便说一句，这恰卡尼战斧，在会使的人手里是件威力惊人的兵器。它一击下来几乎无从躲避；那尖长的喙头砸落之力，能把盾牌和头盔一并击得粉碎，拿剑是招架不住的。

被围的和解围的步步相逼，把耶尼切里兵赶在前面；那些人寸土必争，甚至半死倒在地上，还举着匕首去刺践踏他们的马蹄。

===Original===

Dame Vizaknai sprang towards Denis Banfi and seized his horse by the bridle.

“The danger is great, my lord! The Turk is twenty to one. Come behind the churchyard wall.”

“I’ll not budge a single step,” replied Banfi coolly; “but that is no reason why you should not save yourself behind your barricades.”

“Not another step do I budge either,” rejoined Dame Vizaknai.

“I can defend myself!” cried Banfi vehemently.

“And I too!” replied the lady proudly.

The next instant fresh squadrons came streaming up from every quarter, as if they had fallen from the clouds or sprung from the earth﻿—infantry and cavalry with long muskets, bows and arrows, and ribboned darts.

“Ali! Ali! Allah akbar!”

The Hungarian forces ranged themselves in battle array, with their backs to the churchyard wall, and awaited the attack. From the end of the street a glittering array of horsemen was seen approaching; it consisted of a picked corps of Spahis38 on stately Arabs, whose emerald-set saddles sparkled in the firelight. In their midst rode Ali on a slender, snow-white Barbary steed, in his hand flashed a diamond-hilted scimitar; on his head he wore a turbaned helmet; his long black beard fell down over his silver breastplate. On coming within gunshot of Banfi’s host, he halted and marshalled his squadrons.

Hitherto Banfi had not touched his pistols, the wonderfully-carved ivory handles of which peeped forth from his holsters. But now he drew them forth and handed them to Dame Vizaknai.

“Take them!” said he; “you must have wherewith to defend yourself.”

Meanwhile Ali Pasha had sent forward a herald, who, drawing near to the Hungarians, delivered the following message to them﻿—

“My master, Ali Pasha, informs you, O ye unbelieving Giaours, that every loophole of escape is closed. Wherefore then strive against him further? Lay down your weapons and throw yourselves upon his mercy.”

Scarcely had the herald finished speaking when two shots resounded, and he fell dead from his horse. Dame Vizaknai had fired both pistols at him by way of reply. Then Ali Pasha beckoned furiously to the squadrons surrounding him, and from all sides there rained darts, bullets, and arrows on the little band of Hungarians. The same moment Dame Vizaknai climbed on to Banfi’s stirrups, and supporting herself on his shoulders with one hand, cried﻿—

“Fear nought, my friends!”

===Chinese===

维扎克奈夫人奔向德尼斯·班菲，一把抓住他的马缰。

“危险大得很，大人！土耳其人二十个打咱们一个。快退到教堂墓园墙后面来。”

“我一步也不挪，”班菲冷静地答道，“不过这不妨碍你自己退到街垒后面去保命。”

“我也不挪一步。”维扎克奈夫人应道。

“我自卫得了！”班菲激烈地喊。

“我也自卫得了！”夫人骄傲地答。

下一刻，新的人马从四面八方涌来，仿佛从天上掉下来的、从地里冒出来的——步兵骑兵，端着长枪，挎着弓箭，带着系彩带的大镖。

“阿里！阿里！真主至大！”

匈牙利军背靠墓园墙列成战阵，静待进攻。

街口那头，望得见一队闪亮的骑兵开过来：那是一支斯帕希（Spahis）（注38）精锐，骑着一匹匹神骏的阿拉伯马，嵌翡翠的马鞍在火光里熠熠生辉。队伍正中，阿里骑一匹细腿雪白的柏布骏马，手中钻石柄的弯刀闪着寒光；头戴缠头巾的战盔；黑色长须垂落胸前，覆在银胸甲上。进入班菲军枪程之内，他勒住马，整队布阵。

直到这时，班菲还没碰过他的那对手枪——雕工精美的象牙枪柄从枪套里露着头。此刻他拔出枪，递给维扎克奈夫人。

“拿着！”他说，“你总得有家伙自卫。”

这时阿里帕夏已派出一名军使；军使驰近匈牙利人，向他们传话——

“我的主人阿里帕夏晓谕你们，你们这些不信主的异教徒：一切逃生之门都已关闭。何必再负隅顽抗？放下武器，投到他老人家脚下求饶吧。”

军使话音未落，两声枪响，他一头栽下马来，死了。这是维扎克奈夫人用那两支枪作的回答。

阿里帕夏狂怒地朝四周的马队一挥手，镖、弹、箭矢便从四面八方泼向这支小小的匈牙利队伍。

同一瞬间，维扎克奈夫人踩上班菲的马镫，一只手撑住他的肩，高喊——

“别怕，朋友们！”

===Original===

A crackling report and a hissing shower of darts followed. Dame Vizaknai covered Banfi with her body, and after the fiery tempest had roared past, the Baron felt her hold upon his arm relaxing. An arrow had struck her just above the heart.

“That arrow was meant for you,” said Dame Vizaknai, with a faint voice, and she sank dead to the ground.

“Poor lady!” cried Banfi, with a look of compassion. “She always loved me, and would never show it.”

And then blood flowed instead of tears.

The Turkish host surrounded the Hungarians on every side, but were unable to break through their ranks. Banfi was already fighting with his eighth Spahi, who like the seven others was at last overcome by the Baron’s extraordinary dexterity. Ali Pasha was beside himself with rage.

“Why can’t you cut down that grizzly dog?” roared he furiously, and galloped himself against Banfi, driving his flying followers out of his way with the flat part of his sword-blade. “ ’Tis I, Ali Pasha, who now stands before thee, vile hog!” bellowed he, gnashing his teeth, “thou son of a dog, thou.”

“Keep your titles for yourself,” cried Banfi, and riding up to the Pasha he dealt him a tremendous blow on the helmet with his sword, so that sword and helmet were both smashed to pieces, and the champions reeled back half stunned. Ali quickly snatched from his armour-bearers a round shield, while Banfi was hastily provided with a steel csakany, and again they rushed upon each other.

The csakany fell with fearful force upon the shield, and knocked a hole through it, while Ali lunged forward with his scimitar, and this time only a very dexterous twist of the head saved Banfi’s life.

“I’ll play ball with thy head!” cried Ali contemptuously.

“And I’ll make a broom of thy beard!” retorted Banfi.

“I’ll have thy coat-of-arms nailed up over my stables!”

“And thy skin, stuffed with sawdust, shall serve me as a scarecrow!”

“Thou rebellious slave!”

“Thou barber’s apprentice turned general.”

Every abusive epithet was accompanied by a fresh and furious blow.

“Thou dishonourable girl-snatcher,” cried the Pasha, with foaming mouth. “Thou dost filch Turkish maidens for thy unclean embraces; therefore will I carry off thy wife and make her the lowest slave in my harem.”

===Chinese===

紧接着是一阵爆豆般的枪响和嗖嗖的镖雨。维扎克奈夫人用身子护住班菲；等这阵火雨咆哮着过去，男爵觉出抓着他手臂的那只手松了下来。一支箭正中她心口上方。

“那支箭是冲你来的。”维扎克奈夫人声音微弱地说完，倒在地上死了。

“可怜的夫人！”班菲喊道，满眼怜惜，“她一直爱我，却从来不肯露出来。”

接着，流出来的不是泪，是血。

土军四面围住匈牙利人，却冲不破他们的阵。班菲已经在同第八名斯帕希交手了——同前面七个一样，这人最终也败在男爵非凡的身手之下。阿里帕夏怒不可遏。

“你们怎么就砍不死那条老獾狗？”他狂怒地咆哮，亲自纵马冲向班菲，用刀背拍开四散奔逃的部下。“站在你面前的是我，阿里帕夏，你这肮脏的猪！”他咬牙切齿地吼道，“你这狗娘养的。”

“那些头衔你自己留着吧，”班菲喊道，拍马近前，一剑狠狠劈在帕夏的头盔上，剑与盔一齐碎裂，两条好汉都震得晕头转向，各自倒退。

阿里飞快从执械侍从手里夺过一面圆盾，班菲也急忙换上一柄钢制恰卡尼战斧，两人又互相扑去。

恰卡尼带着骇人之力砸在盾上，把盾砸出一个窟窿；阿里同时挺弯刀直刺，这一回全凭班菲脑袋一偏、偏得巧，才保住性命。

“我要拿你的脑袋当球踢！”阿里轻蔑地喊。

“我要拿你的胡子扎扫帚！”班菲回敬。

“我要把你的纹章钉在我的马厩顶上！”

“你的皮剥下来塞上锯末，给我当稻草人！”

“你这犯上的奴才！”

“你这剃头匠改行的将军。”

每一句痛骂，都伴着新的一记狠击。

“你这不要脸的抢女人贼，”帕夏口沫横飞地喊，“你专偷土耳其姑娘，搂进你那腌臜怀抱；所以我要抢走你的老婆，叫她在我后宫里当最下贱的奴隶。”

===Original===

To Banfi the world seemed all at once to be turning round and round. His soul had received three wounds, which quite divested him of humanity.

“Thou accursed devil,” he roared, gnashing his teeth, seized his csakany by the middle with both hands, sprang closer to Ali, and whirled his weapon with lightning-like rapidity over his head, so that it flew round and round in his hands like the sail of a windmill, crashing down now with its axe-head, now with its bullet-shaped nether end on his antagonist’s shield, and attacking and defending himself at the same time. Ali Pasha, confused at this altogether novel mode of attack, would have retired; but the two warhorses, furiously biting each other about the head and neck, were now taking part in the contest of their masters, and could not be parted.

The Spahis, seeing their leader waver, threw themselves between the combatants and drove from Banfi’s side his escort of hussars. The Baron now perceiving that all his people had fled to the churchyard, directed one last swift stroke at Ali’s shield, which, to judge from Ali’s agonized howl, penetrated it at the very spot where fitted on to the arm. Banfi had no time for a third encounter, as he was now completely surrounded.

At that moment a well-known flourish of trumpets resounded in the rear of the combatants, and a fresh and general battle-cry mingled with the din﻿—

“God and St. Michael.”

George Veer had arrived with the banderia.

“God and St. Michael!” thundered the leader of the nobility, conspicuous among them all in his silver coat of mail with the bearskin thrown over his shoulders; and with his toothed battle-axe he hewed his way through the ranks of the astonished Turks.

The attack was skilfully conducted; the mounted nobility pressed on from all sides, simultaneously bringing the Turkish host everywhere into confusion, so that one wing could not assist the other, and the outermost ranks were always borne down by superior numbers.

Ali Pasha had received a bad wound in the arm from Banfi’s last blow, which had daunted his courage, so he stuck his spurs into his horse’s sides and gave the signal for retreat.

The Turkish host was driven head and heels out of the town, and its leaders endeavoured to retreat among the Gyalyui Alps, hoping to rally it again in the narrow defiles.

===Chinese===

班菲只觉天旋地转。他的灵魂挨了三刀，人性被剥了个干净。

“你这该咒的魔鬼，”他咬牙嘶吼，双手攥住恰卡尼中段，纵马逼近阿里，把兵器闪电般抡过头顶，在手里团团飞转，像风车的一片轮叶，时而斧头砸下，时而锤头捣落，砸在对手的盾上，攻守兼备。

阿里帕夏被这闻所未闻的打法弄得手忙脚乱，本想退开；可两匹战马也恶狠狠互相咬起了脖颈，各自参战护主，哪里分得开。

斯帕希们见主将动摇，一拥而上隔开二人，把班菲身边的骠骑随从也冲散了。

男爵见自己的人全都退到了墓园，便朝阿里的盾猛击了最后一记——听阿里那声惨嚎，这一击正穿透盾上套臂的地方。班菲来不及第三回合，他已被团团围住。

就在这时，交战者背后响起一阵熟悉的号角，一片新的呐喊汇入喧嚣——

“上帝与圣米迦勒！”

乔治·维尔带着贵族旗军（banderia）赶到了。

“上帝与圣米迦勒！”这位贵族统帅雷鸣般高呼；他披着熊皮、身着银铠，在人群中分外醒目，挥动齿刃战斧，在惊愕的土耳其军阵中劈开一条路。

攻击调度得法；马上贵族四面进逼，处处同时搅乱土军阵脚，这一翼救不了那一翼，最外圈的人马总被绝对优势压垮。

阿里帕夏的胳膊挨了班菲最后那一击，伤得不轻，锐气已挫，便双刺一夹马腹，发出了退兵信号。

土军被连人带马赶出城去；首领们竭力退入贾柳伊山地，指望在隘口里重整旗鼓。

===Original===

Outside the town the battle, fast becoming a rout, still raged furiously. The Hungarians scattered about the burning hayricks, and were so intermingled in the darkness of the night with their opponents that they could only distinguish one another by their battle-cries.

The harassed Turkish host, which in the darkness and confusion at one time took refuge among the enemy, and at another cut down their own comrades, tried to imitate the battle-cry of the Hungarians, but this only made the mischief greater; for as they could not pronounce the words “Angel Michael,” but always cried “Anchal Michel,” they exposed themselves more completely to the Hungarians.

The Turkish army was now completely beaten; more than a thousand of its dead lay in the streets and around the church, and only the mountain passes, into which it was not prudent for the Hungarians to follow them, saved them from utter annihilation.

George Veer therefore sounded the recall, whilst Banfi, with restless rage, rushed hither and thither after the flying foe. All in vain; every way was barred by the trunks of trees which the Turks had hewn down in hot haste.

“We must let them escape!” cried Veer, thrusting his sabre into its sheath.

“Say not so! say not so!” cried Banfi excitedly, and riding up to the top of a hillock, he seemed to be observing something in the distance. Suddenly he exclaimed with a joyful voice﻿—“Look yonder. The fire-signals have just been lit!”

And indeed on the crests of the Gyalyui Mountains the fire-signals could be seen flashing up one by one in a long line.

“Those are our people!” cried Banfi, with fresh enthusiasm. “The Turk is caught in the trap. Forward!” And remarshalling his squadrons, he galloped towards the barricaded forest paths, heedless of the warnings of the more circumspect Veer.

---

Meanwhile Ali Pasha, abandoning his tents, camels, and booty-laden wagons to the enemy, sent Dzem Haman, the Albanian commander, on before, to level the roads over the snowy mountains.

As now Dzem Haman was advancing through the darkness and superintending the labours of his Albanian pioneers, he heard voices in the steep rock above his head, and a company of armed men suddenly emerged from the mountain passes before his eyes.

The troops on both sides challenged each other simultaneously.

“Who are ye? What are you doing?”

===Chinese===

城外，这场眼看要变成溃逃的战斗仍在激烈进行。匈牙利人散布在燃烧的草垛之间，在黑夜里同敌人搅作一团，只能凭战斗呐喊分辨敌我。

被追打的土军在昏黑混乱里，一时躲进敌人堆里，一时又砍翻自家弟兄；他们试图模仿匈牙利人的战号，这反而闯出更大的乱子——他们发不出“安吉尔·米迦勒”这串音，总喊成“安查尔·米歇尔”，结果把自己更彻底地暴露给了匈牙利人。

土军这下彻底败了；一千多具尸首横在街面和教堂四周；只因匈牙利人追进山隘并不明智，他们才算免于全军覆没。

乔治·维尔于是吹响了收兵号；班菲却怒气难平，东一头西一头地追歼溃敌。全都白费；条条道路都被土耳其人仓促砍倒的树干堵死了。

“只能放他们跑了！”维尔喊道，把马刀插回鞘里。

“别这么说！别这么说！”班菲激动地喊，纵马登上一个小丘顶，似乎在眺望远处的什么。忽然他欢声叫道——“看那边。烽火刚点起来了！”

果不其然，贾柳伊群山的峰顶上，烽火正一座接一座地燃起，连成一线。

“那是咱们的人！”班菲重新振奋起来，喊道，“土耳其人中套了。前进！”他重新整队，朝堵了栅的林间道疾驰而去，全不理会较为持重的维尔的告诫。

---

与此同时，阿里帕夏把帐篷、骆驼和满载掳获的大车丢给敌人，派阿尔巴尼亚指挥官杰姆·哈曼（Dzem Haman）先行，去铲平雪山的道路。

杰姆·哈曼正在黑暗中前进，督着阿尔巴尼亚工兵干活，忽听得头顶陡岩上有人声，一队武装人员转眼间从山隘里出现在他眼前。

两边的人马同时喝问对方。

“你们是什么人？在干什么？”

===Original===

“We are carrying stones,” answered Dzem Haman. “And you?”

“We too are carrying stones,” was the answer from above.

“We are Dzem Haman’s men, who are removing the stones from the path of Ali Pasha﻿—and ye, are you not Csaky’s men?”

“We are collecting stones for the head of Ali Pasha, and are Michael Angel’s people,” resounded from above, and at the same time a terrible rain of stones rolled down upon the heads of the Albanians, by way of confirming the statement.

“Michel Anchal is here also!” roared the terrified Albanians, falling back aghast, and creating a panic among those behind them by declaring that they were surrounded.

At these tidings, the Turkish host, harassed from before and behind, resolved itself into a disorderly mass, on which, at break of day, the Hungarian infantry began rolling enormous masses of stone and rock.

Ali Pasha attempted first on one side and then on another to break through the enemy’s lines, but was everywhere driven back with fearful loss by the missiles hurled down from above. The boldest warriors, who had fought man to man in a hundred battles, fled back pale and trembling before the thundering masses of rock, which so completely smashed everything that came in their way that horse and rider were undistinguishable.

Ali Pasha tore his beard in impotent rage on perceiving that he and all his host were at the mercy of an army even now much weaker than his own.

“There is neither help nor refuge, save with the Most High God!” cried he, breaking his sword in twain in his despair; and drawing out his pistols, he pointed them at his own heart.

At that moment a hand snatched his weapons from him, and Ali Pasha saw Zülfikar before him.

“What wouldst thou do, madman?” cried he. “Thou wouldst not have me fall into the hands of the unbelievers?”

“I would deliver you and your host out of their hands,” said Zülfikar.

“By the shadow of Allah, thou dost speak brave words, and if thou couldst but do as thou sayst, I would make thee the foremost of my captains.”

“I desire no such honour. Promise me a thousand ducats, and send me as a messenger to Banfi.”

“So that thou mayst betray my position to him, eh! thou cur?”

===Chinese===

“我们在搬石头，”杰姆·哈曼答道，“你们呢？”

“我们也在搬石头，”上面答道。

“我们是杰姆·哈曼的人，替阿里帕夏清路——你们，莫不是恰基的人？”

“我们是在给阿里帕夏的脑袋攒石头，我们是米哈伊·安吉尔的人，”上面轰然答道；与此同时，一阵可怕的石头雨滚砸在阿尔巴尼亚人头上，算是证实了这句话。

“米歇尔·安查尔也到这儿来了！”吓破胆的阿尔巴尼亚人惊呼着溃退，又朝后面的人喊叫已被包围，顿时引起一场大乱。

得到这消息，前有堵后有追的土军乱作一团；天一亮，匈牙利步兵便开始朝这团乱麻滚下巨大的石块岩石。

阿里帕夏东一头西一头想冲破敌阵，却处处被上方砸下的石弹打退，损失惨重。那些在百战之中单打独斗都毫不退缩的勇士，面对轰隆滚落的巨岩，也面无人色、浑身发抖地退了回来；巨岩把挡道的一切砸得稀烂，人和马砸在一起，再也分不出彼此。

阿里帕夏发觉自己和全军竟捏在一支远弱于己的军队手心里，徒然暴怒，扯起了自己的胡子。

“除了至高的真主，再无救、再无路了！”他绝望地大呼，把剑折成两段；又拔出双枪，对准了自己的心口。

就在这时，一只手夺下了他的兵器；阿里帕夏抬头，看见了祖尔菲卡尔（Zülfikar）。

“你要干什么，疯子？”他喊道，“你总不愿我落入异教徒之手吧？”

“我要把您和您的军队从他们手心里救出来。”祖尔菲卡尔说。

“以真主的影子起誓，你说得好豪壮；你要真能说到做到，我封你做众将之首。”

“我不图这等荣耀。许我一千达卡，派我去班菲处当信使。”

“好把我的阵位出卖给他，嗯！你这狗东西？”

===Original===

“I’ve no need to do that. He can see it for himself from yon hilltop. You are as good as dead and buried already, so that you have no choice but to trust to me. You may hold out for a couple of days perhaps; but then you and your bravest heroes must perish with hunger just like me. We are all in the same evil case, there is nothing to choose between any of us.”

“And what wouldst thou do, wretched slave?”

“Induce Banfi to withdraw his troops from the road leading to Kalota, and thus leave us a loophole of escape.”

“And dost thou think that possible?”

“It may, or it may not be so. Where death is certain, a man cares not what he risks. If I can speak to Banfi this evening, you may be able to escape the same night. If I succeed, well. If not, we shall be no worse off than we are now.”

“The fellow speaks boldly. Do as thou dost desire. I’ll trust thee. Allah alone reads the secrets of the heart. Go!”

Zülfikar laid down his arms, and went all alone down to the narrow pass leading to Kalota. When he came to the Hungarian outposts, his eyes fell upon rows of dead Turks who had been hung up on the trees along the wayside. This sight did not appear to disturb the renegade in the least. He stepped boldly among the Magyars, and as they seized him, said quickly to them in the purest Hungarian﻿—

“Bring me to Denis Banfi. I am his spy!”

“You lie!” cried they. “Sling him up.”

“I can prove it,” continued Zülfikar, with a loud voice, and taking a neatly-folded parchment out of his turban, he handed it to the captain.

The letter contained these words﻿—

“I, Gregory Söter, hereby declare to all the commanders of the Hungarian troops that Zülfikar, the bearer of this letter, is my faithful war-spy. Let him pass free everywhere.”

The captain gave back the letter, not without grumbling, and bade two of his soldiers lead Zülfikar to Banfi, but they were to cut him down at once if the general did not acknowledge him. However, at the first glance Banfi recognized in him Pongracz, Balassa’s former servant, and motioned to his men to leave them alone together.

“So you have turned Turk?” said Banfi.

“This is no time for questions, my lord. ’Tis for me to speak, and to the point. I’ll be brief, if you’ll let me. Emerich Balassa expelled me from his house when he learnt that I had helped you to abduct Azrael.”

===Chinese===

“那倒不必。他在那边山头上自己就看得见。您已经同死人埋进土里没什么两样，除了信我，别无选择。你们兴许还能撑个两三天；可到头来，您和您最勇敢的好汉们，都得跟我一样活活饿死。咱们全在一口锅里等死，谁也不比谁强。”

“那你想干什么，下贱的奴才？”

“劝班菲把部队从通往卡洛塔（Kalota）的路上撤走，给我们留一条逃生的缝。”

“你觉得这办得到？”

“也许办得到，也许办不到。横竖是死的人，还怕押什么宝。今儿晚上我要是能同班菲说上话，你们当夜兴许就能脱身。成了，万事大吉；不成，咱们也不比现在更糟。”

“这家伙说话倒有胆气。随你去吧。我信你一回。人心底的秘密，唯有真主读得懂。去！”

祖尔菲卡尔放下兵器，独自一人顺通往卡洛塔的窄隘走下去。走到匈牙利前哨，他一眼看见路旁树上吊着一排排土耳其死人。这光景似乎丝毫没惊动这个叛教者。他大步走进马扎尔人中间；众人捉住他时，他用最纯正的匈牙利语飞快地说——

“带我去见德尼斯·班菲。我是他的探子！”

“撒谎！”他们喊，“把他吊起来。”

“我有凭据，”祖尔菲卡尔朗声接着说，从头巾里取出一方叠得整整齐齐的羊皮纸，递给队长。

信上写着——

“我，格雷戈里·舍特尔，特此昭告匈牙利全军诸位统领：持此信人祖尔菲卡尔，乃我忠实的战时间谍。所到之处，一律放行。”

队长嘟囔着把信还了，派两名士兵押祖尔菲卡尔去见班菲；但若将军不认他，就立刻砍了他。不料班菲一眼就认出他是蓬格拉茨（Pongracz）——巴拉萨从前的仆人，便挥手示意左右退下，让他们单独说话。

“你改宗当土耳其人了？”班菲说。

“现在不是问闲话的时候，大人。该我说了，说正事。您容我长话短说。埃梅里希·巴拉萨（Emerich Balassa）得知我帮您劫走阿兹拉尔（Azrael），把我撵出了家门。”

===Original===

“Good!” said Banfi, contracting his brows. “The girl has flown from me too﻿—whither, I know not.”

“Yes, my lord, you do; and the worst of it is, others know it also. Close to the Gradina Dracului there is a habitation among the rocks, and there she dwells.”

“Silence!” cried Banfi, aghast. “How know you that?”

“Balassa has lodged a complaint with the Prince about the abduction of the girl. The matter is not such a trifle as you imagine. Azrael is the Sultan’s daughter, who, after being betrothed to Ali Pasha, was carried off by Corsar Beg, whom Balassa’s poison alone saved from the silken cord, while Balassa himself has become a homeless vagabond because of her. She has been the ruin of all who ever possessed her. It is your turn now. The Prince having promised the disgraced Ladislaus Csaky everything he likes to ask, if only he can ferret out the girl’s hiding-place, Csaky slyly commissioned the Patrol-officer to make inquiries among the people whether a panther had been seen anywhere in the woods, for he well knew that it is the habit of this wild beast to roam about in search of prey. Its track led them to the rocky retreat, the girl has been seen, and everything discovered.”

“Devils and hell!” cried Banfi, turning pale.

“Listen further. Csaky communicated his plan to Ali Pasha, and it was agreed between them that while the Pasha attacked Banfi-Hunyad, Csaky with two thousand Wallachs was to scour the mountains under the pretext of a hunt, and storm the Devil’s Garden.”

“What infernal villainy!” cried Banfi, striking his sword with his fist.

“It is just possible, my lord, that you might still arrive in time,” added the renegade insidiously, “if you do not stay here too long.”

“We’ll be off at once,” cried Banfi, pale with rage. “I’ll teach these lickspittlers to invade the domains of a free nobleman at the very moment when he himself is fighting against the enemies of his country. A few hundred men will be sufficient to keep Ali Pasha in check from this side. With the rest I wager I’ll be able to pull Master Ladislaus Csaky out by the ears if I catch him trespassing.”

===Chinese===

“好嘛！”班菲皱起眉头说，“那姑娘也从我这里飞了——飞哪儿去了，我不知道。”

“不，大人，您知道；糟就糟在，别人也知道了。格拉迪纳·德拉库卢伊近旁的岩间有一处居所，她就住在那里。”

“住口！”班菲惊骇地喝道，“你怎么知道？”

“巴拉萨为劫女一事已向亲王递了状子。这事可不像您想的那么是件小事。阿兹拉尔是苏丹（Sultan）的女儿，先许给了阿里帕夏，后被科尔萨尔贝伊（Corsar Beg）劫走；科尔萨尔靠巴拉萨下的毒药才逃过绞索，巴拉斯萨自己却为她落得个无家可归的流浪汉。凡是占有过她的，个个毁在她手里。现在轮到您了。亲王许了失势的拉斯洛·恰基（Ladislaus Csaky）：只要能挖出姑娘的藏身之处，要什么给什么。恰基便使了个巧招，派巡官到民间查访林子里可有人见过豹子——他深知这猛兽有四处游荡觅食的习性。豹子的踪迹把他们引到那处岩穴，姑娘被人瞧见，一切都败露了。”

“魔鬼和地狱啊！”班菲脸色发白地喊道。

“还有呢。恰基把他的计划知会了阿里帕夏，两人约定：帕夏攻打班菲-洪纳德的同时，恰基带着两千瓦拉几亚人借打猎之名扫荡群山，强攻魔鬼花园。”

“好毒的奸计！”班菲一拳砸在剑上喊道。

“大人若不在这儿耽搁太久，”叛教者话里藏针地补了一句，“说不定还赶得上。”

“即刻出发！”班菲气得脸色煞白，“正跟国敌拼杀的当口，这帮舔屁股的居然来侵犯一个自由贵族的领地，看我怎么教训他们。留几百人从这边看住阿里帕夏就够了。剩下的跟我走——只要叫我逮着拉斯洛·恰基先生越界，我打赌能拎着他的耳朵把他拽出来。”

===Original===

And immediately Banfi commanded his men to set out for Marisel as swiftly and as silently as possible, and bade the little band he left behind him light many large fires in the wood, so as to make the enemy believe that the whole host was bivouacking there, while he himself hastened towards the imperilled hiding-place. To Zülfikar he paid five hundred gold pieces for his timely warning.

The same night Ali Pasha fell with his whole host upon the two or three hundred Hungarians whom Banfi had left behind him; scattered them after a brief resistance, and hastened back to Grosswardein, swallowing as best he could the indignity of a great defeat, for he left behind him two thousand dead, and the whole of his baggage.

From him too Zülfikar received the covenanted one thousand gold pieces, thus doing a service to the Turks and to the Hungarians at the same time, and making both of them pay him for his pains.

===Chinese===

班菲当即传令：人马以最快的速度、最隐秘的行动开赴马里谢尔；又吩咐留下的那一小队人在林中多点大火，让敌人以为全军都在那儿露营，自己则奔向那处危急的藏身之地。祖尔菲卡尔报信及时，他付了五百金币。

当夜，阿里帕夏率全军扑向班菲留下的那两三百匈牙利人；对方稍作抵抗便被击散；他赶紧退回大瓦尔德因，把一场大败的耻辱尽量咽进肚里——他留下了两千具尸首，还有全部辎重。

祖尔菲卡尔又从他那里领到了约定的一千金币——他就这样同时给土耳其人和匈牙利人各帮了一场忙，还让两家都为他的辛苦掏了腰包。

## V / 五
===Original===

The Banquet Tribunal

The blast of hunting-horns resounded from the Batrina Mountains, the hubbub of the chase came nearer and nearer; a group of well-dressed, well-mounted gentlemen led the way, and at their head rode Count Ladislaus Csaky.

“After him! after him!” resounded on all sides, and the pack were already in full cry, when the cavalcade, emerging from the thicket into an open glade, suddenly encountered another party coming from the opposite direction, in whose leader they all recognized Denis Banfi. Csaky with considerable confusion called the beaters back.

Banfi rode up to the group with an ironical smile.

“Welcome, gentlemen, to my domains. Delighted, I’m sure, at my great good fortune. Probably you have lost your way; but, if not, you are my guests, and consequently doubly welcome. But, pray, why do you stare at me so wildly? You really remind me of the Hindu proverb, which says, ‘He who beats the woods for a stag, oftentimes falls in with a lion.’ ”

“We regard your Excellency neither as a stag nor yet as a lion,” returned Csaky, blushing up to the ears in his confusion. “The fact is, we fancied ourselves on lawful ground.”

“Of course! of course!” returned Banfi, with an offensive smile. “You are on my property, and that is certainly lawful ground. I don’t know how to express my gratitude for such an honour. No doubt you are tired too. I therefore invite you all to Bonczhida, just to take a little potluck with me.”

“We are much obliged,” returned Csaky angrily, “but we are unable just now to accept your invitation.”

“Nay, nay; you’ll not put me off. It is not my practice to let those who have come to me as guests depart hungry and thirsty. I cannot regard you as poachers, I suppose? And if you are not poachers, you must be guests.”

“A third case is also possible.”

“I know of none.”

“Your Excellency shall learn from me that there is, though.”

“Quite right. But there will be time for that at table. So turn your horses’ heads towards Bonczhida, gentlemen.”

“I’ve already said that we can’t accept your invitation.”

===Chinese===

宴席法庭

猎号声从巴特里纳（Batrina）群山间回荡而来，围猎的喧嚷越来越近；一群衣鲜马骏的老爷们当先开路，领头骑行的是拉斯洛·恰基（Ladislaus Csaky）伯爵。

"追啊！追啊！"四面八方齐声喊起，猎犬已经齐声狂吠；忽然，这一行人马从密林钻进一片空旷的林间草地，迎面撞上从另一头过来的另一队人；为首的谁不认得——正是德尼斯·班菲（Denis Banfi）。恰基慌慌张张地把赶围的人叫了回去。

班菲带着讥讽的微笑驰到人群跟前。

"欢迎诸位光临敝地。诸位想必为我这天大的好运欣喜得很。诸位多半是迷了路；若是没迷路，那就是我的客人，故而加倍欢迎。只是请问，诸位为何这样野愣愣地盯着我？真让我想起印度那句谚语：'打林子寻鹿的人，常常撞上狮子。'"

"我们既不把大人看作鹿，也不看作狮子。"恰基答道，窘得红到了耳根。"实情是，我们以为自己在合法的猎场。"

"当然！当然！"班菲带着冒犯人的笑答道，"诸位此刻在我的产业上，那自然是合法的猎场。这份荣耀，我真不知如何感激才好。诸位想必也乏了。因此我邀诸位全体到邦奇达，随我吃顿便饭。"

"多谢了。"恰基怒冲冲地答道，"只是眼下我们无法领受您的邀请。"

"哎，哎；可别回绝我。让上门的客人又饥又渴地走，不是我的规矩。我总不能把诸位当偷猎贼吧？既然不是偷猎贼，那就必是客人。"

"还有第三种可能。"

"我不知道还有什么第三种。"

"大人马上就会从我这里领教到有。"

"好得很。不过饭桌上自有工夫细谈。所以，诸位，拨转马头，朝邦奇达去。"

"我已经说过，我们不能领受您的邀请。"

===Original===

“What! Are you so ill acquainted with my hospitality as not to know that, if necessary, I will carry you off by force? Ha, ha! You must take away with you a reminiscence of Bonczhida. As you know now what my wild animals are like, you must make the acquaintance of my domestic animals also. In any case, I mean to take you by force.”

“A truce to jesting, Banfi. This is not the place for it.”

“Methinks ’tis you that jest. I am perfectly serious when I say that I will take you with me even against your will.”

“We should like to see you do it.”

“Then see it you shall,” and with that Banfi blew on his horn, and instantly armed squadrons poured forth from every corner of the wood. Count Csaky and his merry men were completely surrounded.

“Ha! this is treachery!” cried Csaky wildly.

“Oh dear, no! ’Tis only a little carnival jest,” replied Banfi, laughing. “This time ’tis the quarry which captures the huntsmen. Forward, comrades! Take these gentlemen’s horses by the bridles, and follow me with them to Bonczhida. If anyone stands upon ceremony, tie his legs to the stirrups.”

“I protest against this compulsion,” cried Csaky furiously. “I take you all to witness that I enter my protest against this act of violence.”

“I for my part call everyone to witness,” repeated Banfi, laughing, “that I’ve invited these gentlemen to a banquet in the most friendly manner in the world.”

“I protest! ’Tis violence.”

“Nonsense! ’Tis a merry jest. ’Tis Hungarian hospitality!”

Some of the gentlemen laughed, others swore. As however Banfi had numbers on his side, the Csakyites sulkily and wrathfully submitted at last to their jocose tyrant, and allowed themselves to be conducted to Bonczhida, though Csaky stopped everyone he met on the road, and took them to witness that Banfi was doing him violence, while Banfi laughingly endeavoured to make it plain to the good people that the worthy gentleman was a trifle fuddled, and that they were playing a harmless little practical joke upon him.

“You will live to bitterly rue this!” cried Csaky, gnashing his teeth, and half beside himself with rage.

As they were passing through a village, one of Csaky’s company, a young nobleman, whom his friends called Szantho, broke away from the crowd and vanished before he could be overtaken.

===Chinese===

"什么！诸位对我的好客竟生疏到这地步——不知道必要时我会动粗把你们掳走？哈哈！你们必须带走一份邦奇达的纪念。既然如今领教过我的野兽，也该见识见识我的家畜。总之，我是打定主意要强留你们了。"

"别说笑了，班菲。这不是闹着玩的地方。"

"我看闹着玩的是你们。我说连拖带拽也要把你们带走，这话可是再认真不过。"

"我们倒要看看你怎么带走。"

"那就让你们看。"说罢班菲一吹号角，霎时间全副武装的骑兵从林中四面八方涌出。恰基伯爵和他那班快活人被围了个水泄不通。

"哈！这是奸计！"恰基狂怒地喊。

"哎呀，不！不过是场狂欢节的小玩笑。"班菲大笑着说，"这一回，是猎物捉住了猎人。前进，伙计们！抓住这几位老爷的马笼头，跟我押去邦奇达。谁再讲客套，就把他的腿绑在马镫上。"

"我抗议这等强迫！"恰基怒不可遏地喊，"我请诸位统统作证：我抗议这桩暴行。"

"我呢，也请诸位统统作证，"班菲笑着重复道，"我是以天下最友好的方式，邀这几位老爷赴宴。"

"我抗议！这是强暴。"

"胡说！这是开心的玩笑。这是匈牙利式的款客！"

老爷们有的笑，有的骂。无奈班菲人手占先，恰基一伙只得憋着一肚子火，屈服于这位戏谑的暴君，任人押往邦奇达；一路上恰基逢人便拉住作证，说班菲正在对他施暴；班菲则笑着向好人们解释：这位可敬的老爷有点喝高了，大伙儿正跟他开个无伤大雅的小玩笑。

"总有一天你要为今天悔断肠子！"恰基咬牙切齿，气得半疯地喊。

一行人穿过一座村庄时，恰基一行里有个年轻贵族，朋友们叫他桑托（Szantho），从人群里脱身而出，没等追上就没影了。

===Original===

“Let him go to the devil!” cried Banfi gaily. “We will manage to be merry without him, eh! my lord Ladislaus Csaky?”

Gradually Csaky recovered his sangfroid, and his wrath seemed to abate; indeed, by the time they reached Bonczhida he wore a radiantly smiling countenance, for he was well aware that it would be indecent as well as ridiculous to pull wry faces before ladies. He therefore allowed himself to be presented to Dames Apafi and Banfi as a chance guest picked up on the way, without the least show of ill-humour.

Banfi crowned his insult by assigning to Csaky the place of honour at the head of the table, next his wife, and sitting opposite to him treated him with the most marked attention, through which there ran, however, a vein of the most trenchant irony. And Csaky was not even able to resent it! What must his feelings have been!

As the banquet was drawing to a close and the general mirth increased proportionately, Csaky grew more and more furious. He was sitting all the time on burning coals, and had to smile and simper as if he liked it. At last Banfi invented a fresh torture for him, by raising his pocal and drinking his guest’s health. Csaky was obliged to clink glasses, drain his own to the very dregs, and endure to see Banfi laughing at him in his sleeve all the time. Every drop he drank was so much poison to him with that mocking laugh ringing in his ears.

And all this refined torture was so delicately veiled, that it escaped the attention of the ladies altogether.

Just as the mirth was most uproarious, the folding-doors suddenly flew wide open, and, without any previous announcement, Prince Michael Apafi, to whom the fugitive Szantho had brought the news of Csaky’s capture, entered the room.

Both ladies, with a cry of joyful surprise, hastened towards the unexpected guest; but the gentlemen, perceiving from the Prince’s face that a storm was brewing, suddenly became very grave.

Banfi alone preserved his usual grand seignorial gaiety, which could even express anger with a smiling countenance. He sprang quickly from his seat, and hastened joyfully towards the Prince.

“By Heaven, a lucky coincidence! Your Highness comes to us at the very instant that we are draining our glasses in your Highness’s honour. This is what I call an unlooked-for and most timely arrival.”

===Chinese===

"让他见鬼去吧！"班菲快活地喊，"没有他我们照样乐得起来，嗯！拉斯洛·恰基老爷？"

恰基渐渐恢复了镇定，怒气似乎平息了；到抵达邦奇达时，他脸上竟已焕发着明亮的微笑——他深知在夫人小姐们面前哭丧着脸，既有失体统，又显得可笑。于是听任人家把他当作半路拾来的不速之客，引见给阿帕菲夫人和班菲夫人，丝毫不露愠色。

班菲把羞辱推到了顶点：他把餐桌首座的席让给恰基，紧挨自己的妻子，自己坐在对面，对他殷勤备至——只是这份殷勤里，通体贯穿着最锋利的讥诮。而恰基竟连发作都不能发作！他心里是什么滋味！

宴会渐近尾声，众人的欢腾也水涨船高；恰基却越来越怒火中烧。他一路如坐针毡，还不得不陪着笑脸，仿佛甘之如饴。末了，班菲又为他发明了一样新的酷刑：举起高脚杯，祝这位客人健康。恰基只得碰杯，把自己那杯喝到底朝天，还得眼看班菲一路在袖子里笑他。耳畔响着那嘲弄的笑声，他喝下的每一滴都是毒药。

而这全套精工细作的酷刑，遮掩得又如此精巧，竟全然逃过了两位夫人的眼睛。

欢声正闹到最沸处，折叠门忽然豁然大开，米哈伊·阿帕菲（Michael Apafi）亲王未等通报，径直走进厅来——正是逃走的桑托把恰基被擒的消息报给了他。

两位夫人惊喜地叫出声，齐齐迎向这位不速之客；老爷们却从亲王的脸色看出一场风暴正在酝酿，霎时个个正襟危坐。

唯独班菲保持着他那大领主的欢快风度——这风度连发怒都能挂在笑脸上。他霍地离席，喜滋滋地迎向亲王。

"苍天在上，巧极了！殿下到得正好，我们正为殿下的健康干杯。这才叫不期而至、恰逢其时。"

===Original===

Apafi received this salutation with a slight nod, and leading the ladies back to their places, sat down himself on Banfi’s chair. Several of the guests hastened to offer Banfi their seats, but the Prince beckoned him to approach.

“Your Excellency will remain standing. We would submit you to a little friendly cross-examination.”

“If we are to be the judges in this case,” interrupted the learned Master Csekalusi, taking up his glass, “allow me to inform you that the necessary preliminaries39 have already been observed.”

“I will be the judge,” said Apafi; “although I do not quite know who is the master at Bonczhida, myself or Denis Banfi.”

“The law of the land is the master of us both, your Highness,” returned Banfi.

“Well answered! You would remind us that an Hungarian nobleman permits no one to sit in judgment upon him in his own house. But this affair is after all only a little carnival jest. At least you have been pleased to call it so, and we will follow your example.”

The most anxious suspense was legible in the faces of all present: they did not know whether the jest would end seriously or the reverse.

“Your Excellency,” continued Apafi, “has seized our envoy, Lord Ladislaus Csaky, and brought him to your house by force.”

“Ah!” cried Banfi, with affected astonishment, “I see it all now. Why then did not the Count tell me at once that you had sent him to hunt in my preserves? And besides, if your Highness had taken a fancy to some of my game, why did you not let me know it? I would have shot more excellent bucks for your Highness than any that my Lord Csaky could catch.”

“This has nothing to do with bucks, my lord baron. You know very well the ins and outs of the whole business. Don’t force me to speak out plumply before these ladies.”

At these words Lady Banfi would have risen, but the Princess prevented her.

“You must remain here,” she whispered in her ear.

“So far, I don’t understand a single word,” said Banfi, in an injured tone.

“No? Then we’ll recall to your mind a couple of circumstances. The peasants have caught sight of a panther in your woods.”

===Chinese===

阿帕菲微微颔首受了这一礼，把两位夫人送回座位，自己在班菲的椅子上坐下。几位客人忙起身给班菲让座，亲王却向他招手。

"大人就站着吧。我们要对大人做一次小小的友好盘问。"

"若说这案子该由我们做法官，"博学的切卡卢西老爷端起酒杯插嘴道，"请容我禀告：必要的预备程序39已经履行过了。"

"法官由我来当。"阿帕菲说，"虽然我一时说不清，邦奇达的主人究竟是我，还是德尼斯·班菲。"

"你我二人共同的君主，是国家的法律，殿下。"班菲答道。

"答得好！你是想提醒我们：匈牙利贵族在自己的宅子里，不容任何人坐堂问案。不过这桩事，说到底不过是场狂欢节的小玩笑。至少你是这样赏脸称呼它的，我们便依你的样。"

在场众人脸上都写着极度的惶惑：谁也不知道这场玩笑将以认真的了局收场，还是恰恰相反。

"大人扣押了我们的使臣拉斯洛·恰基老爷，把他强行带回了府上。"阿帕菲接着说。

"啊！"班菲故作惊讶地喊道，"这下我全明白了。那伯爵为何不当场告诉我，是殿下派他来我的禁苑行猎的？再说，殿下若看上了我哪只野味，何不直接知会我一声？我给殿下猎的上等公鹿，保管比恰基老爷能逮的多。"

"这与公鹿无关，男爵大人。这桩事的来龙去脉，您知道得一清二楚。别逼我当着这几位夫人的面，把话挑明。"

一听这话，班菲夫人便想起身回避，亲王夫人却拦住了她。

"你必须留下。"她附耳低语。

"到现在为止，我一个字也没听懂。"班菲用受了冤枉的调子说。

"没听懂？那我们就帮您回想一两桩情形。庄稼人在您的林子里望见过一头豹子。"

===Original===

“It is possible,” returned Banfi, laughing﻿—for a Hungarian gentleman may jest with his guests but never be rude to them, however much they offend him﻿—“it is possible that this panther is a descendant of those which came into the land with Árpád,40 and may therefore be called ancestral panthers.”

“It is no matter for jesting, my lord. That panther has torn a young Wallach to pieces in the sight of several persons, wherefore I sent out Lord Ladislaus Csaky to hunt down the beast and kill it. And Csaky had seen the monster and was hard upon it when you met him in the forest and stopped him.”

“Lord Ladislaus Csaky no doubt mistook his own tiger-skin for a panther.”

“No gibes, please. The lair of the monster is discovered. Do you understand me now?”

“I understand your Highness. But ’twas a pity to put my lord Csaky to so much inconvenience for such a trifle. So ’twas he then who discovered the pleasure-house which I built over a hot spring among the rocks? Well, I don’t think even such a discovery as that will earn for him the title of a Columbus.”

“You persist in sneering then? Will nothing make you bow your haughty head? Suppose now I knew the secret of that mysterious cave, what then?”

Banfi began to change colour, and he answered in a low, husky voice, like a man who finds it very difficult not to speak the truth.

“ ’Tis a very simple matter, sir. It was I who discovered Börvolgy; but as soon as the rumour of the hot spring spread abroad, the public tried to take possession of it. Now, I had also discovered a rich mineral vein beneath the Gradina Dracului, and to prevent it from being appropriated, I had a little private pleasure-house built there among the rocks for the exclusive use of my wife.”

By these last words Banfi wished to make the Prince understand that he ought to spare his wife, but they produced exactly the contrary effect.

“Oh, you vile hypocrite!” cried the Prince, starting up and striking the table with his clenched fist. “You would use your wife as a cloak, well knowing all the time that you keep there a Turkish girl on whose account the Sultan is about to ravage the land with fire and sword!”

Lady Banfi uttered a piercing shriek. Her sister whispered in her ear﻿—

“Be strong! Now is the time to show what you are made of.”

Banfi furiously bit his lips, but controlled himself with a mighty effort, and answered calmly﻿—

===Chinese===

"或许有这事。"班菲笑着答道——匈牙利绅士尽可同客人打趣，却无论客人如何开罪，都不可失礼于人——"或许这头豹子，正是当年随阿尔帕德（Árpád）40进国的那些豹子的后裔，所以不妨称作祖传之豹。"

"这不是打趣的事，大人。那头豹子当着好几个人的面，撕碎了一个年轻的瓦拉几亚人；因此我派拉斯洛·恰基老爷出去猎杀此兽。恰基已经望见那怪物、眼看就要追上，你却在林中截住他，拦了下来。"

"拉斯洛·恰基老爷想必是把他自己的虎皮错当成豹子了。"

"请别插科打诨。那怪物的巢穴已经被发现。现在您听懂了吗？"

"听懂了，殿下。只是为这点鸡毛蒜皮，竟劳恰基老爷这样兴师动众，可惜了。这么说，是他在岩间发现了我在温泉上盖的那座消夏小筑？哼，我以为这样的发现，也还挣不来一个哥伦布的名号。"

"你还执意冷嘲？就没什么能让你那颗高傲的头颅低下？假如——我如今已知道了那神秘岩洞的秘密呢？"

班菲变了脸色，声音低沉沙哑地答道，像个强忍着不说实话的人——

"这是桩很简单的事，殿下。发现伯尔沃尔吉（Börvolgy）的是我；可温泉的风声刚一传开，公众就想来抢占。而我在'魔鬼园'（Gradina Dracului）底下还发现了一道富矿脉；为了不叫它落入他人之手，我在那儿岩石间盖了一座小小的私家用消夏小筑，专给我妻子使用。"

班菲说这最后几句，是想让亲王明白：他该顾惜他的妻子；可效果恰恰相反。

"哦，你这卑劣的伪君子！"亲王霍地站起，攥拳捶桌，"你竟拿妻子当幌子，心里却明知你在那儿藏着一个土耳其姑娘——为了她，苏丹（Sultan）就要挥动火与剑蹂躏这片国土了！"

班菲夫人发出一声刺耳的尖叫。她姐姐附在她耳边说——

"要坚强！现在是亮出你本色的时候了。"

班菲狠狠咬住嘴唇，又以巨大的毅力按捺住自己，平静地答道——

===Original===

“That is not true, sir! That I deny!”

“What! Not true! There are people who have seen her.”

“Who?”

“Clement, the Patrol-officer.”

“Clement the poet? Ah! We all know that lying is the masterpiece of poets.”

“Very well, my lord baron. As you deny everything, I will try to get to the bottom of the matter myself. I will therefore go in person to the place in question, and if I find confirmation of that whereof you are accused, let me tell you that a threefold punishment awaits you: first, for the rape of the Turkish girl; next, for the violence done to a princely messenger; and thirdly, for adultery. Each one of these deeds is sufficient in itself to hurl you down from your presumptuous height. My lord Csaky, lead us to this place; and you, my lord Denis Banfi, will in the meantime remain here.”

Banfi stood there with a bloodless face, and his feet rooted to the ground.

Meanwhile his wife had risen from her seat, and rallying all her strength with a supreme effort, stepped in front of the Prince and said﻿—

“Sir, pardon my husband! He knows nothing of this thing﻿—the fault is mine﻿—the woman whom you seek turned to me for protection in her hour of need﻿—and﻿—I concealed her in that place﻿—without my husband’s knowledge.”

Every word she spoke seemed to cost the pale, fragile lady superhuman exertion. Banfi turned very red and cast down his eyes before her. The Princess looked triumphantly at her sister and pressed her hand.

“Well done!” she whispered. “That was indeed noble and heroic!”

Apafi saw through the magnanimous fraud; but he was determined that Banfi should not escape him that way, so, turning wrathfully upon him, he exclaimed﻿—

“And you permit your wife to commit such indiscretions, which might so easily ruin your family, nay, the realm itself? She must be punished for it, and I therefore request you to reprimand her on the spot!”

Lady Banfi, full of resignation, sank down upon her knees before her guests, and bowed her head like a criminal awaiting punishment.

“It is not my practice to correct my wife in public,” murmured Banfi, with an unsteady voice.

“Then I’ll do so myself,” cried Apafi; and approaching the lady he said﻿—“You deserve, madame, to be sent to jail!”

“That I would not allow, sir!” muttered Banfi between his teeth.

===Chinese===

"那不是真的，殿下！这一点我否认！"

"什么！不是真的！有人亲眼见过她。"

"谁？"

"巡察官克莱门特。"

"诗人克莱门特？啊！谁不知道，撒谎正是诗人的绝活。"

"很好，男爵大人。既然你一概否认，我便亲自把这案子查个水落石出。我将亲自前往那处地方；倘若查实了指控你的罪状，那么告诉你，三重的惩罚正等着你：其一，劫夺土耳其女子；其二，对亲王信使施暴；其三，奸情。这每一桩，单独都足以把你从那妄自尊大的高处掀下来。恰基老爷，领我们到那地方去；你，德尼斯·班菲大人，这期间留在这里。"

班菲脸色煞白地站在那里，双脚像钉进了地里。

这时他的妻子已从座位上站起，拿出全部力气拼死一振，挡在亲王面前，说——

"殿下，饶了我丈夫！这件事他毫不知情——错在我——您要找的那个女子，走投无路之时来投奔我的庇护——是——我把她藏在了那个地方——瞒着我丈夫。"

她说的每一个字，仿佛都让这位苍白孱弱的夫人付出超人的力气。班菲涨红了脸，在她面前垂下了眼睛。亲王夫人得意地望着妹妹，紧紧握她的手。

"干得好！"她低语道，"这才是真正的高贵，真正的豪勇！"

阿帕菲看穿了这舍身的谎局；可他打定主意，不容班菲就这样溜脱，于是又怒冲冲地转向他，喝道——

"你就任凭妻子干出这等孟浪事？它轻易便能毁掉你的家门，不，毁掉整个邦国！她必须为此受罚，因此我要求你，当场责罚她！"

班菲夫人逆来顺受，在众宾客面前双膝跪倒，像待罪的囚犯一般垂下了头。

"当众教训妻子，不是我的习惯。"班菲声音发颤地咕哝道。

"那就由我亲自来。"阿帕菲喊道，走近夫人说——"夫人，你该送进大牢！"

"这个我可不答应，殿下！"班菲从牙缝里挤出一句。

===Original===

He was now as pale as a corpse. All his blood, all his fire, seemed concentrated in his eyes. All his muscles quivered with shame and rage.

“Gentlemen!” interrupted a sweet, sonorous voice. How soothingly it sounded amidst the rough contention of angry men. It was the voice of the Princess, who stepped between the lady and her accuser. “In former times,” she cried reproachfully, “noblemen were ever wont to respect noble ladies.”

“So you are again at hand to defend those whom I attack?” cried the Prince petulantly.

“I am again at hand to prevent your Highness from committing an act of injustice. I have always the *right* to defend my sister﻿—but it becomes my *duty* to do so when she is insulted!”

With these words the Princess embraced Margaret, who no sooner felt herself in the embrace of a stronger than herself, than she lost all her artificial strength, and sank senseless into her sister’s arms.

Banfi would have hastened to his wife’s assistance, but Dame Apafi waved him back.

“Go!” cried she; “I’ll take care of her!”

“Then you mean to remain here?” said the Prince to his consort, in a voice trembling between wrath and compassion.

“My sister has need of me﻿—and you, I see, can do without me.”

Apafi, ever since his wife had begun to speak, had plainly lowered his crest, and fearing lest she might rout him altogether, he hastily quitted the battlefield with a half triumph. He could not fail to be very much discontented with the result of his investigation. He felt that he had wounded Banfi in a sore place, but he also felt that the wound was not mortal. The great nobleman had been affronted rather than humbled. So much the worse for him! What will not bend must be broken.

===Chinese===

此刻他脸白如尸。他全部的血、全部的火，仿佛都聚到了眼睛里。周身的肌肉因羞愤而颤抖。

"诸位老爷！"一个甜美洪亮的声音插了进来。在怒汉们粗声恶气的争斗中间，这声音听来何等抚慰人心。那是亲王夫人的声音；她跨步站到夫人和指控者之间。"从前，"她责备地朗声道，"贵族老爷们向来懂得敬重高贵的夫人。"

"你又来护我攻的人了？"亲王气恼地喊。

"我是来拦住殿下，不做不义之事。捍卫我的妹妹，我永远有这个*权利*；而当她受了欺侮，这权利便成了我的*义务*！"

说罢，亲王夫人拥住玛格丽特；玛格丽特一觉出自己落入了一个比自己坚强的人的怀抱，那强撑出来的一身力气顿时散尽，人事不省地瘫倒在姐姐怀里。

班菲正要抢上前扶妻子，阿帕菲夫人却挥手止住他。

"去吧！"她喊道，"这里有我照料她！"

"那你是要留在这里了？"亲王对夫人说，声音在怒与怜之间颤抖。

"我妹妹需要我——而你，我看，没有我也尽够了。"

自打妻子开口，阿帕菲的气焰便明明白白矮了半截；生怕被她杀得大败，他带着半个胜利，匆匆撤离了战场。对这番查问的结果，他不可能满意。他自觉已刺中班菲的痛处，却也自觉那一伤并不致命。这位大贵族受了冒犯，却并未屈服。那对他只有更糟！不肯弯的，就只有折断。

## VI / 六
===Original===

The Diet of Karoly-Fehervár

It is the fate of many a town, as of many a nation, to rise from the dead.

One people perishes there. The walls fall to pieces. The name of the town passes into oblivion. And again there comes another people, which builds upon the ruins, gives the place a new name; and while the old stones, cast one upon another, seem to bewail the past, the city, radiant with new palaces, rejoices in its youth like a flattered beauty.

The hill on which Transylvania’s only fortress stands was once covered with massive buildings by Diurban’s race. Who now remembers so much as its name? The Roman legions subjected the nation, threw down the shapeless walls, and instead of the altar dedicated to the Blood-God, and stained with human sacrifices, there arose a temple of Vesta; the wooden palace of the Dacian duke vanished, and the marble halls of the propraetor took its place, with their Corinthian columns, their white mosaic floor, their artistically carved divinities. The place was then called Colonia Apulensis.

Again the town grew old, fell down, and died.

A new and mightier race came into it; the former inhabitants were buried beneath the ruins of their palaces and temples, and instead of the propraetor’s palace, the gilded and enamelled dwelling of Duke Gyula,41 with its skittle-shaped roof, towered up like an enchanted castle from the *Thousand and One Nights*, and on the ruins of the temple of Vesta the pagan forefathers of the Magyars built altars under the open sky, where they worshipped the sun, the stars, and a naked sword. Then the town was called Gyula-Fehervár.42

A century passed, and Stephen, saint and king, cast down the altars of the fire-worshippers, and built a vast church on the spot where so many false gods had been adored. The sun-worshippers disappeared, and the Christian world called the church after the name of the Archangel Michael.

What sort of church was it?﻿—Nobody can now tell! Two centuries later the Tartars came, levelled town and church with the ground, and put the population to the sword. On their departure they gave to the town the scornful nickname Nigra-Julia.43

===Chinese===

卡罗伊-费海尔瓦尔的议会

从死里复起，是许多城镇的命运，也是许多民族的命运。

一个民族在那里灭亡了。城墙坍碎。城名湮没无闻。又来另一个民族，在废墟上重建，给这地方换一个新名字；古老的石头层层叠压，仿佛在哀悼往昔，而焕发着新宫殿的城池，却像受人夸宠的美人，为自己的青春洋洋自得。

特兰西瓦尼亚（Transylvania）唯一要塞所踞的山丘，曾被迪乌尔班（Diurban）的族民用厚重的建筑覆满。如今谁还记得这个名字？罗马军团征服了这个民族，推倒不成形状的土墙，在染着人祭之血的血神祭坛原址上，立起了维斯塔（Vesta）神庙；达契亚公爵的木殿消失了，代之而起的是行省总督的大理石殿堂，科林斯石柱、白色马赛克地面、精雕细琢的神像。那时此城名叫阿普卢恩西斯殖民城（Colonia Apulensis）。

城又老去、坍塌、死去。

一个新的、更强大的种族来了；先前的居民埋在自家宫庙的废墟下，总督府的原址上，久拉公爵（Duke Gyula）41那描金施珐琅的宅邸拔地而起，球柱形的屋顶，像《一千零一夜》（*Thousand and One Nights*）里的魔塔；在维斯塔神庙的废墟上，马扎尔人的异教先祖在露天筑起祭坛，礼拜太阳、星辰和一柄裸剑。那时此城名叫久拉-费海尔瓦尔（Gyula-Fehervár）42。

一个世纪过去，圣徒兼君王的斯蒂芬推倒了拜火者的祭坛，在这么多伪神受过膜拜的地方，建起一座大教堂。拜日者绝迹了，基督世界以天使长米迦勒之名为教堂命名。

那是怎样一座教堂？——如今没人说得上来了！两个世纪后鞑靼人来了，把城和教堂夷为平地，屠尽居民；临走时还赐给此城一个轻蔑的绰号：尼格拉-尤利娅（Nigra-Julia）43，“黑色的尤利娅”。

===Original===

Our nation’s greatest man, John Hunniady, rebuilt it. Traces of his huge Gothic arches may still be found there. In the crypt, built at the same time, all the Princes of Transylvania were buried in richly-carved sarcophagi. Here *rested* Hunniady himself and his headless son Ladislaus.44 They *rested* here, but only for a time. Robber-hordes came and scattered the sacred relics, and devastated the church, and the succeeding princes who patched it up again during the Turkish dominion, added to the Gothic groundwork the peculiarities of Arab architecture, serpentine columns, and Moorish arabesques.

And last of all came the renovations and restorations of modern times﻿—four-cornered towers, with little low windows and shapeless portals. The arabesques were all whitewashed, and where here and there the mortar falls from the walls, you may catch a glimpse of the stones with which the church was originally built, relics of every age which has visited the place and vanished tracklessly. Here sculptured fragments of the old Mythra cultus; there mutilated Vestals. Below, the top of an ancient altar with the broken symbol of a sun upon it; above, florid and fantastic arabesques.

And again the town lost its name.

They call it now Karoly-Fehervár.45

---

At the time in which our story is laid, this town was the place where the Princes of Transylvania used to be consecrated and the Diets to be held. Where the episcopal palace now stands stood then the Prince’s residence, restored by John Sigismund,46 with marble inlaid chambers, and walls covered with battle-pieces in fresco. The great hall where the Diet met was separated from the surrounding chambers by a balustrade of tinted marble. Round about the walls hung the busts of princes and woywodes interspersed with trophies. In front stood the throne covered with purple, and round about it a triumphal baldachin made of banners, shields, and morning-stars.

The rest of the town was scarcely in keeping with the pomp of the Prince’s residence, for in 1618 the Diet had been obliged to command the inhabitants to cease dwelling in tents, and build up their ruinous houses again.

---

The Estates of the Realm have already assembled. Everyone is in his place. Only the seat of the Prince is still vacant.

===Chinese===

我们民族最伟大的人物约翰·洪尼亚迪（John Hunniady）重建了它。他那些巨大的哥特式拱门的遗迹至今犹可寻见。同期修建的地下墓室里，特兰西瓦尼亚历代的亲王们躺在雕饰华美的石棺中。洪尼亚迪本人和他那被斩首的儿子拉斯洛（Ladislaus）44就*安息*在这里。他们*安息*于此，但只是暂时的。劫掠的兵群袭来，把圣骨抛散，把教堂洗劫一空；土耳其治下陆续修补它的后世亲王们，又在这哥特的骨架上添了阿拉伯建筑的风味——蛇形的柱子，摩尔人的（Moorish）阿拉伯蔓藤花纹。

最后又添上近世的翻修重建——四方的塔楼，低矮的小窗，不成形状的门洞。蔓藤花纹全被刷上了白灰；墙上灰浆剥落的地方，你还能瞥见教堂始建时的石头——每一个莅临此地又无影无踪地消逝了的时代的遗物。这里是一块古老的密特拉崇拜的雕饰残片；那里是残缺的维斯塔贞女像。下面，是一方古祭坛的顶，上面残留着残破的太阳徽记；上面，是繁复奇诡的阿拉伯花纹。

城又一次失了名字。

如今人们叫它卡罗伊-费海尔瓦尔（Karoly-Fehervár）45。

---

在我们的故事展开的年代，这座城是特兰西瓦尼亚亲王们加冕祝圣、召开议会（Diet）的地方。如今主教宫所在处，当年立着亲王的府邸，是约翰·西吉斯蒙德（John Sigismund）46重修的：大理石镶嵌的厅室，满墙是湿壁画的战阵图。议会聚会的大厅，由一道彩色大理石栏杆与四周的厅室隔开。四壁挂着亲王和沃伊沃达们的半身像，间以战利兵器。正面是紫色铺覆的王座，环绕着由旌旗、盾牌和晨星锤扎成的凯旋华盖。

城的其余部分，便与亲王府的气派很不相称了——一六一八年，议会还不得不明令居民停止住帐篷，把塌屋重新盖起来。

---

全国各等级已经齐集。各人各就其位。只有亲王的座位还空着。

===Original===

There they sit in order of precedence﻿—the Transylvanian patricians, the heads of the Hungarian nobility, the most eminent in wit, wealth, and valour﻿—the Bethlens, the Csakys, the Lazars, the Kemenys, the Mikeses, the Banfis!﻿—those medieval clans whose will is the nation’s, whose deeds form its history, whose ancestors, grandfathers and fathers, have either perished on the battlefield in defence of their princes, or on the scaffold for defying them. And their descendants loyally follow their examples. A new prince comes to the throne, and they take up again the swords which have fallen from their fathers’ hands﻿—to wield it for or against him, as Fate may decree.

The Szekler deputies with their homely garb and sullen, dogged faces, and the Saxon burghers with their simple, round, red countenances, and their primeval German costume, form a striking contrast to the dashing and resplendent Hungarian magnates.

The mob assembled in the galleries and behind the barrier presents a most motley picture. Many amongst it can be seen pointing out the celebrities to their neighbours, or shaking their fists at the deputies they dislike.

At last a flourish of trumpets announces that the Prince has arrived. The pages throw open the doors. The crowd shouts “Eljen!” His Highness appears surrounded by his court.

Denis Banfi, as Marshal of the Diet, leads the way, with the national standard in his right hand. Beside him is Paul Beldi of Uzoni, who, as Captain-General of the Szeklers, bears the mace. Behind them comes the Prime Minister, Master Michael Teleki, bringing with him in a silken case the Imperial *athname*: all three gentlemen are in gorgeous robes of state. In the midst walks the Prince himself, in a magnificent green velvet kaftan and an ermine embroidered hat: he holds the sceptre in his hand. Around and behind him throng the foreign ambassadors, foremost among whom stand the Sultan’s envoy in a robe sparkling with diamonds; Forval, the Minister of Louis XIV, a sleek, courtly man, with silken ribbons in his dolman, gold lace on his hat, and a richly-embossed sword-scabbard; his colleague, the Abbé Reverend, with a smiling countenance, his lilac surplice fastened by a purple sash; and Sobieski’s minister, wearing a *bekesch* with divided sleeves, which so closely resembles the Magyar costume.

===Chinese===

他们按位次依次落座——特兰西瓦尼亚的望族，匈牙利（Hungary）贵族的首领，才智、财富、勇略皆为翘楚的人物——贝特伦家、恰基家、拉扎尔家、凯迈尼家、米克什家、班菲家！——这些中世纪的宗族，其意志便是民族的意志，其功业便是民族的历史；他们的先祖、祖父、父亲，不是为捍卫亲王战死沙场，就是为反抗亲王血染断头台。而他们的子孙忠实地承袭先人的榜样。新亲王登位，他们便再度拾起从父辈手中落下的剑——为他也罢，抗他也罢，全凭命运裁夺。

塞克勒人（Szekler）的议员们衣饰朴实、面色阴倔，撒克逊市民们圆脸红润、一身古德意志装束，与鲜衣怒马、珠光宝气的匈牙利大贵族们形成鲜明的对照。

旁听席上、栏杆后攒动的人群，构成一幅驳杂的画卷。其中不少人正指着自己的邻座，一一数点名流；或者朝他们看不顺眼的议员挥舞拳头。

终于，一阵号角宣告亲王驾到。侍童们敞开大门。人群高呼“万岁！”（Eljen!）殿下在近臣簇拥下现身。

德尼斯·班菲（Denis Banfi）以议会司仪的身份当先引路，右手擎着国旗。他身旁是乌佐尼的保罗·贝尔迪（Paul Beldi），以塞克勒总兵的身份捧着权杖。他们身后是首相米哈伊·泰莱基（Michael Teleki）老爷，捧着一个绸匣，内盛帝国的*阿特纳梅*（athname）敕剑：三位老爷都穿着华贵的礼服。亲王本人行在正中，一身华美的绿天鹅绒卡夫坦，头戴绣貂皮帽，手握权杖。簇拥在他四周和身后的，是各国使节：最前面是苏丹（Sultan）的使臣，一身钻石闪耀的礼袍；路易十四的公使福尔瓦尔（Forval），一个油头粉面、深谙宫廷的人物，多尔曼（dolman）上束着丝绦，帽上缀着金线花边，佩着雕饰繁丽的剑鞘；他的同僚勒韦朗神父，满面春风，丁香色的法衣束一条紫腰带；还有索别斯基的公使，穿一件开衩分袖的*贝凯什*（bekesch）长袍——那衣裳同马扎尔装束像极了。

===Original===

All these dignitaries now take their places. The ambassadors remain behind the Prince’s throne; and while the long and tedious protocols of the last Diet are being read, many of them engage in conversation with the lords behind the barrier.

Among these latter we perceive Nicholas Bethlen, the young Transylvanian whose acquaintance we made a long time ago in Zrinyi’s hunting suite. He is now a vivacious and sensible young man, having spent his youth in travelling through all the civilized countries of Europe, cultivating the acquaintance of their most famous men, and even of their princes, and appropriating the progressive ideas of the age, without losing anything of his national peculiarities. The French themselves tell us that it was he who first acquainted them with the hussar’s uniform, and that the dolman he wore at Versailles served Louis XIV as a pattern for equipping his first Hussar regiments.

When Bethlen caught sight of Forval, whom he had learnt to know in Paris, he hastened to his side and greeted him heartily.

“You’ll lose the thread of the discussion,” said Forval, hearing that something was being read, but not knowing what.

“So far, they can get on without me. The bills now before the house merely regulate how many dishes should be set before servants; or discuss the best method of compelling poor people to grow rich enough to pay more taxes. When the real business of the day begins you will find me also in my place.”

“Then tell me in the meantime who are the capable men here, and who are not. You know everything about Transylvania.” Forval had only just arrived there.

===Chinese===

这些显要人物纷纷就位。使节们立在亲王宝座之后；上届议会冗长乏味的议定书宣读期间，他们中不少人隔着栏杆同老爷们攀谈起来。

在这些老爷中间，我们认出了尼古拉·贝特伦（Nicholas Bethlen），很久以前我们在兹林尼的狩猎队伍里结识的那位特兰西瓦尼亚青年。如今他已是个活跃而明达的人物：青年时代游遍了欧洲各文明之邦，结交各国名流乃至君王，吸收了时代的新思想，而本民族的特质却分毫未失。法国人自己告诉我们，第一个让他们见识骠骑兵军服的就是他；他在凡尔赛宫穿的那件多尔曼，成了路易十四装备第一支骠骑兵团的蓝本。

贝特伦一眼认出了在巴黎结识的福尔瓦尔，忙凑上前去，同他热络地见礼。

“你要漏掉辩论的线索了。”福尔瓦尔听见正宣读着什么，却不知是什么，便说。

“到现在为止，没我他们也过得去。眼下议席上的法案，无非规定该给仆人上几道菜；要么商议让穷人快快富起来好多缴税的最佳法子。等今日的正题开场，你自会看见我也在座上。”

“那趁这工夫给我讲讲，这里谁是有本事的人，谁不是。特兰西瓦尼亚的事你无所不知。”福尔瓦尔才刚到此地。

===Original===

“Such a classification is by no means an easy one,” returned Bethlen. “Formerly, when I was a party man myself, and had seen no country but my own, I was quite convinced that all the members of my own party were honest men, and all its opponents scoundrels without exception; but now that I have severed party ties, and seen a little of the world, I begin to perceive that a man may be a good patriot, an honest man, a valiant warrior, or the reverse, whether he belongs to the Right or the Left. Everything depends on the point of view you take. However, as you desire it, I will give you my own views of the state of parties, you can then draw your own conclusions. That proud man on the right of the Prince is Denis Banfi; the one on the left is Paul Beldi. They are the two most eminent men in the land, and both are determined opponents of the war it is proposed to commence; in all else they are adversaries, but on this one point they are inseparable. Banfi seems to be in league with the Emperor, Beldi with the Turk. In their opinion Transylvania is strong enough to drive back every invader of her territories, but not strong enough to play the invader herself. Now cast a glance at that baldish man on the left of the Prince. That is Michael Teleki. ’Tis the genius of that man which alone keeps the other two in check. He is a near relative of the Princess, and would renew here the war which has been the ruin of the national party in Hungary. The trial of strength between those three men will be an interesting spectacle.”

“And if the peace party should prevail?”

“Then the nation will have declared for peace.”

“And the Prince cannot go against it?”

“Here, my friend, we are not at the Court of Versailles, where a Prince may venture to say, ‘*L’état﻿—c’est moi!*’ Each of those three men has as much authority here as the Prince, and their authority is one with his. But let him only try to act against the will of the nation, and he will soon become aware that he stands alone. So, again, those great nobles would remain isolated if they undertook anything in opposition to the Diet.”

“Be candid now. Do you think the war party will prevail?”

===Chinese===

“这样的分类绝非易事。”贝特伦答道，“从前我自己也是党人，又除本国之外没见过别的国家，那时我深信不疑：本党的党员个个正人君子，敌党的人无一例外全是恶棍；如今我割断了党系的牵绊，也见了些世面，才开始明白：一个人尽可以属于左派或右派，却照样可以是好爱国者、正人君子、骁勇战士——或者恰恰相反。一切全看你站在哪个角度。不过，既然您有兴，我就把我对党局的意见说说，结论由您自下。亲王右边那个骄傲的人，是德尼斯·班菲；左边那个，是保罗·贝尔迪。他们是国内最杰出的两位，都是那场拟议中的战争的死对头；在其余一切事上他们是政敌，唯独在这点上同心同德、拆不开。班菲似乎与皇帝暗中勾连，贝尔迪则与土耳其人相通。照他们看，特兰西瓦尼亚的实力足以把任何来犯者逐出国门，却不足以自己去充当来犯者。再朝亲王左边那个半秃的人瞧一眼。那是米哈伊·泰莱基。唯有那个人的才略，钳得住前两位。他是亲王夫人的近亲，一心要在这里重开那场毁了匈牙利民族党的战争。这三个人之间的角力，将是一场好看的戏。”

“要是主和派占了呢？”

“那便是民族自己宣告了主和。”

“亲王不能违抗吗？”

“我的朋友，这里可不是凡尔赛宫廷，容不得哪位亲王斗胆说一句‘*L'état—c'est moi!*’（朕即国家！）。那三个人，个个在本邦的权威不亚于亲王，他们的权威与亲王的权威本是一体。可他若敢试一试违抗民族的意志行事，很快便会发现自己形影相吊。同样，那几位大贵族若想办什么违背议会的事，也会落得孤立无援。”

“坦白说，你以为主战派会占上风吗？”

===Original===

“Scarcely this time. I do not yet see the man who can bring a war about. Amongst the whole Hungarian party there is no one fit to become the ideal of a martial nation. Zrinyi has perished. Rakoczi has deserted it. Teleki knows how to overthrow but not how to create parties. Besides, he is no warrior, and it is a warrior that they want. He represents cold reason, and here there is need of a soul of fire. He has no *mission* to fight for Hungary, but only a political interest. One of the Hungarian magnates, that moustacheless youth yonder, Emerich Tököli, has lately sued for his daughter’s hand in order to engage the father in his interests. Mark my words. That young man has a career before him. His one idea is power﻿—and Fortune is fickle, and her instruments are many.”

This cold consultation was somewhat distasteful to Forval. Meanwhile the tiresome recitation of the protocols had come to an end, and Bethlen took his seat.

The Prince very sulkily informed the Estates that the reason he had summoned them would now be explained to them by Master Michael Teleki; then, wrapping himself in his kaftan, he leaned negligently back in the depths of his huge armchair.

Teleki stood up, waited until the applause of the crowd had subsided, then, casting a calm look upon Banfi, thus began﻿—

“Worshipful and valiant Orders and Estates! The recent events in Hungary are well known to you all, and if you did not know them, you need only cast a glance around you, and the sad, despairing faces with which your assembly has been augmented would tell their own tale. These are our unfortunate Hungarian brethren, once the flower of the nation, now its withered leaves, which the storm has scattered far and wide. You have not denied your kinsmen in their adversity; you have shared hearth and home with them; you have mingled your tears with theirs. But oh! they have not turned to us for the bread of charity, or for womanly lamentations. Thou, Bocskai,47 thou, Bethlen,48 whose images now look down upon us from these walls with dumb reproaches; whose victorious, dust-stained banners wave around the throne, why can you not rise up again in our midst to seize those banners, and thunder in the ears of an irresolute generation﻿—The banished beg of you a country, the houseless a home?”

===Chinese===

“这回怕是难。我还没有见到那个能挑起战争的人。整个匈牙利党里，没有一个配当尚武民族的偶像。兹林尼死了。拉科齐弃它而去。泰莱基只会搞垮党派，不会缔造党派。再说他不是武人，而他们要的正是武人。他代表冰冷的理性，这里需要的却是一团火的灵魂。为匈牙利而战，于他没有*使命*，只有一桩政治利益。匈牙利的大贵族里，那边的那个没髭须的少年，埃梅里希·托克利，新近向他女儿求了亲，为的是把老丈人绑上自己的船。记住我的话。那个少年前程远大。他心里只有一个念头——权力。而命运女神水性杨花，她使唤的家什多着呢。”

这场冷冰冰的品评让福尔瓦尔颇不自在。这时，议定书那恼人的宣读总算到了头，贝特伦也回到自己的座位。

亲王没好声气地向各等级宣布：召集他们的缘由，接下来由米哈伊·泰莱基老爷向诸位说明；说完便裹紧卡夫坦，懒懒地朝他那把巨大的扶手椅深处一靠。

泰莱基站起来，等人群的喝彩平息下去，然后朝班菲投去平静的一瞥，这样开讲——

“可敬可勇的各等级、各议席！匈牙利的近事你们尽人皆知；即便不知，只消朝四下里望一眼——你们中间添了那么多哀伤绝望的面孔，自会把一切说明。这些是我们不幸的匈牙利兄弟，曾是民族之花，如今是枯萎的叶子，被风暴吹散到四面八方。你们没有在亲族落难时闭门不纳；你们同他们共了炉火屋檐；你们的眼泪同他们的眼泪流在了一处。可是啊！他们投奔我们，不是来讨施舍的面包，也不是来求妇人的哀哭。博奇考伊（Bocskai）47啊，贝特伦（Bethlen）48啊，你们的画像此刻正从这四壁无言地、责备地俯视着我们；你们那染尘的凯旋旌旗正环绕着王座招展——你们为何不能再从我们中间站起来，抓起那些旗帜，向这犹疑不决的一代人耳边雷霆般喝道：被放逐的向你们乞一个国，无家可归的向你们乞一个家？”

===Original===

Here Teleki paused as if awaiting applause, but everyone remained perfectly silent; mere rhetoric did not affect that Assembly in the least. Teleki saw his mistake, and instantly changed his tactics.

“You reply to my words by silence. Am I to take it that *qui tacet, negat*? I’ll never believe that your hearts are too cold to be fired. You only hesitate because you would count up your forces. But let me tell you that we shall not take the field alone. The sight of our despoiled churches and our enslaved clergy has called all the Protestant princes of Europe to arms. Even the Belgian King, whom our fate concerns least of all, has rescued our brethren in the faith from the Neapolitan galleys; nor has the sword of Gustavus Adolphus grown rusty in its sheath. Nay, more, even the most Catholic of princes, even the followers of Muhammad, are ready to assist our cause. Behold the King of France, at this moment the mightiest ruler in Europe, raising troops for us, not only in his own land, but in Poland also; and, if necessary, the Sultan certainly will not scruple to break a peace that was forced upon him; or he will, at the very least, place his frontier troops at our disposal. And when all around us we hear the din of battle, when everyone grasps the sword, shall we alone leave ours in its scabbard, we who owe so much to our brethren and to ourselves? What happened to them yesterday may happen to us tomorrow, and what country will then offer us a refuge? Therefore, my fellow-patriots, hearken to the prayers of the banished as if you stood in their places; for I tell you, that a time may come when you will be as they are now; and as you treat them now, so will Destiny treat you then!”

Teleki had done. He fixed his eyes on Denis Banfi as if he knew beforehand that he would be the first to reply to him.

Banfi arose. It was plain that he was making a great effort to keep within bounds and speak dispassionately.

===Chinese===

说到这里，泰莱基顿住了，仿佛等着喝彩；可全场鸦雀无声——空口的辞令丝毫打动不了这个议会。泰莱基自知失策，立刻换了招数。

“你们用沉默回答我。是要我当作*qui tacet, negat*（沉默即否定）么？我绝不相信你们的心冷得点不着。你们迟疑，只因想先数数自家的兵力。那我告诉你们：我们不会孤军上阵。我们被洗劫的教堂、被奴役的教士，已唤起全欧洲新教君王们披甲执兵。连同我们命运干系最浅的比利时国王，都把我们的教中兄弟从那不勒斯的桨帆船上赎救出来；古斯塔夫·阿道夫（Gustavus Adolphus）的剑，也不曾躺在鞘里生锈。还不止呢：连最天主教的君王们、连穆罕默德的信徒们，都乐于襄助我们的事业。看哪，此刻欧洲最有权势的君主法兰西国王，正为我们募兵——不仅在他本国，还在波兰；而事若有需，苏丹也必不吝撕碎那强加于他的和约；至少，他也会把边军交我们调遣。四下里战鼓喧天，人人拔剑，唯独我们——亏欠兄弟、也亏欠自己这么多的人——把剑留在鞘里吗？他们昨天遭的，明天就可能落到我们头上，到那时哪个国家肯收留我们？所以，我的爱国同胞们，请把流亡者的祈求当自己的祈求来听；因为我告诉你们：总有一天，你们也会落到他们今日的地步；你们今日怎样待他们，命运他日便怎样待你们！”

泰莱基讲完了。他两眼盯住德尼斯·班菲，仿佛早知道第一个回话的必是他。

班菲站了起来。看得出，他正竭力克制，好把话说得心平气和。

===Original===

“My noble colleagues!” he began, in an unusually calm voice. “Compassion towards unfortunate kinsmen and hatred of ancient foes are sentiments which become a man; but in politics there is no room for sentiment. In this place we are neither kinsmen, nor friends, nor yet foes; we are simply and solely patriots, whose first duty it is to coolly calculate, for, to say nothing of the joy or grief resulting from it, the fate of a whole land depends upon the issue of our deliberations. Now the question before us is really this: Are we to stake the existence of Transylvania for the sake of Hungary? Are we to shed our blood for the sake of raising her from the dead? Listen not to your hearts, they can only feel﻿—’tis the head that thinks. Just now there is peace in Transylvania. The people are beginning to be happy; the towns are rising from their ashes; the mourning weeds are gradually being laid aside, and ears of corn are ripening on fields of blood. At present the Magyar is his own master in Transylvania. No stranger, no adversary, no protector exacts tribute from him. None may interfere in our deliberations. The neighbouring powers are obliged to protect us, and we are not obliged to do them homage for it. Reflect well upon all this ere you stake everything on one cast of the die! Would you again see all Transylvania turned into a huge battlefield, and your vassals transformed into an army, perhaps not even a victorious army? And even if our hosts were sufficient, who is there to lead them? None of us has inherited the genius of a Bethlen or of a Bocskai; neither I, nor Master Teleki. And then again, whom can we trust besides ourselves? The capricious Louis XIV perhaps? His policy can be changed every moment by a pair of bright eyes. If we depended only on him, a petty Versailles intrigue might leave us in the lurch when we most required assistance.”

Here Forval coughed to conceal his annoyance.

===Chinese===

“我尊贵的同僚们！”他以一种反常的平静开言道，“怜恤不幸的亲族、仇视宿敌，都是男子汉的情怀；可政治里容不得情怀。在这个地方，我们既非亲族，也非朋友，也非仇敌；我们只是、仅仅是爱国者；爱国者的第一本分是冷静地计算——先不提由此而来的悲欢，整片土地的命运，全系于我们这场审议的结局。摆在我们面前的实际问题其实只有一个：我们要不要为匈牙利押上特兰西瓦尼亚的存亡？要不要为她流血，好叫她起死回生？别听你们的心，心只会感受——会思考的是脑袋。眼下特兰西瓦尼亚是太平的。百姓刚开始过上好日子；城镇正从灰烬里立起来；孝服渐渐卸下，血浸的田里麦穗正在灌浆。如今在特兰西瓦尼亚，马扎尔人自己作自己的主。没有外人、没有对头、没有保护人向他勒索贡赋。谁也不能干预我们的审议。邻邦列强有义务保护我们，我们却没有义务为此向他们称臣。把这一切好好掂量掂量，再往一个骰子上押你们的全部！你们愿再看见整个特兰西瓦尼亚化作一片大战场，你们的佃户化作一支军队——也许还是一支打不赢的军队吗？就算我们的兵员足够，又有谁来统率？我们中间谁也没有继承下贝特伦或博奇考伊那样的天才；我没有，泰莱基老爷也没有。再者，除了我们自己，还能信谁？信反复无常的路易十四吗？他的国策随时可能被一双俏眼睛改了方向。我们若单指望他，凡尔赛一桩小小的阴谋，就能在我们最需要援助的时刻把我们晾在半道。”

听到这里，福尔瓦尔咳嗽一声，掩过自己的不快。

===Original===
